### [FIXED] Redis outage breaks the designed enqueue-degradation contract: first enqueue per outage hangs ~64 s then returns 500 *after* committing rows, leaving baselines stuck pending

- **Original severity**: High
- **Phase**: 3 — Redis Outage Enqueue-Degradation Fix
- **Files changed**: `backend/app/tasks.py` (imports/logger, `_celery_client` backend removal, `_send` containment), `backend/tests/test_tasks_enqueue.py` (new — 5 tests)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live this session against current code with a disposable Redis container on 127.0.0.1:6399 and the project venv (scratch probe outside the repo, deleted afterwards). Sequence: one successful `send_task` (starts the result consumer) → `docker stop redis` → measured subsequent calls through the real `tasks._send`. Outage-onset call raised `kombu.exceptions.OperationalError` after 10.7 s (caught, would map to 503); every following call in the episode raised `RuntimeError: Retry limit exceeded while trying to reconnect to the Celery result store backend. The Celery application must be restarted.` after ~63.7 s/63.8 s, from `celery/backends/redis.py:402 on_task_call → result_consumer.consume_from → _consume_from → asynchronous.py:355 reconnect_on_error`. Static read confirmed the escape path end to end: `_send` catches only `OperationalError`; `_enqueue_or_fail` catches only `HTTPException` (its docstring even says "Any non-503 enqueue error propagates unchanged"), so its `_fail()` recovery is unreachable for RuntimeError; imports.py's bulk-import loop likewise catches only `HTTPException`. Finding fully reproduces; no staleness.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: two layers. (1) The API-side producer configured a result backend it never consumes (`tasks.py:24` `backend=settings.redis_url`; repo-wide grep shows zero AsyncResult/result consumers anywhere in the API). Celery's `send_task` invokes `backend.on_task_call()` *before every publish* (celery/app/base.py:968), and the Redis backend's implementation drives its ResultConsumer pubsub machinery — during an outage that machinery runs its own internal retry loop (~60 s of redis-py reconnect attempts inside `reconnect_on_error`) and terminates by raising `RuntimeError(E_RETRY_LIMIT_EXCEEDED)`, a class outside both the tasks-layer translation (`except OperationalError`) and the service-layer recovery (`except HTTPException`). The poisoned state persists per process until restart (Celery's own message says so). (2) Secondary root: the degradation contract was keyed to specific exception types instead of "the publish did not succeed", so any unexpected escapee class bypasses row-failure recovery entirely and surfaces as a post-commit unhandled 500.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Concurrent access*: each API process owns its client; during an outage every in-flight create-site/scan-now/rebaseline/bulk-import now independently fails fast to 503 + row-marked-failed. Previously each process could additionally be left permanently broken mid-episode (the RuntimeError state survives until restart).
  - *Cold-start first call with broker already down*: no consumer state exists → straight publish failure → OperationalError → designed 503 (verified live post-fix, attempt [2]).
  - *Missing/malformed REDIS_URL*: transport failures wrap into kombu `OperationalError` on the publish path (verified in captured traceback via kombu's `_reraise_as_library_errors`) → same 503 path.
  - *Malformed args / EncodeError-class failures*: deliberately NOT translated to 503 — those are programming errors that must stay loud 500s; disguising them as queue-unavailable would spuriously mark rows failed and misdirect operators.
  - *Partial failure mid-operation*: row commits before publish (by design); on enqueue failure `on_fail()` marks it failed and re-commits — unchanged contract. If that recovery commit itself fails (DB outage), SQLAlchemy error surfaces as 500: pre-existing shape, different outage class, out of scope.
  - *Retry/idempotency*: post-503 the failed row no longer blocks (status=failed), so operator retry proceeds cleanly. Duplicate-site-on-retry remains possible — pre-existing gap owned by the sites.url-uniqueness finding (Phase 15), untouched here.
  - *Unicode/malformed input, boundary numerics*: N/A — no parsing surface changed; task args remain UUID strings over JSON.
  - *Auth/RBAC interaction*: N/A — no dependency or guard changes on any touched path.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes*: Phase 1 harness — new tests run against real Postgres via `db_factory` under alembic schema (and pass in CI without any Redis service: the dead-broker client targets 127.0.0.1:1, refusal is instant). Phase 2 conventions followed (module-level logger idiom as in settings.py; ruff format clean). `health.py:_worker_component` shares `_celery_client` but only uses `control.ping(timeout=2.0)` (broker transport; no backend involvement) inside `except Exception` — unaffected.
  - *Backward compatibility*: worker untouched (separate app/config in worker/celery_app.py); rows stranded pending by past episodes still recover via the existing 10-minute stale-supersede paths; no schema/data migration.
  - *Performance at scale*: steady-state slightly improves (no per-send_task pubsub channel subscription churn); dead-broker latency bounded ~4 s steady-state / ~11 s episode-onset first call (existing deliberate `max_retries=2` budget kept — one cheap retry still rescues brief blips).
  - *Fix-failure mode*: if Redis returns mid-retry the publish succeeds normally (verified live: recovery attempt OK 0.0 s); the defensive branch logs at warning with exc_info before translating, keeping any future trigger diagnosable.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) remove the result backend only — treats the root but leaves the contract keyed to exception classes (a future escapee reintroduces the 500); (B) broaden catches only — keeps the ~60 s hang, violating "fails fast"; rejected; (C) both: make the producer broker-only (`backend=None` → DisabledBackend, whose `on_task_call` is a no-op, so the reconnect machinery never runs) AND add a defensive `except RuntimeError` translation in `_send` to the identical HTTPException 503 — chosen. A broad `except Exception` was rejected on purpose: EncodeError-class bugs must stay loud rather than be laundered into "queue unavailable".
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `tasks.py`: `_celery_client` constructs `Celery("wardress-api", broker=..., backend=None)` with a comment recording why; `_send` gains `except RuntimeError` emitting `logger.warning("enqueue %s failed: ...", exc_info=True)` then raising the identical 503 HTTPException ("Task queue is unavailable — try again shortly"). services.py `_enqueue_or_fail`, routers, and imports.py intentionally unchanged — their HTTPException-based contracts are correct once the single chokepoint translates every outage-class failure.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: new `tests/test_tasks_enqueue.py` (5 tests): (1) `test_api_producer_client_has_no_result_backend` pins `conf.result_backend is None` + `DisabledBackend` — **FAILED pre-fix** (`'redis://redis:6379/0' is None` assertion failure), passes post-fix; (2) `test_send_translates_unexpected_enqueue_failure_to_503` feeds the verbatim historical RuntimeError through a stubbed client — **FAILED pre-fix** (bare RuntimeError propagated), passes post-fix; (3) `test_send_dead_broker_maps_to_503_fast` proves a real dead-broker publish maps to 503 in <20 s (observed ~4 s) with the exact detail string; (4) `test_rebaseline_with_dead_broker_marks_row_failed_not_pending` proves the end-to-end recovery against real Postgres: QueueUnavailableError raised, new baseline row status=failed with the queue-unavailable error, nothing stranded pending, ready trust anchor untouched; (5) `test_bulk_import_dead_broker_reports_enqueue_failure_per_row` proves the router yields the designed 200-with-per-row-details instead of a post-commit 500. Tests 3–5 also pass pre-fix by design — they exercise the designed path which was correct but unreachable once the result-store machinery poisoned the episode; tests 1–2 are the failing-before proofs. Manual verification (not automatable hermetically — requires Docker container lifecycle; documented per Rule 6): full outage episode re-run post-fix through `tasks._send` = OK 0.3 s / HTTP 503 10.8 s / 503 4.1 s / 503 4.1 s / recovery-after-restart OK 0.0 s — zero RuntimeErrors, zero >20 s latencies (same episode pre-fix: escaped RuntimeErrors at 63.7 s and 63.8 s).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **520 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~381 s; warning = pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning; 515 prior + 5 new). Frontend `pnpm test` → **7 files / 40 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → clean; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= baseline). Backend `uv run --frozen ruff check .` → All checks passed; `ruff format --check .` → 118 files formatted.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 1's harness self-tests re-ran green inside the 520 (schema-at-head, partial-index, VARCHAR-width assertions unaffected — no models/migrations touched). conftest's `stub_all_enqueues` patches service symbols above the fixed chokepoint — semantics unchanged. Phase 2's logging/format conventions matched. No earlier phase modified `app/tasks.py`.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) New lead logged above: imports.py enqueues inline on the event loop (no `asyncio.to_thread`), inconsistent with services.py:132 — latency-bounded today, no defect at realistic scale. (2) Duplicate-site-on-retry-after-503 remains possible (sites.url uniqueness — Phase 15 scope). (3) The defensive RuntimeError branch has no natural live trigger anymore; unit test (2) stands guard. (4) Worker-side enqueue paths (beat dispatcher, scan_tasks inter-task sends) keep their own resilience semantics — separate subsystem, outside this finding's API-side contract.
- **Commit**: fix(phase3) — broker-only producer: Redis outage fails fast through the designed 503 path

### [FIXED] Concurrent confirms of one remediation execution all succeed and each enqueues a webhook fire — N operators/double-clicks ⇒ N destructive webhook POSTs

- **Original severity**: High
- **Phase**: 4 — Remediation Confirm Race (double-fire) — executed jointly with Phase 5, see Merge Note above
- **Files changed**: `backend/app/routers/remediation.py` (`confirm_execution` rewritten around an atomic claim; imports), `backend/worker/remediation_tasks.py` (`_fire` gains an atomic delivery claim before the POST; module docstring updated to describe it), `backend/tests/test_remediation_claim_race.py` (new — 10 tests)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced on current code this session with a scratch probe outside the repo (deleted afterwards): disposable Postgres database `wardress_p4_probe` built by `alembic upgrade head` in the pre-existing `wardress-test-pg` container, disposable Redis on 127.0.0.1:6399, real broker publishes (LLEN measured via socket), slow canary HTTP receiver counting POSTs. API half: barrier-synchronized bursts of 6 concurrent `POST /api/remediation/executions/{id}/confirm` through the real FastAPI app → one round returned exactly **5×200 + 1×409** and the default queue grew by **+5** `fire_remediation` messages for one execution (another round interleaved cleanly at 1×200 — window is timing-dependent, matching the audit's note that precise synchronization is required). Worker half: three concurrent `_fire()` calls against one `queued` execution → canary received **3 POSTs**, all three calls returned "succeeded", twice consecutively. Finding fully intact; phases 1–3 had not touched this subsystem.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: `confirm_execution` is check-then-set — SELECT (snapshot read) → Python-level status check → ORM attribute write → commit. Under READ COMMITTED nothing arbitrates two writers that validated the same precondition: Postgres never re-evaluates the application's `if`, both transactions pass, both write `queued` (identical values, no constraint violated, last-committer-wins), and each commits its own enqueue. The worker's guard is the same shape one level down: a plain read of `status == queued` followed by a *non-transactional external action* (HTTP POST); every concurrently-processing message re-reads `queued` before any of them commits a terminal state, so each POSTs. The missing primitive in both places is a conditional UPDATE whose rowcount is the arbiter.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *K concurrent confirms (K≥2)* → exactly 1×200, K−1×409, exactly 1 enqueue, exactly 1 audit row (tested with K=6 ×3 rounds against real Postgres; also live-probed post-fix: 1×200+5×409 and +1 message every round).
  - *Confirm racing dismiss* → covered as Phase 5 (jointly fixed; see next entry).
  - *Stale-queued recovery path (>10 min old queued re-confirm)* → must survive: preserved as a second atomic claim (`status='queued' AND confirmed_at IS NOT NULL AND confirmed_at < now−STALE_INFLIGHT` → refresh confirm fields); tested single-winner under concurrency plus sequential success/enqueue/refresh assertions. Auto-execute rows created directly `queued` with `confirmed_at IS NULL` remain non-re-confirmable (409) — predicate requires confirmed_at.
  - *Fresh queued re-confirm (<10 min)* → still 409 (existing `test_confirm_twice_conflicts` stayed green).
  - *Missing execution id* → 404 unchanged (pre-read retained only for existence + response data).
  - *RBAC* → N/A: `AnalystUser` dependency untouched; viewer-403 test green.
  - *Queue-down after claim (Phase 3 interplay)* → the claim commits, then `enqueue_remediation` raises 503 (Phase 3's translated HTTPException); existing revert-to-`pending_confirm` + detail + re-raise behavior kept and now race-safe (a fresh `confirmed_at` blocks any second claim for 10 min, so nobody else can move the row inside the enqueue-failure window). Tested end-to-end including successful retry afterwards.
  - *Audit trail* → `record_audit` now staged only by the claim winner, committed atomically with the claim; losers stage nothing. Tested: exactly 1 audit row per execution after races.
  - *Response honesty* → sessions are `expire_on_commit=False`, so after the Core UPDATE the ORM object holds stale values; explicit `db.refresh(execution)` added on both the 200 path and the 409 path (so the 409 message reports who actually won).
  - *Unicode/malformed input* → N/A: UUID-typed path params, no new parsing surface.
  - *Backward compatibility* → no schema/migration/enum change; existing rows unaffected; frontend status switch untouched (5-state lifecycle preserved).
  - *Performance* → one extra UPDATE per transition; row lock held only inside the microsecond claim transaction (claim commits before any network I/O) — no locks across awaits or across the webhook POST.
  - *Fix-failure mode* → if the conditional UPDATE itself errors (dialect issue), the endpoint raises loudly rather than silently double-enqueueing — fail-closed direction.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) conditional UPDATE + rowcount arbiter — chosen: DB-level atomicity (Postgres re-evaluates the WHERE when the losing writer's lock wait ends, EvalPlanQual), zero lock time across awaits, and it is the codebase's established primitive (refresh-token rotation, auth.py:186-210, live-race-proven in audit Phase 1). (B) `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` then mutate — equivalent outcome but holds the row lock across the audit INSERT and commit and needs the async refresh dance; rejected. (C) process-local asyncio lock — bypassed by multiple API workers; rejected. For the worker side (shared with the entry below): (A) claim-by-stamping `executed_at` while staying `queued`, with a stale-reclaim window — chosen: no new lifecycle state (frontend/docs/native-PG-enum untouched), crash-after-claim stays recoverable via resweep once the stamp ages past STALE_INFLIGHT. (B) new `firing` status — rejected: native enum ALTER migration + frontend switch arm + docs churn, and creates a permanently-stuck state on mid-fire crash (resweep guards only `queued`). (C) FOR UPDATE held across the ≤20 s POST — rejected (lock+connection across network I/O).
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `confirm_execution` now claims `pending_confirm → queued` (setting `confirmed_by`/`confirmed_at`) via a conditional UPDATE and falls back to the stale-reconfirm conditional UPDATE; rowcount 0 on both → refresh + same-format 409. Winner records the audit row and commits atomically with the claim, then enqueues outside the transaction; the existing queue-down revert path is unchanged. Response built after an explicit `db.refresh`.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: new `backend/tests/test_remediation_claim_race.py` (10 tests, all driving real code against real Postgres; a commit-window shim widens the interleaving gap so the pre-fix check-then-set race reproduces deterministically — post-fix outcomes are arbitrated by the database, not the shim):
  - `test_concurrent_confirms_single_winner_single_enqueue_single_audit` — 6 racers ×3 rounds: `[200,409×5]`, exactly 1 enqueue, exactly 1 audit row, final state queued. **FAILED pre-fix** (observed round: `[200,200,200,200,200,409]` — the audit's signature).
  - Behavior-preservation guards passing both pre- and post-fix: `test_stale_queued_reconfirm_succeeds_and_refreshes`, `test_concurrent_stale_reconfirms_single_winner` (**failed pre-fix** `[200,200]`), `test_enqueue_failure_reverts_claim_then_retry_succeeds`.
  - Worker-side (next entry's mechanism): `test_concurrent_fire_single_post` (**failed pre-fix** with `['succeeded','succeeded','succeeded']` = triple POST), `test_redelivery_after_success_skips`, `test_fresh_claim_blocks_retry_until_stale_window_passes` (**failed pre-fix** — fired immediately instead of skipping), `test_undecryptable_url_marks_failed_after_winning_claim`, `test_non_2xx_webhook_maps_to_failed`.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` (run twice: after the functional fix and again after cosmetic test-only edits) → **530 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~295 s; warning = pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning; 520 prior + 10 new). Frontend `pnpm test` → **7 files / 40 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → clean; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= baseline). Backend `uv run --frozen ruff check .` → All checks passed; `ruff format --check .` → 119 files formatted. Live probe re-run post-fix (same disposable infra): confirms now `1×200 + 5×409` with LLEN +1 in every round; concurrent `_fire` → exactly 1 canary POST, losers "not-claimed". Scratch infra deleted (probe dir, probe database, disposable Redis container removed).
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 3 — the enqueue after the claim flows through `_send`'s 503 translation; the revert-on-HTTPException contract is explicitly tested (`test_enqueue_failure_reverts_claim_then_retry_succeeds`). Phase 1 — all new tests run on the alembic-migrated Postgres harness via `db_factory`; native PG enum comparisons required `==` predicates (`.is_()` compiles to invalid `IS $n` for enums — caught immediately by the first post-fix run). Phase 2 — `record_audit` staging semantics (atomic-with-caller-commit) relied upon and verified by the audit-row counts.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Resweep still re-enqueues legitimately-in-flight queued rows older than the grace every 5 minutes (message churn) — the new worker claim makes those duplicates harmless skips instead of double-fire ammunition; the pileup itself remains Finding 7.x/Phase 26 territory. (2) Sequential redelivery of a claimed-but-crashed row skips until the stamp goes stale (≤10 min extra delay in the rare crash-after-claim case) — accepted trade documented in code; STALE_INFLIGHT already bounds it well under the historical stuck-forever alternative. (3) Agent pending-action confirm has the same disease (Finding 6.2, Phase 25 scope) — deliberately untouched here.
- **Commit**: fix(phase4+5) — atomic claims make remediation confirm/dismiss/fire single-winner and single-fire

### [FIXED] Concurrent confirm+dismiss of one remediation execution both return success and last-writer-wins — an operator's explicit rejection can be overwritten and the destructive webhook fires anyway

- **Original severity**: High
- **Phase**: 5 — Remediation Confirm+Dismiss Race — executed jointly with Phase 4, see Merge Note above
- **Files changed**: `backend/app/routers/remediation.py` (`dismiss_execution` rewritten around the atomic claim — same file/mechanism as Phase 4), `backend/tests/test_remediation_claim_race.py` (race coverage shared)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced on current code in the same session/probe as Phase 4: barrier-synchronized confirm+dismiss pairs through the real API repeatedly produced rounds where **both** requests returned 200 (e.g. observed `('confirm', 200), ('dismiss', 200)`), leaving last-writer-wins final states and two contradictory audit rows for one execution — matching the audit's 2/10 dangerous-ordering rate plus the harmless-direction overwrites. Static re-check confirmed dismiss was still plain check-then-set (`remediation.py` dismiss branch) with the destructive ordering (dismiss commits first, blind confirm overwrite fires anyway) fully reachable. Not stale.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: identical to Phase 4's — check-then-set with no atomic claim — complicated by the cross-endpoint dimension: confirm and dismiss guard the *same* precondition (`status is pending_confirm`) from different request handlers, so under READ COMMITTED both validate against the pre-race snapshot and whichever commits last silently wins. In the dangerous ordering (dismiss commits first, confirm second) the operator's rejection is overwritten to `queued` and the already-planned fire proceeds; the reverse ordering leaves a stale-but-harmless enqueue. Both operators see success; the audit log records both actions contradictorily.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Confirm vs dismiss, both orderings* → exactly one winner in either interleaving; loser observes the winner via re-select and gets the same-format 409 ("already dismissed"/"already queued"). Destructive direction eliminated: a won dismissal can never be overwritten into a firing, because confirm's claim predicate (`status == 'pending_confirm'`) fails against the committed `dismissed` row.
  - *Winner-specific side effects* → enqueue only on confirm wins (tested per-round: winner=confirm ⇒ execution enqueued + final `queued`; winner=dismiss ⇒ final `dismissed`, no enqueue).
  - *Audit trail* → exactly one audit row per execution regardless of outcome (was: two contradictory rows). Tested across 6 raced pairs.
  - *Dismiss on non-pending rows* → unchanged 409 semantics (sequential confirm-then-dismiss still conflicts — existing `test_dismiss` happy path and `test_confirm_twice_conflicts` green; queued/succeeded/failed rows un-dismissable exactly as before, which is Finding 7.3's stuck-row territory, out of scope here).
  - *Detail field* → dismissal detail string `"Dismissed by operator"` moved verbatim into the claim's VALUES (identical stored result).
  - *Stale-queued interplay* → dismiss claims only `pending_confirm`; a stale `queued` row remains recoverable exclusively through re-confirm (unchanged design).
  - *Concurrent access beyond pairs* → the claim primitive generalizes to N actors (covered by Phase 4's 6-racer test on the same predicate family).
  - *Auth/RBAC* → N/A: `AnalystUser` on both endpoints untouched.
  - *Unicode/boundary inputs, retry/idempotency, performance, backward compat* → as enumerated in the Phase 4 entry (same mechanism; no new surface).
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) give both endpoints the same conditional-UPDATE claim on the shared precondition — chosen: one mechanism closes both findings' races simultaneously, matches the plan's merge contingency, mirrors auth rotation. (B) fix confirm only and rely on its atomicity to starve the dangerous cross-ordering — analyzed and rejected: it does eliminate fire-after-rejection (a first-committed dismissal makes confirm's claim miss), but a late-committed blind dismiss would still overwrite a legitimate `queued` win and return false success with a contradictory audit row — dishonest contract left open by design. (C) serialize via table-level advisory lock — gross over-lock for a row-local decision; rejected.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `dismiss_execution` claims `pending_confirm → dismissed` (+`confirmed_by`/`confirmed_at`/detail) via a conditional UPDATE; rowcount 0 → `db.refresh` + identical-message 409 reflecting the actual winning state; winner stages the `remediation.dismiss` audit row committed atomically with the claim; response refreshed before serialization. Confirm's claim (Phase 4) is the mirror half on the same predicate.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in `backend/tests/test_remediation_claim_race.py`: `test_confirm_dismiss_race_single_winner` — 6 raced pairs, each asserting exactly one 200, final status consistent with the winner, correct per-winner enqueue behavior, and exactly one audit row per execution. **FAILED pre-fix** (observed round: both returned 200). Shared guards listed under the Phase 4 entry cover the surrounding invariants (single enqueue, stale recovery, revert-on-outage).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: identical runs to the Phase 4 entry (same merged commit): backend **530 passed / 0 failed / 1 warning** (twice), frontend vitest **40 passed**, `tsc -b --noEmit` clean, oxlint 0 errors / 12 warnings, ruff check + format clean. Live probe post-fix: no both-200 rounds possible (claim arbitration), consistent with the test outcomes.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: same subsystem as Phase 4 (this entry) and Phase 3 (enqueue path behind the confirm winner); audit-log atomicity from Phase 2 relied on for the single-row guarantee. No earlier phase had touched `dismiss_execution`.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) The docs' lifecycle description of the confirm queue (remediation-hooks.mdx) is now accurate under concurrency but doc sweeps belong to Phases 33/41 per the plan — no doc edits made here. (2) Finding 6.2 (agent pending-action confirm race, same family) remains Phase 25 scope; its fix should reuse this exact pattern. (3) Dismiss still cannot resolve stuck-`queued` rows (Finding 7.3, Phase 26) — unchanged by design.
- **Commit**: fix(phase4+5) — atomic claims make remediation confirm/dismiss/fire single-winner and single-fire

### [FIXED] Layer-1 hash gate permanently disables the visual layer for defacements that don't alter the serialized DOM — server-side asset swaps are structurally undetectable

- **Original severity**: High
- **Phase**: 6 — Layer-1 Hash Gate Visual-Layer Bypass
- **Files changed**: `backend/worker/detection/pipeline.py` (module-docstring gate-rules block rewritten; `layer4_visual_diff` removed from `GATED_BY_IDENTICAL_HASH`; now-dead `key != "layer4_visual_diff"` clause removed from the baseline-html-missing branch), `backend/tests/test_pipeline_visual_gate.py` (new — 5 tests), `backend/tests/test_detection_fusion_pipeline.py:429-450` (`test_pipeline_identical_hash_gates_content_layers` updated to the corrected expectations)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live this session against current code with scratch PIL probes driving the real pipeline and real layer functions (project venv, scripts outside the repo, deleted afterwards). Identical HTML on both sides (hash equal by construction) + clean banner vs black "HACKED BY CYBER WARRIORS" banner: direct `layer4_visual_diff` = **0.338** (ssim 0.725, pHash 120/256 bits) while `run_detection` reported layer4 `skipped=True` ("gated by layer 1…") and fused risk **0.0352** — matching the audit's own measurements (0.2418 direct / 0.0352 fused with their images). A magnitude sweep additionally measured a small server-side logo swap (120×48 px region): direct score 0.0196, equally gated to nothing (fused stayed 0.0352). Finding fully intact; Phases 1–5 had not touched the detection subsystem.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: `GATED_BY_IDENTICAL_HASH` conflated two evidence domains. Layers 2/3/5/8 are pure functions of the serialized-DOM string that layer 1 hashes — an identical hash genuinely implies identical inputs for them. Layer 4 is *not* a function of that string: its inputs are screenshot pixels produced by Chromium rendering, which include externally-referenced asset bytes (`<img>`/CSS-referenced images, external JS painting over the page after load, cross-origin iframe internals) that never appear in `page.content()`. The gate's justification ("a byte-identical page could only differ visually through non-deterministic rendering noise") treats DOM identity as pixel identity — false exactly for server-side asset replacement, the most common low-sophistication defacement class. The missing primitive is the predicate distinction "layer input ⊆ hashed content" vs "layer input independent of hashed content".
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Concurrent access*: N/A — `run_detection` is a stateless pure function per scan (executed in a worker thread); no shared state, no DB writes.
  - *Empty/missing/malformed input*: missing or corrupt screenshots under an identical hash now surface as a ran-with-note result (`score 0.0`, evidence note, no `skipped`) instead of hiding behind a gate skip — capture degradation stays visible downstream; explicitly tested.
  - *Unicode/oversized/boundary inputs*: N/A — binary image path; PIL decompression-bomb guard and compare-size bounds unchanged.
  - *Partial failure mid-operation*: a layer-4 exception under identical hash hits the existing per-layer crash isolation (`skip_result` + error detail), exactly as it already does on the changed-hash path — no new failure mode introduced.
  - *Retry/idempotency*: detection recomputes deterministically from stored artifacts under acks_late redelivery; `_persist_findings` still writes one row per layer (9 rows per scan — asserted by existing `test_scan_clean`), only the layer-4 row's skipped/score content changes.
  - *Auth/RBAC interaction*: N/A — worker-internal computation; no endpoint, guard, or dependency touched.
  - *Interaction with other findings already fixed (Rule 11)*: Phases 1–5 touch harness/CI/enqueue/remediation only — zero overlap with detection code; their fix-log entries re-read before starting. Suppression bbox masks are applied inside layer 4 itself, so they now also function on hash-identical rescans (previously unreachable through the gate).
  - *Backward compatibility*: no schema/model/enum change; persisted historical scans unaffected; the frontend renders skip reasons dynamically from `scan.layer_scores`, so hash-identical rescans simply display a real visual score where they previously displayed "skipped".
  - *Verdict semantics*: the `changed` computation counts any non-skipped nonzero score — truly-identical pixels score exactly 0.0 (SSIM=1.0, hash distance 0), so clean verdicts are preserved; renders that genuinely differ now flip clean→changed, which is honest (the pixels did change). Fused risk for the measured banner-swap scenario rises 0.0352 → 0.1322 — still below the default 0.5 flag threshold by design: closing the flagging gap belongs to Phase 7 / the fusion arc, not here.
  - *Performance at realistic scale*: zero new artifact I/O (`_baseline_page_data` already materialized both artifacts before detection regardless of gating); added cost is one SSIM+pHash pass (~tens of ms CPU inside the existing worker thread) per hash-identical scan only, bounded by COMPARE_WIDTH 683 / MAX_COMPARE_HEIGHT 4096 — negligible next to the multi-second Playwright capture.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: if layer 4 itself fails on some pathological input, the pipeline degrades loudly-but-locally (crash isolation) rather than silently double-reporting — fail-closed direction.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) remove layer 4 from the identical-hash gate so it always runs — chosen: matches the audit's preferred direction ("let suppression/rendering-noise handling happen inside layer 4 as designed"), one-line predicate change plus docstring truthing, and the layer already handles its own noise/masking. (B) keep a gate but on *(hash identical ∧ screenshot bytes identical)* — rejected: byte-equality across two independent Chromium encodes is fragile (any encoder drift falls through to the full diff anyway, i.e. ≈(A) with an extra hashing pass), and it adds a second comparison mechanism beside the layer's own. (C) micro-optimize (A) with an early byte-equality return inside layer 4 — rejected as unnecessary complexity for ~tens-of-ms savings in a background thread. (D) keep the gate and add a separate lightweight always-on pHash check outside layer 4 — rejected: creates a second production caller for visual comparison, breaking the audited single-caller property.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `pipeline.py`: `"layer4_visual_diff"` removed from `GATED_BY_IDENTICAL_HASH` (now exactly the four DOM-derived layers); module docstring's gating-rules block rewritten to state the real invariant (serialized DOM ≠ pixels; layer 4 runs unconditionally; noise handling lives inside layer 4); the baseline-html-missing branch's `and key != "layer4_visual_diff"` carve-out became dead code once layer 4 left the gate set and was removed. No other file's runtime behavior changed.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: new `backend/tests/test_pipeline_visual_gate.py` (5 tests, all against the real pipeline with PIL-crafted screenshot pairs, no mocks):
  - `test_visual_layer_not_in_identical_hash_gate` — tripwire pinning the gate-set membership decision. **FAILED pre-fix.**
  - `test_banner_asset_swap_with_identical_dom_runs_visual_layer` — identical DOM + server-side banner replacement → layer 4 runs, score > 0.2, ssim < 0.9, pHash > 32 bits. **FAILED pre-fix** (`skipped=True`).
  - `test_logo_asset_swap_with_identical_dom_runs_visual_layer` — small-region asset swap also opens the visual track (guards against a partial re-gate that only catches full takeovers). **FAILED pre-fix.**
  - `test_pixel_change_raises_fused_risk_above_identical_control` — pixel change raises fused risk strictly above the pixel-identical control run (relative assertion chosen deliberately: it survives Phases 8–10's model refit given the monotonicity those phases enforce). **FAILED pre-fix.**
  - `test_missing_screenshots_under_identical_hash_still_report_layer4` — artifact loss surfaces as ran-with-note, never as a silent gate skip.
  Updated `test_pipeline_identical_hash_gates_content_layers` (previously pinned the defective behavior: asserted layer4 `skipped=True` on identical hashes — audit Phase 11 finding "Tests codify defective behavior" listed it for rewrite in Phase 32): now asserts layers 2/3/5/8 gated with reason, layer 4 runs (empty screenshots → 0.0-with-note), layers 6/7/9 unaffected. This update was necessarily pulled forward into this phase because Rule 7 (full suite green) cannot coexist with the old assertion; the remaining three Phase-32 items are untouched.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **535 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~291 s; warning = pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning; 530 prior + 5 new). Frontend `pnpm test` → **7 files / 40 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → exit 0; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= recorded baseline). Backend `uv run --frozen ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 120 files formatted (new test file formatted before commit). Post-fix live probe re-run: pipeline layer4 `skipped=False, score=0.338`, fused 0.1322 (was 0.0352 gated).
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: none mechanical — no earlier phase modified any detection file (verified via fix-log re-read + `git log -- backend/worker/detection/`). Phase 1's harness conventions followed for the new test file (pure-function tests need no DB fixtures, matching `test_detection_fusion_pipeline.py`). Phase 3's `changed`-verdict consumer (`scan_tasks.py:258-262`) analyzed above and preserved.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) The docs-tree claims disproved by this finding (README.md:199, docs/detection-layers.mdx:45, docs/layers/1-content-hash.mdx:55/:83 — audit Finding "Three separate docs assert…") were deliberately NOT edited here: §4.0.5 schedules doc corrections last and that Medium finding is Phase 34's scope; the code-level docstring contradiction IS fixed in this phase. (2) The flagging gap remains: fused risk 0.13 < 0.5 default threshold means asset swaps now produce visible layer-4 evidence and a higher score but still no alert at defaults — owned by Phase 7 (non-model part) and the fusion arc (Phases 8–10), which will re-run this phase's adversarial tests. (3) Degradation-as-zero (score 0.0 indistinguishable from measured-zero in fusion) is untouched — Finding "Capture/probe degradation…", Phase 24 scope; this phase only ensures it can no longer hide behind the gate. (4) New leads: none observed.
- **Commit**: fix(phase6) — visual layer runs regardless of identical content hash

### [PARTIALLY FIXED — Phase 7 scope: the non-fusion-model component] Realistic single-vector defacements fuse far below the default flag threshold AND below the LLM escalation floor — measured end-to-end misses across every layer

*(Header deviates from the §3 template deliberately: this phase implements only the finding's non-model component, exactly as the phase plan directs — "implement whatever non-fusion-model part of the fix belongs here … and explicitly hand off anything that's actually a fusion-coefficient problem to Phase 8." The seed rebuild/refit/integration hand-offs are enumerated under Residual risk below; the finding closes fully when Phases 8–10 land.)*

- **Original severity**: High
- **Phase**: 7 — Single-Vector Defacement Fusion Miss (non-model part)
- **Files changed**: `backend/worker/detection/fusion.py` (module docstring + `_RULE_FLOORS`/`_rule_floor` + floors composed into `layer9_fusion`'s model path and fallback path), `backend/tests/test_rule_floors.py` (new — 26 tests)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced on current code with two scratch probes outside the repo (deleted afterwards) driving real layers + real fitted fusion model. (a) Coefficients extracted from `get_fusion_model()` byte-identical to the audit's fit (l2 −6.5359, l6 −1.3853 negative; l1 +0.1483). (b) The audit's scenario table reproduces within noise: new-domain script injection 0.0959 (audit 0.096), known-domain 0.0018 (audit 0.0016), hidden spam div 0.0035 (audit ~0.004), bare hash flip 0.0406 — all far below flag threshold 0.5, escalation floor 0.35, and mostly below MATERIAL_CHANGE_RISK 0.15. (c) Post-Phase-6 update to the table's asset-swap row: with the visual gate removed, a real banner swap now emits l4=0.098 and fuses to 0.0523 — raised from the gated 0.035 but still sub-threshold, so the finding remains fully intact. (d) New evidence beyond the audit: the Finding-5.1 laundering lever applied to *conclusive* evidence is reachable through the real pipeline today — "HACKED BY CYBER WARRIORS CREW" (l5=1.000, signatures.py's own "essentially conclusive" tier) plus just 5 attacker-added hidden divs fuses to **0.1171**, and with 15 hidden divs to **0.0969** — unflagged, unaudited by any second opinion, cadence not even tightened.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: the finding's mechanism decomposes into four causes. (1) *Fusion-coefficient pathologies* — the near-unregularized fit on collinear fiction seeds assigned sign-inverted coefficients to l2/l6 and near-zero weight to l1, so attack evidence cancels itself (l3=+0.593 contributes +4.21 while the same page's l2=0.503 subtracts −3.29) → Phase 8–10 territory. (2) *Layer-emission gaps* — several scenarios produce little or nothing for fusion to consume because individual layers under-detect (visual score weak for real swaps, hidden-element counting inline-only, semantics cap, leetspellings, cloaking knee breadth) → separately filed findings (Phases 20–23, 36). (3) *Escalation-band dead zone* — ESCALATION_LOW/MATERIAL_CHANGE_RISK are constants disconnected from the actual score distribution; re-deriving them requires the refit distribution → Phase 10. (4) **The non-model root this phase fixes**: no mechanism anywhere in the pipeline guarantees any minimum outcome from high-specificity hostile evidence — the entire decision is one seed-fitted scalar dot-product, so every single-vector result can be pushed arbitrarily low by padding attacker-controlled features. The missing primitive is a deterministic lower bound composed *outside* the fitted model — exactly the "per-layer rule-based overrides … so single strong signals cannot cancel out" mechanism the finding's own fix direction names.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Concurrent access*: N/A — floors are computed inside `layer9_fusion`, a stateless pure function per scan (same analysis as Phase 6); no shared state.
  - *Empty/null/missing/malformed input*: skipped layers contribute feature 0.0 → structurally cannot fire any floor (triggers ≥ 0.40); malformed/non-numeric/NaN scores coerce to 0.0 via `_coerce_score` before rules read them — tested directly (string score, NaN score → no floor).
  - *Boundary numeric values*: each trigger asserted at exactly-below/exactly-at (0.549/0.55, 0.849/0.85 ×2 rules) — tested parametrized.
  - *Partial failure mid-operation*: floor computation sits inside fusion's existing never-raise contract; if the model fit itself explodes, the fallback path applies the same floors (`max(fallback_max_subscore, floor)`) — floors are model-independent by design and tested in the broken-model scenario.
  - *Retry/idempotency*: deterministic pure function of stored artifacts; acks_late redelivery recomputes identical results.
  - *Auth/RBAC interaction*: N/A — worker-internal computation; no endpoint/guard touched.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 6's gate removal verified compatible — no visual-layer floor exists deliberately (benign redesigns share moderate visual scores with attacks, so specificity is insufficient; Phase 6's relative assertion test passes unchanged). Suppression interplay verified: content-rule suppression strips subtrees from both sides BEFORE fusion, so suppressed injections can never fire a floor (tested end-to-end with a css_selector rule). scan_tasks consumers analyzed: `changed` counts per-layer scores (unaffected — floors raise only the final risk), flagged/scheduling consume final risk only.
  - *Backward compatibility*: risk scores change ONLY where a floor fires — i.e., exactly the laundering/single-vector cases the finding documents as defects. No schema/model/evidence-consumer change; frontend renders risk via tone bands on [0,1] which floored values stay within.
  - *Performance at realistic scale*: three float comparisons plus one evidence dict per fusion call — nil against SSIM/MiniLM costs already paid.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: any unexpected exception inside the floor path lands in the pre-existing except → pre-fix fallback behavior (fail-safe direction: worst case equals today's behavior, never worse).
  - *False-positive danger analysis (the core constraint)*: flagged scans create Alerts AND remediation executions, and auto-execute hooks fire destructive webhooks (git_rollback/docker_restart) — an over-eager floor would let benign churn damage production infrastructure. This drove the tier design below.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) max-composed rule floors inside `fusion.py`, keyed to the fixed feature vector — **chosen**: one source of risk truth (UI/alerts/remediation/escalation all consume the single score), coefficient-independent so it survives Phases 8–10 unchanged, structurally monotone (`max` composition: more evidence never lowers risk — the property the audit demanded, guaranteed at system level even before Phase 9 enforces it at model level). (B) Verdict-time rule engine in scan_tasks — rejected: splits "risk" truth across two layers and needs duplicate plumbing into alerts/remediations/UI. (C) Clamping/rewriting features before the model — rejected: corrupts feature-space semantics, couples the fix to the current model, collides with the Phase 8 refit. (D) Doing nothing until the refit — rejected: leaves the today-reachable laundering path (conclusive signature → 0.02–0.12 fused) open for three more phases, and the phase plan explicitly assigns the non-model mechanism to this phase.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `_RULE_FLOORS` + `_rule_floor()` in fusion.py; `layer9_fusion` returns `max(model_probability, fired_floor)` in the success path and `max(fallback_subscore_max, fired_floor)` in the exception path, recording `evidence["rule_floor"] = {"applied": [...], "model_probability": ...}` whenever any floor fires. Tier rationale (specificity sets the tier): **conclusive tier** — strong-tier signature text (trigger ≥0.85: medium patterns weigh 0.55 each and profanity caps at 0.6, so only a strong hit or multiple mediums reach it) and severe cloaking divergence (≥0.85: crawlers served a different site; benign dynamic variation stays at 0.0 via the soft knee) floor at **0.90** ≈ their current unlaundered behavior, making them uncancellable; **ambiguous tier** — brand-new external script/iframe/form-action domain(s) (trigger ≥0.55 ⇔ weighted-new-domain count ≥ ~0.89 on layer 3's saturation curve, i.e. one heavy ref or ~3 lighter ones, with the hash necessarily flipped since layer 3 cannot run otherwise) floors at **0.40**, inside the LLM escalation band: cadence tightens (≥ MATERIAL_CHANGE_RISK), the semantic second opinion engages when configured, but a lone ambiguous vector never auto-alerts — because legitimate sites add vendor scripts routinely. Deliberately NO floor on layers 1/2/6/8 or visual: layer 2 IS the laundering lever (flooring it would let attackers manufacture false alarms at will), and the others carry high benign base rates.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: new `backend/tests/test_rule_floors.py`, 26 tests. Failing-before proof performed formally: `git stash push` of fusion.py → run file → restore. Pre-fix, exactly the 10 floor-dependent tests failed: `test_padded_strong_signature_cannot_launder_below_floor` (real pipeline: 0.0969 < 0.90), `test_severe_cloaking_with_churn_padding_stays_above_floor` (~0.03), `test_severe_cloaking_with_hidden_padding_stays_above_floor_pipeline` (real pipeline, UA variants), `test_new_external_script_domain_reaches_escalation_band_floor` (0.0959 < 0.40), `test_fallback_path_honors_floors` (0.87 < 0.90), three `test_trigger_boundaries[fire]` cases, `test_floor_evidence_shape`, `test_floor_never_lowers_a_high_model_score`. The 16 guards passed BOTH before and after — precisely the designed split (benign/noise/deploy/redesign profiles never trip floors; sub-trigger signature/profanity/flip values need corroboration by design; single light-ref additions editorial-only; skipped layers structurally silent; suppression removes triggers before fusion; malformed inputs coerced; monotonicity sweep non-decreasing; max-composition never lowers a high model score). Assertions deliberately avoid pinning model probabilities (guards check "no floor fired", proofs check floor bounds) so they survive the Phase 8–10 refit.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **560 passed, 1 failed** (561 total = 535 prior + 26 new). The single failure is `tests/test_ai_migration.py::test_ollama_cloud_key_flows_into_deployment` — the environment-triggered DNS64/NAT64 case recorded in the Phase 0 baseline (Finding 0.1; host resolver currently returns a `64:ff9b::/96` AAAA again). Proven pre-existing and unrelated per protocol: fails identically with this phase's changes stashed (re-ran against pre-phase-7 tree mid-session), zero overlap with detection code, owned by Phase 16 (NAT64 SSRF policy) / Phase 30 (hermeticity). Frontend: `pnpm test` → **7 files / 40 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → clean; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= baseline). Backend `uv run --frozen ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 121 files formatted. Post-fix live probe re-run: laundered conclusive signatures fuse to exactly 0.9000 (flagged=True) in every padding configuration (was 0.097–0.607, unflagged for hidden-pad variants); injected-script scenario fuses to 0.4000 inside the escalation band (was 0.0959); every benign control byte-identical to pre-fix (churn 0.0013/0.0026, noise 0.0406).
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 6 — its five adversarial tests pass unchanged (relative assertions proved refit/floor-robust as designed; the pixel-change test's control has no floors to fire). Phase 1 harness conventions followed for the new test file (detection tests need no DB fixtures). No earlier phase modified `worker/detection/fusion.py` (verified via fix-log re-read + git log).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups (explicit hand-offs, per the phase plan)**:
  1. **Phase 8 (Fusion Arc A)**: rebuild `_SEED_ROWS` from measured layer outputs across balanced classes/evasion axes — the coefficient pathologies (l2 −6.54 cancelling everything, l1 ≈ 0, l6 −1.39) remain the dominant cause of sub-threshold fusion for scenarios whose specificity does NOT warrant a floor (known-domain script injection 0.0018, hidden-spam-div-without-heavy-infrastructure 0.0035). Floors deliberately do NOT paper over these: keying floors to layer 2 would hand attackers a false-alarm weapon, and honest closure requires the refit.
  2. **Phases 9–10 (Fusion Arc B/C)**: refit + monotonicity/calibration validation; then re-derive ESCALATION_LOW/HIGH and MATERIAL_CHANGE_RISK from the refitted distribution — Phase 10 must keep the band coherent with these floor constants (floors assume [0.35, 0.75) escalation band and 0.5 default threshold exist as documented; if the band moves, revisit `_RULE_FLOORS` values with justification) and re-run this phase's adversarial tests per the plan.
  3. **Phases 20–23/36**: layer-emission gaps (weak visual scores for real asset swaps, inline-only hidden-element counting, leetspeak variants, semantics cap, cloaking knee breadth) mean some attack classes still emit too little evidence for ANY downstream mechanism to act on — floors cannot see what layers never measure.
  4. Phase 24 (degradation-as-zero) and Phase 32 (tests codifying defective behavior) untouched, per plan.
  5. Docs claims about fusion ("calibrated") remain for the scheduled doc sweeps (33/34/41); module docstring now describes floors honestly without touching docs/.
- **Scratch hygiene**: probe scripts lived in `%TEMP%\opencode\p7`, deleted after use; repo tree contains only the intended changes plus the pre-existing dispositioned items (`scripts/lib.ps1` user-intentional edit, untracked audit files).
- **Commit**: fix(phase7) — rule-based minimum-risk floors bind single-vector attack evidence

### [PARTIALLY FIXED — Phase 8 scope: Fusion Arc Part A, the dataset] Fusion coefficients are sign-inverted for DOM-churn and security-metadata evidence: padding benign noise actively launders unsaturated attacks below the flag threshold

*(Header deviates from the §3 template deliberately: this phase builds the measured training dataset that Phases 9–10 will refit and integrate against — exactly as the phase plan directs ("Fusion Arc Part A — dataset construction"). The coefficient pathologies themselves remain in the fitted model until Phase 9's refit; this entry documents the dataset deliverable and its anti-bias controls.)*

- **Original severity**: High
- **Phase**: 8 — Fusion Arc Part A — Synthetic Dataset Construction
- **Files changed**: `backend/tools/build_fusion_dataset.py` (new — deterministic generator/CLI), `backend/worker/detection/training/fusion_dataset.json` (new — 646-sample committed artifact), `backend/tests/test_fusion_dataset.py` (new — 15 tests), `backend/pyproject.toml` (+ `uv.lock`) — `faker==40.37.0` added to the dev dependency group plus S311 per-file ignores for the seeded-RNG tooling/test files
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced on current code with a scratch probe (deleted afterwards). Coefficients extracted from `get_fusion_model()` identical to the audit's fit: l2 −6.5359, l6 −1.3853 (sign-inverted attack evidence), l1 +0.1483, intercept −3.3104. Monotonicity sweeps with l1=1: raising layer2 0→1 drives risk 0.0406 → 0.0001 monotonically DOWN; layer6 0→1 drives 0.0406 → 0.0105 down. Laundering demo BELOW all Phase-7 floor triggers: semantic-rewrite profile [1,.2,.05,.25,.3,0,0,.9] fuses 0.8502 unpadded but **0.0295** with attacker-padded l2=1.0 — no floor fired (floors bind only conclusive-tier triggers ≥0.85 / new-domain ≥0.55). Also confirmed MiniLM loads from the local HF cache offline (real embeddings available for measurement). Finding fully intact at the model level; Phase 7's system-level floors bound only their trigger tiers.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: `_SEED_ROWS` is a 24-row hand-authored fiction whose feature values were never measured from the layers, with class/churn correlation inverted relative to reality: benign rows carry HIGH authored l2 (redesign 0.6; deploys 0.45/0.35) while positives are authored LOW-l2, and negatives include cert-rotation rows at l6=0.55 while positives sit ≤0.2. With near-unregularized C=50 lbfgs, credit splits along these correlated axes and partial coefficients flip sign. Two structural absences compound it: no training row whatsoever occupies the laundering region (attack label + high churn/metadata features — exactly where an attacker steers the vector), and no row reflects realistic single-vector emission profiles. The missing primitive is **measured, class-balanced, stratified ground truth spanning the reachable feature space** — including high-churn attack rows so a refit can fit non-negative, monotone behavior honestly.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Concurrent access*: N/A — offline single-process deterministic batch tool, no shared state.
  - *Empty/null/missing/malformed input*: scenario-crafting or measurement failures raise loudly (a silent per-row skip would bias the set); the layers' never-raise contract is relied upon; every sample post-validated (8 finite features in [0,1]).
  - *Unicode/non-English*: first-class axis — ar/ru/zh via faker localized lorem plus compact manifesto pools; attacks injected into non-Latin pages AND English rewrites of non-Latin pages (script-flip both directions); artifact written UTF-8 `ensure_ascii=False`.
  - *Boundary numerics*: features rounded to 4 dp after the pipeline's own clamping (`layer_result`/`_coerce_score`); asserted finite in-range for all 646 samples.
  - *Partial failure mid-operation*: artifact written atomically (tmp + `os.replace`) only AFTER full validation passes.
  - *Retry/idempotency*: fixed seed everywhere — per-sample `random.Random(f"{SEED}|{axis}|{idx}")`, per-build faker instance reseeding, shared screenshot render seeds; proven by byte-identical full regeneration (SHA-256 match) after lint-only edits.
  - *Auth/RBAC interaction*: N/A — no app code touched.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 7 floors read only the FEATURE_KEYS vector — unaffected by dataset work; `test_rule_floors.py` deliberately avoids pinning probabilities so it survives the refit; fusion.py untouched (refit = Phase 9); measurements embed Phase 6's gate semantics (visual layer always runs; identical-hash content-layer skips recorded per-sample in `layers_skipped`).
  - *Backward compatibility*: artifact carries `schema_version`; the Phase 9 consumer validates it. No runtime code changed this phase, so nothing existing can regress from the data alone.
  - *Performance at realistic scale*: ~5 min wall for the full build on this machine (real MiniLM encodes dominate); CI exercises smoke scale only (~90 samples, embedder stubbed) in ~15 s.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: generation refuses to write anything if real embeddings are unavailable (fail-loud rather than silently-degraded data); `meta.embedder.mode` records which mode produced an artifact.
  - *Leakage specifics*: input-level uniqueness hard-enforced via sha256 fingerprints over each complete page pair (baseline+current HTML, UA-variant HTMLs, screenshot bytes) — globally unique across all 646 samples; splits assigned per-label with budgeted stratified allocation (disjoint by construction). Distinct inputs may still MEASURE identically where the coarse 8-dim space saturates (257 cross-split exact-feature pairs, e.g. every full cloaking takeover → [0,…,1.0,0]); that is feature-space physics recorded as evidence, not sample duplication.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**:
  - (A) Measure the real layers on procedurally generated scenario pairs — **chosen**: closes the audit's named "seed-fiction vs measured-reality" gap and auto-tracks future layer-emission fixes via regeneration.
  - (B) Hand-author a bigger balanced `_SEED_ROWS` — rejected: recreates the pathology under a new name.
  - (C) Hybrid measure-some/hand-set-rest — rejected: inconsistent attribution poisons coefficient interpretation.
  - Content sourcing sub-decisions (per explicit user direction): procedural generation instead of hand-typed page corpora; benign multilingual text from **faker** (dev group, seeded, offline); attack payload strings sampled **mechanically from the production regex tables themselves** via a small regex-string sampler (coverage tracks detector definitions, not audit literals); ONE leet axis spanning the **full substitution algebra** at seeded per-character probability so currently-detected (h4ck3d/hack3d) and currently-missed (@/$/0 forms) appear at natural rates with generative labels — today's emission boundary is NOT baked into the axes.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `tools/build_fusion_dataset.py`: 34 scenario axes (20 attack / 14 benign) covering every protocol-required evasion family — single-vector per layer family, leet algebra, non-English/script-mixed rewrites and injections, hue-only recolors (attack side paired against a benign theme-refresh twin), sub-threshold combinations, cloaking (heavy + divergence-targeted partial), laundering padding (attack + K hidden churn divs with optional CSP/tls hardening on the CURRENT side, K∈{2..60}), multi-vector screamers; benign drift (rotating ads, timestamps/counters, A/B variants, CSS churn, cache-busting refs, vendor script additions, redesigns, cert/header rotation incl. Finding-4.9's direction-blind header noise, editorial updates, benign UA variation, i18n updates, combined noise). Each sample: seeded identity (nonce brand/domain/archetype/language), parametric DOM skeleton with RNG-drawn element counts, deterministic PIL pseudo-render (banner/hue-shift/extra-lines/jitter deltas on a SHARED render seed), then **real `run_detection` execution** → 8-feature vector + label + axis/language/provenance/skip-mask/fingerprint. 6 sanity rows (train-only): total takeover must peak ≥0.85 among CONTENT layers (2–8; l1 excluded — a pure byte-flip flag), quiet benign rows must stay ≤0.35 there. Anti-bias controls enforced in `validate()`: exact global balance (323/323), strict per-split band (±6%) for splits ≥30 rows with a both-classes-present rule below that, budgeted stratified ~70/15/15 split (per-label holdout budgets make balance structural at any scale), language coverage per class across all four languages, fingerprint uniqueness, nearest-cross-split L∞ distance recorded. Artifact meta records schema version, seed, embedder mode, full counts, leakage evidence, and methodology notes.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: `backend/tests/test_fusion_dataset.py`, 15 tests. Failing-before mapping for infrastructure-deliverable phases: without the new artifact/generator every test fails outright (import/collection error), and the Step-1 probe stands as the pre-fix defect proof. Coverage: schema/meta coherence incl. real-embedder-mode requirement on the shipped artifact; structural validity of all samples incl. gating contract (skipped ⇒ feature 0.0) and fingerprint format/uniqueness; exact class balance; meta-counts ↔ recomputation equality; per-split balance bands; split proportion ≈70/15/15; per-axis stratification reaching train+val+test; both classes covering all four languages; laundering coverage (attack rows with l2≥0.9 alongside benign churn ≥0.4; both classes spanning l6>0) — the specific property that lets a refit avoid sign inversion; leakage-evidence recomputation matches meta; sanity guardrails (train-only, content-layer peaks); regex-sampler correctness (every sampled payload string matches its source production pattern, 200+ draws); smoke-build determinism (two builds byte-identical) and stub-embedder recording, run under the suite-wide `embed_text→None` convention. Full-fidelity generation itself stays outside CI by design (~5 min + local model cache).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **576 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~298 s; warning = pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning; 561 prior + 15 new). Frontend: `pnpm test` → **40 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → clean; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= recorded baseline). Backend `ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 123 files formatted. Supply-chain gates re-run because a dependency was added: `uv lock --check` → clean; `uv run --frozen pip-audit --skip-editable` → no known vulnerabilities (torch CPU-index skip row unchanged — pre-existing Low, Phase 40); `pnpm audit --audit-level high` → No known vulnerabilities. Integrity proof: full regeneration into a temp path after all lint/format fixes → **SHA-256 byte-identical** to the committed artifact (A5D446EC…).

Phase 6's gate removal and Phase 7's floors are embedded in what the measurements exercised and cannot regress from data addition.

### [PARTIALLY FIXED — Phase 9 scope: the refit & calibration artifact] Fusion coefficients are sign-inverted for DOM-churn and security-metadata evidence: padding benign noise actively launders unsaturated attacks below the flag threshold

*(Header deviates from the §3 template deliberately, as in Phases 7–8: this phase delivers the constrained, validated model artifact that Phase 10 will integrate into `layer9_fusion` — exactly as the phase plan directs ("Fusion Arc Part B — Refit & Calibration": refit against the Phase 8 dataset; validate monotonicity, calibration, and cross-validation; document every coefficient). The runtime pipeline still executes the seed-fitted model until Phase 10's integration; production behavior is unchanged by this commit.)*

- **Original severity**: High
- **Phase**: 9 — Fusion Arc Part B — Refit & Calibration
- **Files changed**: `backend/tools/refit_fusion_model.py` (new — deterministic trainer/validator CLI), `backend/worker/detection/training/fusion_model.json` (new — committed model artifact: coefficients + intercept + full provenance/metrics), `backend/tests/test_fusion_refit.py` (new — 11 tests), `backend/pyproject.toml` (+ `uv.lock`) — `scipy==1.18.0` added to the dev dependency group (already locked transitively via scikit-learn; declared direct per the pillow/numpy precedent because the tool imports it)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced on current code with a scratch probe outside the repo (deleted afterwards). Coefficients extracted from `get_fusion_model()` byte-identical to the audit's and Phase 8's records (l2 −6.5359, l6 −1.3853 sign-inverted; l1 +0.1483, intercept −3.3104). Monotonicity sweep re-measured: raising l2 0→1 at l1=1 drives risk 0.0406 → 0.0001 monotonically down; max sweep violation 0.1611 across contexts. Laundering demo through the real fitted model: stealthy-injection profile 0.7337 unpadded → 0.0518 with l2=1.0 padding. Dataset integrity confirmed: sha256 of `fusion_dataset.json` = A5D446EC2810B970… exactly matching Phase 8's pinned record; splits intact (454 train / 96 val / 96 test). New evidence quantifying how badly the seed fit transfers to measured reality: evaluated on the dataset's VAL split it scores accuracy 0.6354 / logloss 1.1541 / Brier 0.2989 / ROC-AUC 0.7146 / ECE 0.3214 with confusion tp=13 fp=0 fn=35 tn=48 — i.e. it misses 73% of measured attacks while claiming near-certainty elsewhere. Finding fully intact at the model level.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: as established by the audit and Phases 7–8 — hand-authored collinear seed rows under a near-unregularized unconstrained fit (C=50) let credit split along correlated axes until two attack-evidence weights flipped sign. The primitive this phase adds is the missing *constraint*: an L2-regularized MAP fit whose coefficients are structurally bounded ≥ 0 (so ∂p/∂xᵢ = σ′(z)·wᵢ ≥ 0 everywhere — sign inversion becomes impossible rather than accidentally absent), fitted exclusively on measured data, selected and validated by held-out discipline, and gated so a degraded fit cannot silently ship.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Concurrent access*: N/A — offline single-process deterministic batch tool, no shared state; runtime untouched this phase.
  - *Empty/null/missing/malformed input*: the loader refuses loudly on missing file, wrong `schema_version`, feature-key order divergence from runtime `FEATURE_KEYS`, wrong feature width, non-finite or out-of-[0,1] features, bad labels, unknown splits, empty/single-class splits — a silently-degraded model is worse than none.
  - *Boundary numerics*: stable formulations throughout (`np.logaddexp` loss, `scipy.special.expit`, clipped log for metrics); optimizer bounds `[(0,None)]×8 + intercept free`; convergence asserted (`res.success`); post-fit verification that no coefficient escaped the box (< −1e-12 fails the build).
  - *Partial failure mid-operation*: artifact written atomically (tmp + `os.replace`) only AFTER every quality gate passes.
  - *Retry/idempotency/determinism*: no RNG anywhere (deterministic stratified folds), no wall-clock in the artifact; rebuilds proven byte-identical repeatedly, including after lint edits and across git checkout line-ending normalization (see fix applied).
  - *Auth/RBAC interaction*: N/A — repo-side dev tooling; no app code touched.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 7 floors read only the fixed FEATURE_KEYS vector and compose as `max(model, floor)` — unaffected by any coefficient change; their band assumptions ([0.35,0.75) escalation, 0.5 default threshold) are deliberately untouched here (coherence is Phase 10's declared job). Phase 6's gate semantics and suppression interplay are embedded in what the measurements already exercised. Phase 8 conventions followed (exact-pin dep declaration, atomic writes, determinism proofs, meta provenance).
  - *Backward compatibility*: artifact carries `schema_version` 1 for Phase 10's consumer validation; since no runtime code changed, nothing existing can regress from this commit alone.
  - *Performance at scale*: CV over the λ grid = 40 tiny fits (~seconds total); hermetic tests re-running the full build stay ~4 s — CI-suitable.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: quality gates (non-negativity, monotonicity ≤1e-12, takeover sanity rows ≥0.85, val AUC/Brier/ECE floors, train-accuracy floor AND ceiling) raise before any write — fail-loud direction. One gate fired during development exactly as designed when an over-broad predicate mis-scoped sanity rows; fixed by scoping to actual `sanity_*` rows (the dataset's own contract).
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**:
  - (A) Constrained MAP logistic regression via scipy L-BFGS-B with box constraints w≥0 wrapped into the existing sklearn-shaped usage — **chosen**: principled objective, deterministic, structural monotonicity, drop-in compatible with Phase 10 integration (`coef_`/`intercept_` semantics preserved).
  - (B) Unconstrained sklearn fit + post-hoc clamp of negatives + intercept-only refit — rejected: ad hoc; clamping distorts the optimized objective, gives no optimality guarantee at the constrained optimum, and remains fragile across regenerations.
  - (C) Recalibrating the old model's output (Platt/isotonic) — rejected: keeps the sign-inverted base model and only patches the output mapping; the audit demands elimination at coefficient level.
  - (D) Different model class (gradient boosting etc.) — rejected for now: docs' own upgrade path reserves that for "once volume allows" (real labeled history), not synthetic-data era.
  - Sub-decision, λ selection: plain argmin of mean fold log-loss slid monotonically to the weakest grid value (near-separable data keeps paying for sharper weights), producing near-step-function scores around thin margins — e.g. benign rendering jitter at l4≈0.095 fused to 0.83. Adopted the standard one-standard-error rule instead (strongest grid λ within 1 SE of the CV optimum): deterministic, train-only, and directly serves the protocol's "not simply saturated" demand.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: new `tools/refit_fusion_model.py`: loads + validates the committed dataset; split discipline TRAIN fits / VAL reports / TEST untouched (reserved for Part C's end-to-end validation, per Phase 8's hand-off); λ∈{0.001..3} selected by deterministic balanced-stratified 5-fold CV on train under the 1-SE rule (**λ=0.03** chosen); final constrained MAP fit on the full train split; gates enforced; artifact written atomically with meta recording dataset sha256 binding (line-ending-insensitive), split counts, selection rule + full CV table, train/val metrics incl. confusion matrices and extreme-prediction fractions, reliability bins (the calibration curve, 10 bins over val), label-conflict disclosure (6 unique train vectors carry both labels — Bayes-error ceiling, reported not hidden, honoring Phase 8's effective-diversity hand-off), recorded sanity-row predictions, per-coefficient justification notes, library versions, and methodology notes. Coefficients (intercept −2.2810): l1 +1.0365, l2 +1.7489, l3 +1.5359, l4 +9.6075, l5 +10.2239, **l6 +0.0000**, l7 +10.9420, l8 +8.4074. Every weight positive except layer 6, which the constraint settles at exactly zero — the honest reading of a channel whose information content is ~0 in both classes (cert/header rotation happens benignly AND hostilely, and layer 6 scores direction-blindly per Finding 4.9) versus the old fit letting collinear noise assign it −1.39.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: new `backend/tests/test_fusion_refit.py` (11 tests, hermetic pure JSON/numpy): schema+dataset-sha256 binding; non-negative finite coefficients aligned to FEATURE_KEYS; empirical per-feature monotonicity sweeps through the exact deployed math (contexts × features × 11 steps, tol 1e-12) plus zero recorded violation; laundering-padding profiles never reduce risk (audit Finding 5.1's exact demo shapes); val calibration/discrimination thresholds (AUC ≥ 0.85, Brier ≤ 0.18, ECE ≤ 0.20, acc ≥ 0.75, logloss ≤ 0.50) with stored-metric equality recomputed from raw data; reliability-bin consistency; non-saturation bounds (train accuracy ∈ [0.70, 0.98], extreme-prediction fractions ≤ 0.5) plus conflict-count recomputation; CV table completeness + independent 1-SE-rule recomputation equaling the stored choice; byte-identical double rebuild JSON-equal to the committed artifact; sanity rows recorded with takeovers enforced ≥ 0.85. **Failing-before proof performed formally, two ways**: (a) with the artifact/tool absent (git stash -u equivalent verified) the test module fails at collection (FileNotFoundError); (b) a scratch probe evaluated each property against the pre-phase `_SEED_ROWS` fit and confirmed violations: negative coefficients (−6.54/−1.39), max monotonicity violation 0.1611, laundering 0.7337→0.0518 under padding, and ALL five val quality thresholds violated (auc/brier/ece/accuracy/logloss).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **586 passed, 1 failed, 1 warning** (~422 s; 575 prior + 11 new). The single failure is `tests/test_ai_migration.py::test_ollama_cloud_key_flows_into_deployment` — the environment-triggered DNS64/NAT64 case recorded in the Phase 0 baseline (Finding 0.1): host resolver currently returns a `64:ff9b::/96` AAAA again; proven pre-existing and unrelated by stashing all four phase files and re-running the test against the clean tree (identical failure, `ProviderConfigError … blocked address (64:ff9b::2224:850f)`); owned by Phase 16/30. Frontend: `pnpm test` → **7 files / 40 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → exit 0; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= recorded baseline); `pnpm audit --audit-level high` → No known vulnerabilities. Backend `uv run --frozen ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 125 files formatted. Supply-chain gates re-run because a dependency declaration changed: `uv lock --check` → clean; `uv run --frozen pip-audit --skip-editable` → no known vulnerabilities (torch CPU-index skip row unchanged — pre-existing Low, Phase 40). Environment note: Docker Desktop was down at session start (engine pipe absent; disposable `wardress-test-pg` exited), which first surfaced as harness connection errors mid-run; restored per db_harness' documented instructions and the full suite re-run green afterwards — unrelated to phase changes (none touch test infrastructure).
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 7 — floors untouched and coefficient-independent by design; their escalation-band assumptions remain valid inputs for Phase 10 rather than assumptions altered here. Phase 8 — its artifact is consumed read-only (sha256 binding verified in-test); builder untouched; regeneration workflow documented (dataset rebuild → rerun trainer). Phases 1–6 — none touch fusion/training surfaces (verified via fix-log re-read + git log); harness self-tests and detection suites re-ran green inside the 586.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups (explicit hand-offs to Phase 10, per the phase plan)**:
  1. Integration into `layer9_fusion` (consume `fusion_model.json`, keep fallback + floors composition, make the evidence dict describe the deployed model honestly — "seed-fitted" strings and the module docstring's "calibrated" claim are now stale in the other direction and belong to Phase 10's edit surface, with docs-tree wording still reserved for the scheduled doc sweeps).
  2. Operating-point coherence: under the refitted model a minority of benign dynamic-content rows land above the default flag threshold (train editorial-rewrite rows observed at 0.61/0.77 where layer-8 drift fired; val FPR@0.5 = 4/48 ≈ 8%; the quiet-benign jitter row sits at 0.418, inside the escalation band). Part C must weigh ESCALATION_LOW/HIGH, MATERIAL_CHANGE_RISK and default-threshold coherence against Phase 7's floor constants using exactly these recorded numbers, then validate on the untouched TEST split (its stated mandate) and re-run Phase 6/7 adversarial tests.
  3. Val FNR@0.5 = 15/48 ≈ 31% reflects the honestly-recorded emission gaps (hue recolors measuring ~invisible, beyond-cap SEO spam, stylesheet-hidden spam) — no downstream mechanism can recover signal the layers never emit; closure belongs to Phases 20–23/36 with dataset regeneration afterwards.
  4. Cross-machine regeneration may differ in float noise; the committed artifact is canonical (byte-stable same-environment rebuilds proven; LF-normalized output + line-ending-insensitive dataset binding make the checks robust to git checkout filters).
