A comprehensive sweep of IANA special-purpose ranges on Python 3.12.11 additionally found ONE MORE member of the inversion class beyond NAT64: SRV deployments `5f00::/16` (also is_global=True ∧ is_reserved=True).
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: Python's IPv6 `is_reserved` and `is_global` derive from different columns of the special-purpose registry and disagree for globally-routed special ranges. The relaxed policy's predicate treated bare `is_reserved` as "unusable reserved space", which is false precisely for that class — so opting into private networks *narrowed* reachability for addresses the default policy accepts, inverting the module's own documented contract ("that flag relaxes only the address-range checks", ssrf.py docstring; "relaxed only ever loosens"). Compounding root: the relaxed predicate existed in TWO hand-copied places (inline closure + `_address_blocked` shared with the pinning transport), a drift surface that let the inversion ship unnoticed.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Concurrent access*: N/A — pure function of address bytes; no shared state.
  - *Boundary numerics (exact range edges)*: `64:ff9b::` (prefix low) and `64:ff9b::ffff:ffff` (high) allowed under BOTH policies post-fix (parametrized tests); `64:ff9b:1::/48` (RFC 8215 local-use NAT64) stays blocked under BOTH policies (is_global=False → no inconsistency to repair; conservative treatment pinned by test); `5f00::1` allowed under opt-in post-fix; `::1` loopback carve-out intact (allowed only under opt-in); ULA fd00::1 / link-local fe80::1 / CGNAT 100.64.0.1 / RFC1918 unchanged (opt-in-only); multicast ff02::1 / unspecified :: / reserved v4 240.0.0.1 / discard-only 100::1 still refused even with opt-in (parametrized guards); IPv4-mapped ::ffff:127.0.0.1 reports loopback properties → behavior unchanged.
  - *Empty/malformed input*: N/A — classification runs after assert_url_allowed's scheme/host parsing; no new parse surface.
  - *Interaction with other findings already fixed (Rule 11)*: `SSRFPinningTransport` imports `_address_blocked`, so the fix flows to the DNS-pinning transport automatically (shared-symbol behavior pinned by test); existing test_ssrf.py BLOCKED_URLS/opt-in/multicast-reserved suites re-ran green unmodified.
  - *Stdlib drift robustness*: the rule keys on the property RELATIONSHIP (`is_reserved and not is_global`) rather than hardcoded prefix lists, so it auto-covers future registry misclassifications (it already covers 5f00::/16, which prefix-enumeration would have missed).
  - *Backward compatibility*: default-policy decisions byte-identical for every address (verified by guard suite); relaxed strictly WIDENS for exactly the global∧reserved class; no schema/data/config change.
  - *Message honesty*: refusal under an active opt-in no longer instructs enabling the already-enabled flag (audit Impact named the misleading text); default-policy refusals keep the original hint verbatim. No test pinned these strings (grepped).
  - *Performance*: two extra boolean property reads per classified address — nil against the DNS resolution that precedes it.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: if the predicate were wrong in the strict direction, the boundary/guard parametrized tests fail loudly; fail direction is bounded (worst case: a refusal where today's code also refuses).
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) general rule — relaxed refuses only `is_multicast or is_unspecified or (is_reserved and not is_global and not is_loopback)` — **chosen**: structurally enforces the documented superset invariant for every current AND future global∧reserved misclassification, minimal semantic statement, matches the audit's primary suggested direction ("relaxed policy is a strict superset of the default policy for all non-genuinely-reserved ranges"). (B) explicit carve-outs for `64:ff9b::/96` (+`64:ff9b:1::/48`) — rejected: whack-a-mole enumeration that provably misses siblings (5f00::/16 was found only by sweeping), brittle across interpreter upgrades. (C) unwrap NAT64-embedded IPv4 and apply v4 rules under the DEFAULT policy too — rejected: tightens the default policy beyond this finding's scope and adds parser machinery; the audit explicitly offers "treat NAT64-synthesized addresses as global in both paths" as acceptable since connection goes through the host's own NAT64 gateway; recorded instead as a New Lead (residual hardening idea). (D) rely on a future Python fixing the classification — rejected: shipped behavior must be pinned now against stdlib drift, not deferred to it. Sub-decision: the duplicated inline predicate was deleted and `assert_url_allowed` now delegates to `_address_blocked` — single source of truth for both callers (assert_url_allowed + pinning transport), eliminating the drift surface that caused this bug.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `_address_blocked`: relaxed branch now `(addr.is_multicast or addr.is_unspecified or (addr.is_reserved and not addr.is_global and not addr.is_loopback))` with a docstring stating the superset invariant and naming the NAT64/SRV registry disagreement. `assert_url_allowed`: inline `blocked()` closure removed; both raise sites delegate to `_address_blocked`; messages append `_PRIVATE_HINT` ("Enable 'allow private networks'…") only when `allow_private_networks=False`.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in new `tests/test_phase16_outbound_fetch.py` (34 tests total, shared with the entry below): `test_nat64_wellknown_allowed_with_optin` (3 boundaries — **FAILED pre-fix**), `test_srv_range_global_reserved_allowed_with_optin` (**FAILED pre-fix**), `test_relaxed_never_stricter_than_default` (property sweep incl. plain-global control — **FAILED pre-fix**), `test_shared_helper_flows_to_pinning_transport` (**FAILED pre-fix**), `test_optin_refusal_message_does_not_suggest_enabling_the_flag` (**FAILED pre-fix**), `test_validate_base_url_accepts_nat64_for_local_provider_types` (**FAILED pre-fix**). Guards passing both pre- and post-fix: `test_nat64_wellknown_allowed_by_default`, `test_localuse_nat64_stays_blocked_in_both_policies`, blocked-class parametrized guards (multicast/unspecified/reserved-v4/discard under opt-in), hostable-range parametrized guards (loopback/RFC1918/ULA/CGNAT/link-local need opt-in), `test_default_refusal_message_keeps_the_hint`. **Failing-before proof performed formally**: `git stash push -- backend/app/ssrf.py backend/app/routers/settings.py` → full file run against pre-fix tree → exactly 17 FAILED / 17 passed; stash popped, all 34 green.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **696 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~501 s; warning = pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning; 662 prior + 34 new). The historically environment-flaky `test_ai_migration.py::test_ollama_cloud_key_flows_into_deployment` passed and is now resolver-independent by construction (a NAT64 answer takes the allowed path post-fix — its exact shape is pinned by the validate_base_url NAT64 test). Frontend: `pnpm test` → **7 files / 40 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → exit 0; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= recorded baseline). Backend `uv run --frozen ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 132 files formatted. No dependency changes → supply-chain gates stand green from Phase 9.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: none mechanical — no earlier phase touched ssrf.py (verified via fix-log re-read + git log); `ssrf_transport.SSRFPinningTransport` consumes the fixed shared helper and its existing tests (test_phase5_ratelimit_ssrf.py) re-ran green inside the 696. Phase 1's harness conventions followed for the new test file.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Default-policy treatment of NAT64-wrapped non-global IPv4 literals (`64:ff9b::7f00:1` embeds loopback) is unchanged — reachability then depends on a translator willing to map non-global IPv4 (RFC 6052 forbids it; operator config could differ); logged as a New Lead for Phase 42 rather than tightened here. (2) Local-use NAT64 (64:ff9b:1::/48) remains blocked under both policies — deliberately conservative; revisit only if a real deployment needs it. (3) Docs never described either policy at this granularity — nothing to sync (doc sweeps are Phases 33/34/41 anyway). (4) New leads observed: see the Phase 16 lead block above (embedded-IPv4 hardening idea; assignment-time capability probe without request-time revalidation).
- **Commit**: fix(phase16) — superset-safe relaxed SSRF policy; ollama pull/list-models validated, rate-limited, honest provider errors


### [FIXED] No brute-force defense on login beyond the shared per-IP window, and failed login attempts are invisible in the audit trail

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 17 — Auth & Audit-Log Robustness
- **Files changed**: `backend/app/routers/auth.py` (login handler rewritten around a persisted per-account lockout), `backend/app/models.py` (User gains `failed_login_attempts`/`locked_until`), `backend/alembic/versions/j4k5l6m7n8p9_user_login_lockout.py` (new — additive migration), `backend/app/ratelimit.py` (`enforce_login_rate_limit` + third limiter instance wired into `reset_limiters`), `backend/app/config.py` (`login_rate_limit_per_ip: int = 30`, 0 disables), `backend/tests/conftest.py` (`LOGIN_RATE_LIMIT_PER_IP=0` suite default), `backend/tests/test_phase17_auth_audit.py` (new — shared with the entries below)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live this session with a scratch probe outside the repo (deleted afterwards): disposable Postgres database from migrations in `wardress-test-pg`, disposable Redis on 127.0.0.1:6399, real app over ASGI transport with PRODUCTION rate-limit defaults (no env disablement). 35 wrong-password POSTs to `/api/auth/login` → every one 401, wall time **2.08 s** (~17 guesses/s), zero 429s; 15 unknown-email attempts identical; the correct password logged in successfully immediately afterwards; `GET /api/audit-log` contained exactly ONE auth row — the single successful `auth.login` — and zero rows for the 50 failures. Finding fully intact; no prior phase had touched the login handler (fix-log re-read + git log).
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: the login endpoint's failure paths mutate nothing: both 401 branches raise without writing any state anywhere (no counter, no lockout, no audit row, no log line), so the only brake is the generic per-IP fixed-window middleware budget (300 req/min shared with all `/api/*` traffic, fully reset every 60 s). Two missing primitives: (a) persisted per-account failure accounting that survives process restarts, and (b) any forensic record of failures at all.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Concurrent access*: N simultaneous failed logins must produce an exact count — increment is a single SQL expression (`failed_login_attempts = failed_login_attempts + 1 … RETURNING`) serialized by Postgres row locks, not read-modify-write; tested with an 8-way asyncio.gather burst through the real ASGI client (exact final count asserted).
  - *Lock-setting race*: two racers computing escalating locks concurrently could shorten each other's deadline → the lock UPDATE carries `WHERE locked_until IS NULL OR locked_until < :candidate`, so it only ever extends. Documented in code.
  - *Mid-lock oracle*: denying only wrong passwords during lockout would let a guesser detect the correct password INSTANTLY mid-lockout (200 vs 429) — so a locked account rejects even the CORRECT password until expiry (pinned by test).
  - *Timing side-channels*: the unknown-email path keeps its dummy-hash verification unchanged; the locked path deliberately skips Argon2 (tripping a lock already required 5 observed failures, so lock-state timing reveals nothing new); generic "Invalid email or password" body preserved byte-for-byte on both non-lock failure shapes (guard test).
  - *DoS-by-lockout* (attacker deliberately locking a victim): accepted tradeoff, bounded by design — escalating windows start at 60 s and cap at 900 s, never permanent lockout; counter resets on every successful login.
  - *Password spraying across accounts* (lockout never trips): closed by the dedicated per-IP login limit (30/min vs the general 300/min) which counts regardless of email — tested by spraying distinct emails under a tight budget and watching later requests draw 429 while early ones got normal 401s.
  - *Empty/malformed/unicode input*: emails ≤320 chars enforced at schema (422 before handler); CJK attempt strings audited without crash (tested); whitespace/lowercase normalization reused from the existing lookup so lockout keys on exactly what was looked up.
  - *Partial failure / retry*: each failed attempt commits its own counter+audit transaction before raising — no partial state.
  - *Auth/RBAC interaction*: none added — the endpoint remains unauthenticated by nature; audit rows for failures carry `actor=None` into the admin-only audit log (reasons distinguish unknown_account/inactive_account/invalid_credentials for forensics).
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 2's audit-write path (atomic-with-caller-commit) reused via `record_audit`; Phase 16 established the `request: Request` + explicit-limiter-call idiom mirrored here; conftest's suite-wide limiter disablement extended to the new setting so unrelated tests can't cross-contaminate buckets (client fixture already resets limiter state per test).
  - *Backward compatibility*: additive columns with server_default — existing installs upgrade with zeroed counters and NULL locks, no data repair; refresh/logout/me untouched; Telegram bot and agent surfaces don't use password login; frontend login page renders `err.message` generically (verified) so 429 bodies need no UI change.
  - *Performance*: successful logins gain two attribute writes inside the existing commit — nil; failed logins add one UPDATE+INSERT commit; locked rejection is FASTER than pre-fix (skips ~100 ms of Argon2).
  - *Fix-failure mode*: if the increment/lock SQL fails unexpectedly the request errors loudly rather than admitting the attempt silently — fail-closed direction.
  - *Config-surface note*: lockout thresholds are module constants in auth.py (precedent: STALE_INFLIGHT/ESCALATION_LOW) rather than Settings fields, deliberately NOT adding three more env knobs to the known compose-forwarding gap (Phase 39 scope); the login IP limit IS a Settings field because it must participate in the existing env-disablement/reset contract shared with its sibling limiters.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) DB-persisted counter+lock on users (atomic increment, conditional lock extension, reset on success) + failure auditing/logging + tight dedicated login IP limiter — **chosen**: satisfies "persists across restarts" explicitly, uses the codebase's established conditional-UPDATE primitive (refresh rotation, remediation claims), covers both single-account guessing AND multi-account spraying. (B) In-memory FixedWindowLimiter keyed on normalized email — rejected: lost on restart (the finding demands persistence) and invisible to operators. (C) CAPTCHA / admin-unlock lockout — rejected: feature-class addition (Rule 2). (D) Only broaden the generic per-IP window — rejected: doesn't survive window reset, doesn't stop spraying, adds no forensics.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: models + migration `j4k5l6m7n8p9`: `users.failed_login_attempts INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, `users.locked_until TIMESTAMPTZ NULL`. `auth.py`: `_LOCKOUT_THRESHOLD=5`, `_LOCKOUT_BASE_SECONDS=60`, `_LOCKOUT_CAP_SECONDS=900`; login enforces the dedicated limiter first, then: unknown account → dummy verify, `auth.login_failed` audit row (target_id None, reason unknown_account), warning log, commit, 401; locked account (`locked_until > now`) → fast 429 + Retry-After with NO verification/counter/audit churn; failed verify or inactive account → `_register_failed_attempt` does atomic RETURNING increment, sets `locked_until = now + min(60·2^(n−5), 900)` when n ≥ threshold via the extend-only UPDATE, stages the audit row (reason/attempts/lockout_engaged), logs, commits, 401; success resets counter and lock alongside rehash/token issuance. `ratelimit.py`: third limiter keyed `login-ip:{ip}` built from the new Settings field.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in new `tests/test_phase17_auth_audit.py` (21 tests, shared file): below-threshold guard; threshold-trips-lock incl. correct-password-denied + full audit-row shape (attempts 1–5, reasons, lockout_engaged flag) and zero `auth.login` rows; persistence across `reset_limiters()` (proves DB-backed); second-lockout-longer-than-first escalation; success-resets-counter; concurrent-failures exact count (8 racers); inactive-account accounting + reason; unknown-email attempts audited with target_id None; unicode attempt recorded; generic-message parity guard; dedicated-login-limit engagement under valid credentials. **Failing-before proof performed formally**: production files stashed (+ new migration moved aside) and the module run against a FRESH disposable database migrated from the stashed tree (`wardress_p17_prefix_test` — the harness guard requires "test" in the name) → **exactly 17 FAILED / 4 passed** (guards passing both sides by design: below-threshold, success-reset trivially true pre-fix, generic-message parity, boundary-256 verbatim); stash popped, all green re-verified.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: joint with the entries below — backend **717 passed / 0 failed / 1 warning** (~493 s final run on the exact tree; warning = pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning), frontend vitest **7 files / 40 passed**, `tsc -b --noEmit` exit 0, oxlint 0 errors / 12 warnings (= recorded baseline), ruff check All checks passed! + format 134 files formatted. Scratch infra (disposable Redis container, two probe databases, probe scripts) deleted after use.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 2 (record_audit atomicity consumed as-is); Phase 16 (limiter-call idiom); Phase 15 (one of its tests needed adaptation for MY migration — see its own entry below). No earlier phase modified login semantics (git log verified).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Deliberate-victim-lockout remains possible within the 900 s cap — standard soft-lock tradeoff; per-(account,IP)-scoped counting is the recorded alternative if abused. (2) Failed-attempt audit volume bounded only by the two IP limits (~330 req/min/IP worst case) — acceptable self-hosted posture. (3) Lockout constants are code constants; Phase 39 owns any env-forwarding sweep. (4) usage.mdx's "rate limits … prevent brute-force attacks" claim is now closer to true but doc wording belongs to Phase 41 per §4.0.5. (5) New leads: none.
- **Commit**: fix(phase17) — persisted login lockout with failure auditing; audit labels capped to column width

### [FIXED] Audit `target_label` overflows its VARCHAR(256): remediation-hook create/update/delete with long site+hook names returns 500 and silently performs no action

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 17 — Auth & Audit-Log Robustness
- **Files changed**: `backend/app/audit.py` (`_MAX_LABEL_CHARS = 256` + `_cap_label` applied to `target_label` in `record_audit`), `backend/tests/test_phase17_auth_audit.py` (shared)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live this session with scratch probes (same disposable harness as the entry above): site named `"S"*200` created 201; hook create with `name="H"*200` → server raised `asyncpg.exceptions.StringDataRightTruncationError: value too long for type character varying(256)` from the audit_log INSERT inside `create_hook` — HTTP 500 over the wire, ZERO hooks persisted (action silently undone); PATCH rename of a hook to 200 chars under the long-named site → same DBAPIError. **New evidence beyond the filed text**: the same overflow reproduces on USER management — `POST /api/users` with a 300-char email (`User.email` is VARCHAR(320), schema allows it) raises the identical truncation error from `user.create`'s audit row (`users.py:76`), leaving the user uncreated and un-loginable. Every `target_label=user.email` writer shares the defect class. Finding fully intact and slightly wider than filed; no prior phase had touched the audit write path.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: `record_audit` caps snapshot VALUES (`_cap_text`, 500 chars into JSONB) but passes `target_label` through verbatim into `String(256)`. Composite label builders concatenate two independently-legal names (`f"{site.name}: {hook.name}"` reaches 200+2+200=402 chars) and user emails reach 320. SQLite ignores VARCHAR widths, so the class shipped invisible to tests (a named instance of Phase 1's structural finding). The missing primitive is width-bounding AT THE WRITE PATH, where every current and future caller is covered structurally.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Boundary numerics*: exactly 256 chars stores VERBATIM (tested — proves no off-by-one); 257+ stores 255 chars + ellipsis marker totaling exactly 256 (unlike `_cap_text`, whose output may exceed its cap — safe there only because snapshots go into unbounded JSONB).
  - *Unicode/multibyte*: Postgres VARCHAR(n) counts CHARACTERS, so Python len()-based slicing is correct — tested with 300 CJK characters (256 stored, marker appended).
  - *Where the cut lands*: head-truncation keeps the site prefix and may clip the hook name for extreme inputs — deliberate, mirroring `_cap_text`'s trailing-ellipsis idiom; before/after SNAPSHOTS retain full metadata either way, so nothing is destroyed, only the display label shortened.
  - *Concurrent access / partial failure / retry*: N/A — pure string transformation staged on the caller's session; behavior otherwise byte-identical.
  - *Auth/RBAC interaction*: none — write-path-only change behind unchanged guards.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: composes with this phase's brute-force fix (failure-audit labels for attempted emails up to 320 chars are now safe by construction); Phase 2's redaction pipeline untouched (webhook URLs remain redacted via key-name rules).
  - *Backward compatibility*: historical rows unaffected (immutable log, no rewrite); callers keep exact strings wherever they fit; nothing reads target_label for equality anywhere (grep — display/filter only), so capped values break nothing.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: wrong-in-the-strict-direction would fail the boundary tests loudly; worst case a shorter label, never a failed action.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) Cap inside `record_audit` at column width with an ellipsis marker — **chosen**: the audit's primary suggested direction verbatim ("truncate composite target labels to the column width inside record_audit, same spirit as _cap_text"); one place, every caller covered forever. (B) Widen the column to 402 via migration — rejected: whack-a-mole against future composite builders; the next caller re-introduces the 500. (C) Cap at each call site — rejected: five sites today, every future caller a fresh landmine. (D) Truncate in routers before calling — subsumed by (C)'s rejection.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `audit.py`: `_MAX_LABEL_CHARS = 256` (documented as matching the column width and WHY the cap lives here), `_cap_label(text)` returning text unchanged when ≤256 else `text[:255] + "…"`, applied as `target_label=_cap_label(target_label) if target_label else None`. No router or model changes.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in `tests/test_phase17_auth_audit.py`: long-hook CREATE succeeds end-to-end with hook persisted + audit label exactly 256 ending "…" (**FAILED pre-fix**: DBAPIError, zero rows); rename+DELETE of a hook under a LONG-NAMED site succeed with audited labels ≤256 (**FAILED pre-fix** once anchored to the long-site scenario — an initial draft built on a short-named site passed both sides and was strengthened rather than accepted); boundary-exact-256 stored verbatim without marker (passes BOTH sides — off-by-one guard); long-email user create + login work (**FAILED pre-fix**); unit parametrize over `_cap_label` lengths 0/255/256/257/400 plus multibyte (**failed pre-fix** via ImportError — helper absent). Failing-before split documented above (formal stash run: 17F/4P across the shared file).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: joint with the entries above — backend 717/0/1, frontend green.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 1's Postgres harness is what makes these tests able to observe VARCHAR widths at all (SQLite masked this exact class — the audit's named mechanism). No earlier phase touched audit.py.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Labels longer than 256 chars are lossy IN THE LABEL (snapshots stay complete) — accepted, honest-marker tradeoff. (2) `AuditLog.actor_email` is VARCHAR(320) matching User.email — verified consistent. (3) Other composite builders (`{site.name}: {rule.type.value}` etc.) measured under 256, untouched. (4) New leads: none.
- **Commit**: fix(phase17) — persisted login lockout with failure auditing; audit labels capped to column width

### Pre-existing-test adaptation forced by this phase's migration (record-keeping entry, not a finding fix)

- **Phase**: 17 — transparency per Rule 8
- **What changed**: `backend/tests/test_sites_router_integrity.py::test_migration_repairs_preexisting_duplicates_keep_oldest` stepped `alembic downgrade -1` from HEAD, which implicitly assumed Phase 15's `i3j4k5l6m7n8` remained the chain tip. This phase appended `j4k5l6m7n8p9` on top, so `-1` no longer reached the pre-repair schema and the test failed in the full regression (planted duplicates survived because the repair revision never re-ran).
- **Resolution**: the downgrade now anchors to the repair revision itself — `alembic("downgrade", "i3j4k5l6m7n8-1")` with a comment explaining why — making the test robust to any future appended revisions instead of re-pinning to head-relative stepping. Test intent (dedupe keeps oldest, unique index present after upgrade) unchanged and green.

### [FIXED] `/api/settings/ai/ollama/pull` fetches an arbitrary unvalidated `base_url` and streams the response back; a swallowed exception also turns bad `provider_id`s into silent redirects

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 16 — Outbound-Fetch / SSRF-Adjacent Medium Fixes
- **Files changed**: `backend/app/routers/settings.py` (`pull_ollama_model`: per-user rate limit, surfaced provider-resolution errors, request-time validation of the effective normalized target URL; `list_ollama_models`: stored base_url validated before the live call; module logger removed — its only user was the swallow this fix eliminates)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live this session with a scratch canary HTTP server + the real app over ASGI transport against the real Postgres harness (probe outside the repo, deleted afterwards): (a) `POST /api/settings/ai/ollama/pull {"base_url": "<canary>", "model": "canary-1"}` → canary logged `POST /api/pull {"model":"canary-1","stream":true}` and the SSE response echoed the canary's content back to the client — fetch+reflection exact as filed; (b) `provider_id="not-a-uuid"` + raw base_url → NO 404, warning logged (Phase 2's observability fix), identical fetch of the raw base_url; (c) internal targets actively attempted — `http://127.0.0.1:5433` actually CONNECTED (to the harness Postgres; "server disconnected without response"), metadata-endpoint and `[::1]` targets attempted; (d) 30 rapid pulls → 30 fetches, zero throttling. Finding fully intact; no prior phase had touched this endpoint's semantics (Phase 2 touched only the except-block's observability, by design deferring the behavior change to the finding's own phase — this one).
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: three cooperating gaps versus the module's own discipline for outbound-fetching configuration surfaces. (1) No request-time validation of the URL actually fetched: provider save/update run `validate_base_url` and the sibling validate endpoint is rate-limited specifically because it "makes a live outbound call… probe internal endpoints", but pull fetched any body-supplied `base_url` raw. (2) A broad `except Exception` converted `_require_provider`'s 404 HTTPException (and anything else) into a silent fallback that changes the FETCH TARGET — operator error altered semantics instead of failing loudly. (3) No per-user rate limit although the endpoint is a POST-anywhere primitive with reflected output. Additionally `list_ollama_models` fetched the STORED base_url without revalidation — stored values can be unvalidated because the legacy migration seeds providers with validation skipped. Missing primitives: validate-the-final-resolved-target at request time; narrow/surfaced error handling; explicit limiter engagement.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Concurrent access*: N/A beyond the limiter — FixedWindowLimiter is thread-safe (lock-guarded buckets); pulls are independent requests.
  - *Malformed/wrong-type input*: unknown or malformed `provider_id` → surfaced 404 (was silent fallback); valid id with non-ollama type → explicit 400 mirroring `list_ollama_models`' existing convention (was silent fall-through pulling from the DEFAULT daemon); non-http scheme → 422 via the SSRF gate (pre-fix: 200 SSE whose error event arrived only after the fetch attempt); >2048-char URLs → 422 via the same gate's length cap.
  - *Empty/null input*: empty-string base_url normalizes to None via the schema's existing validator → falls back to the provider's stored URL, else the code-default Ollama target — unchanged semantics, now validated like everything else.
  - *Effective-target correctness*: validation runs on `normalize_base(base_url)` — the EXACT URL the client fetches (default substitution, trailing-slash strip, `/v1` suffix removal) — pinned by a spy test asserting the captured argument; prevents validating X while fetching Y.
  - *Partial failure ordering*: validation raises BEFORE `record_audit`/commit → rejected pulls leave no misleading audit row (consistent with the atomicity verified in Phase 2); streaming behavior after validation byte-identical to pre-fix.
  - *Relaxed-by-type semantics preserved*: ollama-type targets legitimately live on loopback/private/link-local hosts (local daemons), so `validate_base_url(url, "ollama")` (allow_private=True) is the correct bar — parity with provider save, exactly as the audit's fix direction specifies; hosted types stay strict (google + metadata IP still refused — tested).
  - *Retry/idempotency*: unchanged; 429s add standard `Retry-After` backpressure.
  - *Auth/RBAC*: AdminUser guard untouched (analyst-403 guard test green); the limiter is keyed on the admin's id; budget arithmetic documented below (the auth dependency already charges every authenticated request, so the explicit enforcement shares that bucket — double-charge parity with the validate endpoint is the established idiom).
  - *Unicode/IDN hostnames*: resolution failures surface as clear 422 messages from the SSRF gate; no new parsing surface.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 2's S110 accommodation (module logger + `logger.warning(…, exc_info=True)` in this endpoint) is superseded — the broad except is GONE, so there is nothing left to make observable; both the logger import and definition were removed (their sole consumer was that block) and repo-wide ruff check/format stay green. Phase 9's `validate_base_url` reused unchanged. Conftest's suite-wide limit disablement (RATE_LIMIT_*=0 defaults) keeps the dedicated limit test isolated via the monkeypatch+cache-clear+reset pattern from test_phase5_ratelimit_ssrf.py.
  - *Backward compatibility*: both frontend call sites keep working — saved-provider flow (`providerId` + stored base_url + Bearer key, pinned end-to-end against the canary) and form flow (raw baseUrl); inside Docker the seeded `http://ollama:11434` resolves and passes; failure STATUS changes only for previously-broken inputs (404/400/422 instead of silent wrong-target fetches or late streamed errors).
  - *Performance*: one threaded DNS-resolving validation per pull/list-models call — same cost class as site-create/provider-save; off the event loop via `asyncio.to_thread` inside validate_base_url.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: if validation itself fails unexpectedly the request errors loudly before any outbound call or audit write — fail-closed direction; worst case equals the pre-existing exposure of every other validated path (resolver timeout bounds latency).
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) request-time `validate_base_url(normalize_base(effective_url), "ollama")` in pull + list-models, delete the broad catch (404 propagates naturally, 400 mirrors the sibling endpoint), add `enforce_user_rate_limit(request, str(user.id))` before any work — **chosen** (the audit's suggested direction, matching sibling idioms). (B) Validate only at save time and trust stored values — rejected: migration-seeded providers skip save-time checks BY DESIGN (Docker service names may not resolve at migration time); the request-time check sits at the actual fetch boundary. (C) Route pulls through `SSRFPinningTransport` — rejected: new machinery beyond the finding's declared scope; rebinding is the module-wide documented limitation and provider-save parity is the stated bar; noted as residual. (D) Keep the broad catch but re-raise only HTTPException — rejected: still launders unexpected errors into wrong-target fetches; with `provider_api_keys` handling decryption failures internally there is nothing legitimate left to swallow. (E) Schema-level HttpUrl/max_length on `OllamaPullRequest.base_url` — rejected: pydantic normalization would alter echoed strings without adding DNS/range enforcement; the 2048 cap lives in the gate itself.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `pull_ollama_model` signature gains `request: Request`; first statement enforces the per-user limit (mirroring `validate_ai_provider`). Provider resolution is now straight-line: `_require_provider` 404s propagate; non-ollama type raises 400 "Not an Ollama provider"; the try/except and its logger.warning block were deleted entirely. After resolution, `await validate_base_url(normalize_base(base_url), "ollama")` runs with ProviderConfigError→422 mapping (identical to create/update handlers) BEFORE record_audit/commit/streaming. `list_ollama_models` validates `normalize_base(provider.base_url)` before the live `/api/tags` call, same 422 mapping. Module-level `import logging`/`logger` removed (sole consumer eliminated).
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in `tests/test_phase16_outbound_fetch.py` (the 17 Finding-B tests of the file's 34): `test_unknown_provider_id_surfaces_404_and_fetches_nothing` (**FAILED pre-fix**: 200 + live canary fetch), `test_malformed_uuid_provider_id_also_404s` (**FAILED pre-fix**), `test_non_ollama_provider_returns_400_not_silent_fallback` (**FAILED pre-fix**: fell through toward the default daemon), `test_rejects_non_http_scheme_before_any_request` (**FAILED pre-fix**: 200 stream), `test_validates_the_effective_normalized_target` (spy asserts validation received the normalized fetched URL — **FAILED pre-fix**: never called), `test_endpoint_enforces_user_rate_limit_explicitly` (spy — **FAILED pre-fix**), `test_pull_draws_from_the_same_per_user_budget_as_validate` (limit=5: login=1 token, each pull=2 tokens ⇒ third pull 429; **FAILED pre-fix**: all 200), `test_stored_base_url_validated_before_live_call` (direct-DB provider bypassing save-time validation → 422 instead of attempted fetch; **FAILED pre-fix**: 502 after connection failure). Guards passing both pre- and post-fix: `test_loopback_canary_flow_still_works_end_to_end` (real canary server through the real streaming path: hit counted, model echoed, digest reflected), `test_provider_flow_uses_stored_url_and_key` (stored base_url wins over body, `Authorization: Bearer daemon-key` observed by the canary), `test_analyst_cannot_pull`. The failing-before proof ran formally via git stash (see the Finding-A entry above: 17 FAILED / 17 passed split across the whole file).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: identical runs to the Finding-A entry above (same commit): backend **696 passed / 0 failed / 1 warning**, frontend vitest **40 passed**, `tsc -b --noEmit` clean, oxlint 0 errors / 12 warnings, `ruff check .` All checks passed!, `ruff format --check .` 132 files formatted.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 2 — superseded accommodation as described above (its intent — no silent swallowing — is fulfilled structurally; lint gates re-run green). Phase 9 — `validate_base_url` consumed read-only, unchanged. Phase 1 — harness conventions for the new test file. No earlier phase modified `pull_ollama_model`/`list_ollama_models` semantics (fix-log re-read + git log).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Rebinding TOCTOU applies to these calls as to every non-scanning fetch path (documented module-wide limitation; pinning transport deliberately out of scope here — residual note, not a new gap introduced by this fix). (2) In bare-venv environments without Docker DNS, pulls targeting the seeded `http://ollama:11434` now fail EARLIER with an honest 422 ("Could not resolve host 'ollama'") instead of a later streamed connection error — status-shape change only for previously-broken configurations; frontend renders the hint either way. (3) Assignment-time tool-capability probing (`resolve_tool_capability` → `/api/show`) fetches provider.base_url without request-time revalidation — logged as a New Lead (boolean output, no reflection, admin-only). (4) The docs do not document the pull endpoint's validation/rate-limit behavior; doc sweeps belong to the scheduled phases.
- **Commit**: fix(phase16) — superset-safe relaxed SSRF policy; ollama pull/list-models validated, rate-limited, honest provider errors

### [FIXED] Concurrent scan-now through the stale-supersede path loses the race to the DB index: losers get an unhandled 500 (IntegrityError), their audit rows silently dropped

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 18 — Scan/Baseline Concurrency Races
- **Files changed**: `backend/app/services.py` (`trigger_scan_now` rewritten around a conditional-UPDATE supersession claim + flush-time IntegrityError backstop; `_unique_violation_on` helper factored out of Phase 15's classifier; new `scans_inflight_unique_violation`/`baselines_inflight_unique_violation` predicates), `backend/app/models.py:479-491` (`ix_scans_one_inflight_per_site` declared in ORM metadata — was migration-only), `backend/tests/test_phase18_concurrency_races.py` (new — shared with the entry below)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live this session with a scratch probe outside the repo (deleted afterwards): disposable Postgres database from migrations in the pre-existing `wardress-test-pg` container, disposable Redis on 127.0.0.1:6399, real app over ASGI transport with a widened-commit shim so all racers overlap. Seeded ready baseline + stale in-flight scan (`created_at = now − 11 min`), then 10 barrier-synchronized `POST /api/sites/{id}/scan-now` ×3 rounds → every round had losers escaping as `sqlalchemy.exc.IntegrityError … UniqueViolationError … ix_scans_one_inflight_per_site` straight through the request path (raw ASGITransport re-raises what uvicorn renders as 500; round 0 observed 1×202 + **9 escapes** + 0×409, later rounds 1/5/4 splits matching the audit's {202:4, 500:16, 409:20} aggregate shape); winner-only audit row; data integrity survived (1 pending + 1 failed). Finding fully intact; no prior phase had touched these service functions (fix-log re-read + git log).
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: two stacked gaps. (1) The stale-supersede branch is check-then-set on the in-flight scan's status via plain ORM attribute mutation — under READ COMMITTED nothing arbitrates which racer "owns" the recovery, so all K racers proceed to insert their own Scan. (2) The DB partial index then picks exactly one winner at commit, but the losers' IntegrityError has no translation anywhere between the service layer and uvicorn — so the designed conflict contract (409 like the live-check produces) is unreachable for every race loser, and each loser's staged `scan.now` audit row rolls back silently with its transaction. The missing primitives are a single-winner claim for supersession and an exception translation at the insert boundary that lets the existing unique index act as the universal arbiter.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *K concurrent stale-supersedes* → exactly one 202, K−1×409, zero 500-class escapes; exactly one superseded-failed old row carrying the verbatim historical error string; one new pending scan; one `scan.now` audit row; one enqueue (tested K=8 through the real API).
  - *Live-window race (no stale row)*: two service callers pass the in-flight SELECT before either commits → loser's index violation translated to ConflictError (service-level test with barrier + widen shim; pre-fix produced raw IntegrityError).
  - *Claim lost after another request recovered the row* → rowcount-0 on the conditional UPDATE (its status predicate fails against the committed failed state) → rollback + same-message 409 without attempting an insert.
  - *Sequential stale-supersede preserved byte-for-byte*: same superseded error string, verdict, finished_at set, single audit row (guard test + pre-existing `test_scan_now_recovers_stale_inflight` green).
  - *Beat dispatcher interplay*: the dispatcher's own supersede+insert path (beat_tasks.py:149-169) is deliberately untouched (its tick-loop isolation is Phase 37 scope); API-vs-dispatcher races now resolve safely in BOTH directions — an API loser gets the translated 409 (the classifier covers the shared index), and a dispatcher loser lands in its task wrapper's catch-all exactly as before my change.
  - *Worker redelivery*: acks_late redelivery of a superseded/completed scan short-circuits on terminal status (pre-existing guard, untouched).
  - *Rollback-expiry hazard*: `Session.rollback()` expires loaded instances even under `expire_on_commit=False`; building the conflict message from `site.name` AFTER a rollback raises MissingGreenlet (implicit refresh IO) — caught by the first post-fix test run and fixed at root by hoisting `site_name = site.name` above any rollback path. This would have been a real production 500-in-a-409-path defect had the tests not forced the iteration.
  - *Auth/RBAC*: unchanged — AnalystUser guard on the endpoint; agent tool and Telegram bot inherit the fix through the shared service layer (their ToolError/message translation consumes ConflictError.message unchanged).
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 15's classifier idiom extended (shared `_pg_sqlstate`, same unnamed-23505 rationale — on a scans INSERT only the PK and the in-flight index are unique constraints, and PK violations carry `scans_pkey`, correctly excluded); Phase 3's enqueue-failure contract downstream of commit untouched; Phase 1's harness conventions followed.
  - *Backward compatibility*: no schema change on the scans side (the index already existed since g1h2i3j4k5l6); declaring it in models aligns metadata with the migrated schema and REMOVES one item from `alembic check`'s pre-existing drift set (verified fresh-database: five items → four; the remaining four are the recorded Phase 40 lead, untouched per Rule 1).
  - *Performance*: one extra explicit flush boundary per scan-now; the exception path exists only under contention — nil against request latency.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: misclassification is bounded by constraint-name matching; any other integrity failure re-raises loudly rather than masquerading as 409.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) conditional-UPDATE claim on supersession + IntegrityError→ConflictError translation around the insert flush — chosen: combines the codebase's established primitive (remediation claims Phases 4/5, refresh rotation, login lockout) with the DB index as universal arbiter covering the narrow live-window race AND dispatcher races that the claim alone cannot see. (B) Translation only — works but leaves K−1 losers discovering conflicts via exception machinery and keeps supersession multi-writer (benign today, fragile tomorrow). (C) `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the in-flight read — equivalent outcome but holds the row lock across audit staging; rejected for consistency with the conditional-UPDATE precedent. (D) Fixing the dispatcher loop too — rejected: separate filed finding, Phase 37 scope.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `trigger_scan_now`: the stale branch now executes `UPDATE scans SET status='failed', verdict='error', error=<verbatim string>, finished_at=now WHERE id=:id AND status IN ('pending','running')` with a rowcount arbiter (0 → rollback + ConflictError with the unchanged live-conflict message); the insert gained an explicit flush wrapped so `scans_inflight_unique_violation` / `concurrent_write_aborted` translate to the identical 409 while anything else re-raises. Classifier block refactored minimally: private `_unique_violation_on(exc, constraint)` extracted, `sites_url_unique_violation` delegates (behavior pinned unchanged by its existing unit test), two new predicates added.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in new `tests/test_phase18_concurrency_races.py`: `test_concurrent_scan_now_single_winner_no_integrity_escape` (**FAILED pre-fix**: loser's UniqueViolationError escaped through ASGITransport — the uvicorn-500 class), `test_service_level_insert_race_backstopped` (**FAILED pre-fix** with outcomes `['won','raw-integrity-error']`), `test_sequential_stale_supersede_unchanged` (behavior-preservation guard passing both sides — exact superseded record asserted). Failing-before proof performed formally: `git stash push` of services/models/harness-test + migration file moved aside, module run against a FRESH database migrated to the stashed head (`wardress_p18_prefix_test`) → **exactly 7 FAILED / 1 PASSED**, signatures quoted per entry; stash popped, all green re-verified. (Method note: the suite's `widen_commit_window` needed a 500 ms window rather than Phase 15's 50 ms — ten racers traverse more per-request startup than six; verified empirically that 50 ms produced false non-overlap.)
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: covered jointly by the phase entry below.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 15 classifiers consumed/extended without behavior change (its `TestViolationClassification` re-ran green inside the regression); Phase 3/Phase 4+5 contracts untouched and green; Phase 1 harness self-tests re-ran green including the updated metadata assertion (see adaptation note below).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) The Beat dispatcher still aborts its whole tick if IT loses an insert race (pre-existing Low finding, Phase 37). (2) The unnamed-23505 fallback in the classifiers is justified per INSERT shape, documented in the helper docstring. (3) New leads observed: none.
- **Commit**: fix(phase18) — DB-arbitrated scan-now supersession and baseline in-flight uniqueness

### [FIXED] Baselines have no in-flight uniqueness backstop: concurrent rebaselines all succeed, enqueuing N simultaneous captures of the same site

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 18 — Scan/Baseline Concurrency Races
- **Files changed**: `backend/alembic/versions/k5l6m7n8p9q1_baselines_inflight_unique_index.py` (new — duplicate-repair migration + partial unique index + reversible downgrade), `backend/app/models.py:425-443` (index declared in Baseline metadata alongside its one-current sibling), `backend/app/services.py` (`rebaseline_site` rewritten around the same claim + translation shape), `backend/tests/test_phase18_concurrency_races.py` (shared), `backend/pyproject.toml` (S603 per-file ignore for the alembic-driving proof test, Phase 15 precedent)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live in the same scratch session/probe harness as the entry above: 10 barrier-synchronized `POST /api/sites/{id}/rebaseline` → every round returned **5×202 + 5×409** with SQL count showing **5 simultaneous pending baselines** for the site (= five concurrent Playwright captures enqueued), five `site.rebaseline` success audit rows; identical outcome when the initial capture was stale-pending (supersede variant) or already-resolved (plain variant) — matching the audit's {202:20, 409:20} / 5-pending signature exactly. No DB backstop existed anywhere (`\di uq_baselines*` showed only the one-current index). Finding fully intact.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: scans have had a DB-level in-flight arbiter since migration g1h2i3j4k5l6; baselines never got the equivalent, so `rebaseline_site`'s check-then-insert window had no floor beneath it: concurrent racers all validated against the same pre-commit snapshot and each inserted a pending baseline and enqueued `wardress.capture_baseline`. Resolution afterwards depended on worker-side luck — the one-current index arbitrates who becomes trust anchor only after ALL N captures have run to completion (N full fetches + probes of the monitored target, N−1 failed history rows). The missing primitive is exactly the scans pattern: a partial unique index plus application translation of its violation into the domain conflict vocabulary.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *K concurrent rebaselines (plain)* → one 202, K−1×409, ONE pending baseline, ONE capture enqueue, ONE audit row (tested K=10 through the real API; pre-fix `[202×10]`).
  - *K concurrent rebaselines through stale-supersede* → same single-winner outcome; the old stale row ends failed with the verbatim superseded string written by exactly one winner (loser claims roll back entirely) (tested K=8; pre-fix all-202 with N duplicates).
  - *Pre-existing duplicate data* → the index cannot build over twins, so the migration repairs first: per site, the earliest-created `(created_at, id)` in-flight row is kept untouched and every later one is marked `failed` with an explicit repair marker; policy documented loudly in the migration docstring (duplicates ARE the defect's litter — each twin ran its own capture and fought over the anchor). Exercised end-to-end: anchored downgrade → plant three real duplicates → upgrade → keeper intact, two repaired, index present.
  - *Predicate coverage* → both in-flight statuses rejected (pending AND capturing inserted directly via raw SQL SAVEPOINT-free transactions), terminal rows freely coexist (ready + failed siblings alongside an in-flight one) — mirrors the harness's scans-index test shape.
  - *Flush-ordering hazard* → supersession now executes as an immediate Core UPDATE (before the INSERT flushes), making the sequential stale-supersede order explicit and deterministic instead of relying on ORM unit-of-work ordering; the pre-existing sequential guards (`test_rebaseline_recovers_stale_inflight` etc.) stayed green.
  - *Same rollback-expiry hazard as the scan side* → `site_name` hoisted before any rollback path.
  - *Who else inserts baselines?* → exhaustive grep: only `create_site` (fresh site_id — cannot collide cross-site), bulk import (new sites only), and `rebaseline_site`. The worker mutates but never inserts baselines, so the index creates no new failure surface outside the service function; the dispatcher inserts scans only.
  - *Worker-side capture losers become structurally unreachable from the API* (at most one pending/capturing row per site can exist); the existing wrapper-level IntegrityError handling remains as defense-in-depth for direct task paths.
  - *Auth/RBAC*: unchanged — AnalystUser guard; agent/bot inherit via services.
  - *Downgrade compatibility*: downgrade drops only the index; repaired rows stay failed (history not rewritten backwards); app code simply never sees the violation afterwards.
  - *Performance*: one indexed lookup's worth of maintenance cost on inserts that previously raced unchecked; nil.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: fail-closed — worst case is a 409 where an operator expected success, never duplicate captures.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) mirror the scans pattern exactly — repair migration + partial unique index declared in models + service translation (+ the Phase-4-style claim for supersession shared with finding A) — **chosen**: codebase-established primitive, metadata↔schema alignment, and the audit's primary suggested direction. (B) Advisory-lock serialization of rebaselines — over-broad process-spanning machinery for a row-local decision; rejected. (C) Application-side distributed lock table — reinvents what Postgres indexes already do; rejected. (D) Keep newest in-flight row during repair instead of oldest — arbitrary either way (all twins are race litter); chose oldest for consistency with Phase 15's documented keeper rule.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: migration `k5l6m7n8p9q1` repairs duplicates then creates `uq_baselines_one_inflight_per_site ON baselines(site_id) WHERE status IN ('pending','capturing')` (Postgres-dialect-guarded like its scans sibling; downgrade drops it). Models declare the index beside `uq_baselines_one_current_per_site` (postgresql_where + sqlite_where, matching that sibling's dual-dialect style). `rebaseline_site` gained the same conditional-UPDATE supersession claim and flush-translation as `trigger_scan_now`, using `baselines_inflight_unique_violation`.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in `tests/test_phase18_concurrency_races.py`: `test_concurrent_rebaselines_single_winner_single_capture` (**FAILED pre-fix** `[202×10]`), `test_concurrent_rebaselines_stale_supersede_single_winner` (**FAILED pre-fix**, all-202 duplicates), `test_baseline_inflight_index_backs_the_contract` (**FAILED pre-fix**: index absent; post-fix pins presence, UNIQUE + predicate text, both-status rejection, terminal-row coexistence), `test_migration_repairs_preexisting_duplicate_inflight_baselines` (anchored-downgrade plant-and-upgrade proof; **FAILED pre-fix** — revision unlocatable), `TestViolationClassification::test_sqlstate_predicates` (new predicates incl. cross-constraint negatives; **FAILED pre-fix** via ImportError). All failing-before signatures captured in the formal stash run recorded above.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **725 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~515 s; warning = pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning; 717 prior + 8 new; run on the final formatted tree, with the nine directly-neighboring modules re-run green again after the format pass). Frontend: `pnpm test` → **7 files / 40 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → exit 0; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= recorded baseline exactly). Backend `uv run --frozen ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 136 files formatted. Fresh-database `alembic upgrade head` applies cleanly through the new revision; `alembic check` drift set shrank five → four items (delta documented above; remaining four are Phase 40's lead). No dependency changes → supply-chain gates stand green from Phase 9. Live probe re-run post-fix: every scenario now 1×202 + 9×409 with exactly ONE capture enqueued and zero escaped exceptions. Scratch infra (probe databases ×3, disposable Redis container, probe scripts) deleted after use; repo tree contains only intended changes plus the long-dispositioned items (`scripts/lib.ps1` user-intentional unstaged edit, untracked audit files).
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 15 — its repair-migration/test pattern consciously mirrored (including anchoring downgrades to the named revision, the adaptation Phase 17 introduced); its classifier refactor is behavior-pinned by its own tests, re-run green. Phases 3/4+5/13 — no surface overlap; their suites re-ran green inside the 725. Phase 1 — harness conventions followed; one of its self-tests required a deliberate expectation update (adaptation note below).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Operators with precious duplicate in-flight rows should export before updating — same disclosure shape as Phase 15's repair (though in-flight captures are far less likely to hold valued data than sites). (2) Exact-string/case semantics are N/A here (site_id-scoped index). (3) Dispatcher tick isolation remains Phase 37. (4) New leads observed: none.
- **Commit**: fix(phase18) — DB-arbitrated scan-now supersession and baseline in-flight uniqueness

### Pre-existing-test adaptation forced by this phase (record-keeping entry, not a finding fix)

- **Phase**: 18 — transparency per Rule 8
- **What changed**: `backend/tests/test_test_harness.py::test_migration_only_inflight_index_exists_and_is_partial` pinned `"ix_scans_one_inflight_per_site" not in model_indexes` — true when the harness was built precisely because the index lived only in the migration. This phase's fix declares the index in ORM metadata (the audit's own suggested direction for finding A, and the property that makes metadata↔schema agreement hold), so the old assertion now contradicts the fixed system.
