  - *Alarm-safety analysis (the core design constraint)*: naive weight renormalization would push a totally-blinded scan toward p≈0.5 — above the default flag threshold, i.e. capture failures manufacturing alerts (and crossing the 0.35 material bar → cadence churn / LLM escalation cost). The adjustment therefore saturates at 0.30 < min(MATERIAL_CHANGE_RISK, ESCALATION_LOW)=0.35; coherence pinned by a tripwire test importing both constants.
  - *Backward compatibility*: no schema/migration change; historical scans' layer_scores lack the flag and read as non-degraded (their capture health cannot be reclassified); additive evidence keys only (`unmeasured`, `confidence_mass`, conditional `uncertainty_capped`) — frontend renders unknown keys not at all (verified pattern since Phase 10); scan-detail UI already renders skipped findings' reasons, so degraded layers surface there automatically.
  - *Performance*: one fsum/division per fusion call; site aggregate = ≤10-row indexed query + JSON peek; health aggregate = windowed query capped at 2000 rows — nil against existing costs.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: any fusion exception lands in the pre-existing fallback path; misclassification worst case equals today's conservative silence — fail-safe direction.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**:
  - (A) Two-state taxonomy + capped intercept shrinkage — **chosen**: `skip_result` = structural proof-of-zero (hash gate only; contributes 0.0 at FULL confidence, preserving every quiet-site reading byte-for-byte); `degraded_result` = capture/probe failure (feature 0.0 but excluded from the model's evidence mass, so the intercept shrinks proportionally: z = Σ_known(c·x) + b·(W_known/W_total)); final probability = min(p_adjusted, max(p_assume_zero, CEILING)). This makes degraded scans strictly distinguishable from measured-clean ones, never LOWERS a high conservative reading (monotonicity preserved system-level), and can never manufacture alarms.
  - (B) Naive renormalization over ran layers only (the audit's literal example) — rejected with measured cause: it rescales every identical-DOM rescan (0.0927 → ~0.29) because normal gating would count as uncertainty, shifting all Phase-10/12 operating-point pins and taxing quiet sites for healthy operation.
  - (C) Adding a ninth "confidence" feature — rejected: FEATURE_KEYS order is schema-pinned by the committed dataset/model artifacts (loader refuses divergence); changing it forces a full regeneration arc for no behavioral gain over intercept shrinkage.
  - (D) Representation-only fix (no score change) — rejected: leaves "no fused-risk-visible indication", the finding's core impact, open.
  - Sub-decision (cloaking): bot-blocked/error rotated variants beside a usable reference stay MEASURED 0.0 (deliberately recorded-not-scored per the module's own contract — target refusal is stable observed behavior, and treating it as our-degradation would put Cloudflare-fronted sites into permanent elevated risk); only total-probe death (no variants / unusable reference) degrades.
  - Sub-decision (metadata): TLS-absent on both sides of an http://→http:// pair is a genuine measured property (scheme read from stored final_urls); any other scheme combination leaves TLS unmeasurable and feeds the all-dark predicate.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: types.py gains `degraded_result(reason, **extra)` (score None, skipped True, degraded True). visual/cloaking/metadata emission branches switched to it (visual: missing/unreadable screenshots; cloaking: no UA variants or unusable desktop reference; metadata: fully-dark probe with the http-site and robots-rescue carve-outs above). pipeline.py: baseline-html-missing and crash-isolation branches emit degraded results; hash-gate branch keeps plain `skip_result`. fusion.py: `_unmeasured_keys()` classifies unknown channels (result missing OR degraded); success path computes z over known contributions plus intercept scaled by known-mass share, applies the min/max cap, and records `unmeasured`/`confidence_mass`(always)/`uncertainty_capped`(when saturated); fallback path records `unmeasured` too. scan_tasks.py: `_summarize_layer_scores()` persists `{score, skipped, degraded}` per layer onto the scan row and feeds escalation prompts. routers/sites.py computes `consecutive_degraded_scans` (leading run of completed scans, newest-first, lookback 10, each having ≥1 degraded layer) on the detail endpoint; routers/health.py reports `sites_with_degraded_scans` (sites whose LATEST completed scan in 24h ran degraded, bounded 2000-row window). models.py comment documents the summary shape.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: new `tests/test_phase24_degradation_signaling.py` (22 tests): nine layer-emission tests (each degraded branch + three guards proving bot-blocking, http-site TLS absence, partial probes, and robots-signals stay MEASURED); six fusion-arithmetic tests (full-evidence closed-form equality incl. confidence_mass 1.0; gate-skips-as-proofs with the historical 0.0927 quiet-rescan pin; THE headline property — lost screenshots vs identical pixels now produce different fused risk with the uplift inside the ceiling; catastrophic blinding saturates exactly at the ceiling whether the lone visible channel reads clean or flipped; floors bind beside dark channels; crash-vs-measured-zero contrast); pipeline-level tests (baseline-artifact loss, degraded quiet scans stay under ESCALATION_LOW/MATERIAL_CHANGE_RISK/flag bars); the constants-coherence tripwire; artifact-binding guard; summary-helper unit test; two integration tests through the real app + Postgres harness (site-detail consecutive count with streak-break; health fleet aggregate counting only latest-in-window scans). Updated codifying tests (the audit's own Phase-11 list, pulled forward under Rule 7 exactly as Phase 6 did): `test_detection_layers.py::test_layer4_missing_screenshot_degrades`/`test_layer4_corrupt_png_degrades` (now assert the degraded shape), `test_pipeline_visual_gate.py::test_missing_screenshots_under_identical_hash_still_report_layer4` (degraded shape + fusion unmeasured keys), `test_detection_fusion_pipeline.py::test_layer6_no_metadata_at_all`, `::test_layer7_no_variants_at_all`, `::test_layer7_unusable_reference_degrades` (degraded shapes), `::test_layer6_degraded_header_probe_not_a_downgrade` (fixture given a healthy TLS channel so its header-note intent survives the new all-dark rule), `::test_pipeline_identical_hash_gates_content_layers` (gate-skips asserted NOT degraded; bare-fixture l6/l7 now honestly degraded; visual case updated), `::test_pipeline_changed_hash_runs_all_layers` (fixture given healthy probe data + real PNG so "all layers run" remains literally true), `::test_layer7_variant_fetch_error_degrades` (now also pins not-degraded). **Failing-before proof performed formally**: stashing the eleven production files, the committed module fails at COLLECTION (`ImportError: cannot import name '_UNMEASURED_RISK_CEIL'`), AND a symbol-free scratch runner against the stashed tree reproduced the filed signatures — visual emitted 0.0-with-note with no degraded flag, and measured-identical vs lost-screenshot fused risks were byte-equal (0.09270694361705178 both); stash popped cleanly, all suites green re-verified.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **866 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~576 s; warning = the pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning recorded since Phase 0; 844 prior + 22 new, run on the exact final formatted tree — includes the DNS64-sensitive migration test passing and all Phase-12 end-to-end pins green UNMODIFIED). Frontend: `pnpm test` → **7 files / 40 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → exit 0; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= recorded baseline exactly). Backend `uv`-less venv ruff: `ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 142 files formatted. No dependency changes → supply-chain gates stand green from Phase 9.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 6 — visual-gate suite green unmodified except its own missing-screenshot test rewritten to the corrected contract (its relative assertions proved robust as designed); asset swaps remain visible because screenshots now FAIL LOUDLY instead of silently reading zero. Phase 7 — floor composition untouched and green; dark channels neither fire nor suppress floors. Phases 8–10 — deployed artifact consumed read-only with a new binding guard; every ≥0.30 conservative reading is byte-identical to pre-phase behavior by the cap construction, which is why all Phase-12 scenario pins passed without edits. Phase 12 — all 11 end-to-end pins green unmodified (verified explicitly inside the regression). Phases 20–23 — sibling detection layers' emission contracts untouched; their suites green inside the 866. Phase 1 harness conventions followed for the integration tests (real Postgres, loud-fail).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Malformed (non-finite/non-numeric) sub-scores keep their coerce-to-0.0-with-ran=True contract — arguably also "not a measurement"; changing it alters pinned robustness tests and buys little (numeric faults are rare and already evidence-flagged via score_fault); recorded for Phase 42 to weigh. (2) Per-sub-channel confidence inside a PARTIALLY degraded layer-6 (e.g. TLS dark, headers fine) is not modeled — the layer measures what it can and notes the rest, as before. (3) Aggregates are API-level surfacing; rendering them in the dashboard belongs to the scheduled frontend sweeps (Phase 27 owns health-page honesty, Phase 43 the final sync) — the data now exists for them. (4) The site-detail lookback (10) and health window (24h, 2000 rows) are deliberate bounds, not forensic guarantees. (5) Dataset/model regeneration hand-off (recorded workflow since Phase 20) is unaffected: degraded-channel training rows cannot exist (a dark channel has no feature value to measure), and no emission profile changed for measurable inputs. (6) New leads observed: none.
- **Scratch hygiene**: probes lived in `%TEMP%\opencode\p24` (reverify ×2, smoke, failing_before runner), deleted after use; repo tree contains only intended changes plus the long-dispositioned items (`scripts/lib.ps1` user-intentional unstaged edit, untracked audit/recon files).
- **Commit**: fix(phase24) — capture/probe degradation signaled distinctly: degraded channels read as unknown, not measured-zero

### [FIXED] Concurrent confirms of one pending agent action double-execute the frozen args — check-then-set with no atomic claim (same family as Finding 3.1)

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 25 — Agent Subsystem Medium Fixes
- **Files changed**: `backend/app/agent/guard.py` (`resolve_pending` rewritten around an atomic claim; `expire_stale` converted to the same conditional-UPDATE primitive; imports), `backend/tests/test_phase25_agent_subsystem.py` (new — shared with the entry below)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live this session with a scratch probe outside the repo (deleted afterwards): disposable Postgres database `wardress_p25_probe` built by `alembic upgrade head` in the pre-existing `wardress-test-pg` container, real app over ASGI transport, commit-window shim guaranteeing overlap. Seeded analyst + site + conversation + one pending `create_suppression_rule` action (an executor with NO uniqueness/idempotency backstop, so double-execution is directly observable as duplicate rule rows — the audit's original rebaseline vehicle is now partially masked by Phase 18's in-flight baseline index and Phase 15's sites.url uniqueness, which convert duplicate executions into domain-level conflicts for those specific tools; the underlying guard race itself is unchanged). 6 barrier-synchronized `POST /api/agent/actions/{id}/confirm` → **all six returned 200, six suppression rules created, six execution audit rows** — full N-fold double-execution of the frozen args, worse than the audit's own {200:10, 409:20} observation because precise synchronization plus a backstop-free executor exposes every racer. Finding fully intact; no prior phase had touched `guard.py`'s resolve path (fix-log re-read + git log).
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: identical family to Findings 3.1/6.2's siblings already fixed — `resolve_pending` validates against its request-local snapshot (status read → Python checks → ORM attribute writes → commit) and under READ COMMITTED nothing arbitrates two writers that validated the same `status == pending` precondition: every concurrent confirmer passes, each flips the row to confirmed, each commits, and each then executes the frozen args through `tool.executor`. The Telegram confirm callback shares the same code path, so web+Telegram on one account double-fires too. The missing primitive is exactly the codebase's established one: a conditional UPDATE whose rowcount is the arbiter (Postgres re-evaluates the WHERE when a losing writer's lock wait ends).
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *K concurrent confirms* (double-click, two tabs, web+telegram same account) → exactly one 200 + one execution + one audit effect, K−1×409 reporting "This action was already confirmed." (tested K=6 ×API with backstop-free executor).
  - *Confirm racing cancel (cross-endpoint)* → both target the same `pending` precondition from different endpoints; one conditional-claim shape per endpoint arbitrates: exactly one 200 per pair, final status consistent with the winner, executor ran iff confirm won (tested across 4 raced pairs; pre-fix produced rounds where BOTH returned 200).
  - *Expiry semantics preserved byte-for-byte*: expired-but-still-pending cards settle as `expired` and refuse both confirm and cancel with the historical message. The claim predicate carries `expires_at >= now`, so an action valid at check time but expired by claim-commit time loses cleanly; the loser path refreshes and distinguishes still-pending (⇒ expiry transition via a second conditional UPDATE, so racing expirers stay coherent) vs already-resolved (⇒ historical "already {status}" message). Boundary: `expires_at == now` remains NOT-expired exactly as the old `expires < utcnow()` comparison defined it.
  - *RBAC/tool re-check ordering*: availability + `can_call` run BEFORE the claim (confirm path), so a role change between propose and confirm still fails closed WITHOUT burning the card — the row stays pending until expiry exactly as before (tested); the docstring's re-check contract is preserved. Precedence nuance accepted: an expired AND demoted card now reports the role error first where the old code reported expiry first — fail-closed either way, message-only difference.
  - *Ownership* unchanged: only the proposer may resolve; cross-user races die at the ownership check before any mutation (so all-K racers are same-account by construction).
  - *Crash-after-claim* (the audit's noted honesty gap): a won claim commits before execution; an executor failure leaves the row terminal `confirmed` — visible to the caller as 409, never re-runnable, never silently absorbed (behavior UNCHANGED from pre-fix, now pinned by test; the audit's optional result-column suggestion deliberately not implemented — schema churn beyond this finding's declared defect, recorded as residual).
  - *Janitor interplay*: `expire_stale` was select-then-mutate; a slow confirm commit crossing the expiry instant could be overwritten to `expired` post-execution (pre-fix reachable interleave: janitor SELECT while pending-expired → confirm claims+executes → janitor blind UPDATE wins). Converted to ONE conditional UPDATE carrying the same `status='pending'` predicate — Postgres re-evaluates at write time, so settled rows can never be flipped. Also strictly fewer queries. Existing janitor suite test green unmodified.
  - *Native PG enum*: predicates use `==` (`.is_()` compiles invalid `IS $n` for enums — Phase 4 lesson applied).
  - *Response honesty*: sessions are `expire_on_commit=False`; explicit `db.refresh(action)` after the claim commit on winner paths and after the failed claim on loser paths, so every responder reports the one committed state.
  - *Unicode/malformed input*: N/A — UUID-typed path params, enum-typed state machine; no new parsing surface.
  - *Auth/RBAC interaction*: none added — router guards untouched (`CurrentUser` + ownership inside the guard); viewer has no tier≥2 tools to freeze.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 11's containment gate routes gated actions through this exact function — its 23 tests re-ran green unmodified inside the regression; Phases 4/5/18/19 established the conditional-claim primitive mirrored here; Phase 18/15 domain indexes analyzed above (they mask duplicate executions for rebaseline/add-site specifically — the guard race remained fully open for every other tool).
  - *Backward compatibility*: no schema/migration/enum/API-shape change; statuses and messages preserved; frontend confirm-card UX renders 200/409 shapes it already handles (audit live-probed healthy-flow {200:1, 409:5}).
  - *Performance*: one indexed conditional UPDATE per resolution replaces read+attribute-write; refresh adds one SELECT — nil against LLM/HTTP costs.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: mis-arbitration impossible without the DB lying (rowcount is the arbiter); unexpected errors raise loudly before any execution — fail-closed direction.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) conditional-UPDATE claim with expiry in the predicate + RBAC-before-claim ordering + janitor hardened onto the same primitive — **chosen**: DB-level arbitration, zero lock held across awaits or across executor I/O, preserves every sequential failure semantic (including leave-pending-on-RBAC-refusal), and matches the precedent (refresh rotation auth.py; remediation confirm/dismiss Phases 4/5; scan/baseline supersession Phase 18; alert ack Phase 19). The audit's own suggested direction verbatim ("UPDATE … WHERE id=… AND status='pending' AND expires_at > now(); 0 rows = 409"). (B) `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` then mutate — rejected: holds the row lock across the audit-free claim commit and needs the async dance; rejected for the same reasons as Phases 4/19. (C) Claim-first ordering (before tool/RBAC checks) — rejected: a demoted user would burn the card, regressing the documented re-check semantics. (D) New `firing/executing` lifecycle state — rejected: native-enum migration + frontend/docs churn for zero arbitration gain (the claim already prevents double-execution).
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `resolve_pending`: existence → ownership → (confirm path: tool lookup + `can_call`) → atomic claim (`UPDATE … SET status=confirmed|cancelled, resolved_at=:now WHERE id=:id AND status='pending' AND expires_at >= :now`) → commit → refresh. Rowcount 0 ⇒ refresh; still-pending ⇒ conditional settle-to-`expired` (+commit) with the historical expiry ToolError; otherwise historical "already {status}" ToolError. Cancel path returns refreshed action; confirm path executes the frozen args exactly as before (unchanged executor seam). `expire_stale`: single conditional UPDATE returning rowcount. Module/function docstrings document the claim mechanism. No other production file touched.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: new `backend/tests/test_phase25_agent_subsystem.py` (12 tests, real API/services against real Postgres; commit-window shim guarantees overlap while outcomes are arbitrated by the claim):
  - `test_concurrent_confirms_single_execution_single_audit` — 6 racers through the real API on one backstop-free executor: `[200]+[409×5]` with exact loser message, exactly 1 rule row + 1 audit row, final status confirmed. **FAILED pre-fix** (all-six-200, 6 rules, 6 audits — signature quoted in Step 1).
  - `test_confirm_cancel_race_single_winner` — 4 raced confirm/cancel pairs: exactly one 200 per pair; rule created iff confirm won. **FAILED pre-fix** (observed round: both 200 — `(200 confirmed…, 200 cancelled)`).
  - Behavior-preservation guards passing BOTH sides (the designed split): sequential lifecycle messages (double-confirm / confirm-after-cancel / cancel-after-confirm), expired-settle path, racing expirer coherence, role-refusal leaves card pending, executor-failure-after-claim terminal-not-rerunnable, janitor flips only still-pending expired rows (two-conversation seeding because create_pending supersedes within one conversation).
  - **Failing-before proof performed formally**: `git stash push -- app/agent/guard.py app/agent/tools.py` → module run against the pre-fix tree → **exactly 5 FAILED / 7 PASSED** (the 5 listed above with signatures; the 7 guards green both sides); stash popped cleanly, all 12 green re-verified.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **878 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~681 s; warning = the pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning recorded since Phase 0; 866 prior + 12 new; includes all Phase-11 containment tests, the full detection suites, and the DNS64-sensitive migration test passing). Frontend: `pnpm test` → **7 files / 40 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → exit 0; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= recorded baseline exactly). Backend `ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 143 files formatted (new file formatted before the final runs). No dependency changes → supply-chain gates stand green from Phase 9.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 11 — its dispatcher gate feeds this function; all 23 containment tests green unmodified (frozen-action confirm flow re-proven). Phases 4/5/18/19 — the same primitive applied once more; their suites green inside the regression. Phase 15/18 domain indexes — analyzed as partial masks for two specific tools, not fixes for the guard race itself. Phase 6-verified confirmation lifecycle (freeze card → press → frozen args verbatim) reused as-is. Telegram surface inherits the fix with zero call-site changes (shared `resolve_pending`). No earlier phase modified `guard.py` logic (verified via fix-log re-read + git log).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Executor-failure-after-claim remains terminal-confirmed-without-result (audit's optional result-column suggestion) — pinned honestly by test; implementing it means a schema change that belongs to a deliberate future pass if operators want the visibility. (2) `create_pending`'s supersede loop stays ORM-based: two simultaneous turns in ONE conversation could theoretically race supersede — engine turns are user-driven SSE streams; impact bounded (a cancelled-vs-cancelled race, never an execution); not part of this finding, noted for Phase 42's re-audit to weigh knowingly. (3) Attribution nuance: with N racers the winner is the first COMMITTER (lock-order arbitration) — the only level a database can define; the executed action's audit trail names the true actor. (4) New leads observed: none.
- **Scratch hygiene**: probes lived in `%TEMP%\opencode\p25` (repro ×2, debug runner, task-dump harness) and were deleted after use; transient pytest log files created in backend/ during debugging were removed before commit; repo tree contains only intended changes plus the long-dispositioned items (`scripts/lib.ps1` user-intentional unstaged edit, untracked audit/recon files).
- **Commit**: fix(phase25) — atomic claim makes agent action confirm/cancel single-winner; hooks tool raised to admin

### [FIXED] Agent tool `list_remediation_hooks` exposes admin-only remediation-hook configuration to analysts, contradicting the documented cross-surface RBAC parity

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 25 — Agent Subsystem Medium Fixes
- **Files changed**: `backend/app/agent/tools.py` (registry entry min_role analyst→admin with rationale comment), `backend/tests/test_phase25_agent_subsystem.py` (shared)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live this session (same scratch probe): `tools_for_role(analyst)` included `list_remediation_hooks`; calling the executor directly via `ToolContext(db, analyst_user)` returned the site's hook posture dict (name/action_type/threshold/active/confirm flags) while the SAME analyst's `GET /api/sites/{id}/remediation-hooks` returned **403** — divergence exact as filed. Registry read confirmed `min_role=UserRole.analyst` at the registration site. Not stale: the registry entry had been touched only by Phase 2's import sort and Phase 11's fencing work (neither altered roles).
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: the tool was registered with `min_role=analyst` when the product's chosen boundary for hook data is admin everywhere else — all four REST hook routes carry `AdminUser` (routers/remediation.py:89-107 + update/delete) and the dashboard panel gates on admin. The registry's own module contract says executors call "the same domain logic the REST routers use … so RBAC … stay identical across surfaces"; the declaration simply lagged the boundary decision (hook config points at infrastructure that can roll deployments back). Enforcement lives in `tools_for_role` (declaration filtering — analysts' models never even see the schema) and the dispatcher's `can_call` re-check; both key off this single `min_role` value, so one field was the entire gap.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Analyst surface post-fix*: tool disappears from declarations (smaller model-facing schema — the registry's stated anti-social-engineering property) and the dispatcher refuses a hallucinated/direct call with the standard refusal result fed back as data ({"error": "That action is not available to you."}) — no hook configuration enters model context (tested end-to-end through the real turn loop with seeded hook rows that would have leaked pre-fix).
  - *Admin/viewer surfaces*: admin keeps the tool (parity restored, not capability removal); viewers were never included (min_role monotone) — unaffected.
  - *Pending-action interplay*: the tool is tier 0 read-only — it can NEVER be frozen into an `agent_pending_actions` row, so no historical pending actions reference it and the confirm-time `can_call` re-check (Phase 25 entry above) cannot newly fail on old artifacts.
  - *Webhook URL secrecy*: unchanged and orthogonal — the executor never returned URLs (hint-free by design); this fix closes the metadata channel (names, action types, thresholds, automation posture).
  - *Historical transcripts*: past conversations containing tool results rendered as stored — no rewrite of history; the boundary applies going forward.
  - *Auth/RBAC interaction*: this IS the RBAC fix; no other route/guard touched.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 11's injection containment unaffected (refusal path produces no untrusted content); Phase 6's verified dispatcher refusal mechanics (hallucinated-tool result fed back) are exactly what now serves the analyst case; Phase 19's ack work unrelated. The audit's alternative direction ("consciously lower the REST/UI boundary") rejected outright — hook configuration being admin-only is the documented safety posture (remediation.py module docstring), and weakening a destructive-infrastructure boundary to match a straggling tool would invert the finding's intent.
  - *Backward compatibility*: registry-only change; no schema/model/config change; `engine._tool_label` keeps its label entry (harmless for undeclared-to-analysts tools).
  - *Docs*: `docs/agent.mdx`'s tier-table omissions and RBAC-parity framing remain the filed Phase 34 finding's scope (§4.0.5 schedules doc corrections last); the tools.py module docstring's parity claim becomes TRUE again with this change — no doc edits made here.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: wrong direction (lowering REST instead) would have failed the REST-parity pin test loudly; the tripwire pins admin.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) raise `min_role` to admin matching REST/UI — **chosen**: the audit's primary direction; restores the three-surface agreement with a one-field change; enforcement machinery (declaration filtering + dispatcher re-check) already exists and is tested. (B) Lower REST hook routes to analyst — rejected: weakens a deliberate destructive-infrastructure boundary to accommodate a documentation-lag bug. (C) Keep analyst tier but filter fields further — rejected: the finding is about the role boundary, not field granularity; half-measures preserve the parity contradiction.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: registry entry for `list_remediation_hooks` sets `min_role=UserRole.admin` with a comment stating the cross-surface rationale. Nothing else.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in `tests/test_phase25_agent_subsystem.py`:
  - `test_hooks_tool_min_role_matches_rest_admin_only` — registry tripwire. **FAILED pre-fix** (analyst ≠ admin).
  - `test_analyst_declarations_exclude_hooks_admin_keeps_them` — declaration filtering both directions. **FAILED pre-fix** (present for analyst).
  - `test_dispatcher_refuses_analyst_hooks_call_and_returns_no_config` — end-to-end through the REAL turn loop with a scripted model and SEEDED hook data (name/type/threshold present to make leakage concrete): refusal fed back as the tool result, no done event, and zero hook-config strings anywhere in the captured model context. **FAILED pre-fix** (tool executed; `'list_remediation_hooks' not in ['list_remediation_hooks']` — i.e., it ran).
  - `test_rest_hook_surface_parity_admin_ok_analyst_forbidden` — REST boundary pin (analyst 403 / admin 200 with the seeded hook listed); passes both sides, guarding against the rejected direction (B).
  - Failing-before split covered by the formal stash run recorded in the companion entry above (5 FAILED / 7 PASSED across the whole file).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: joint with the entry above — backend **878 passed / 0 failed / 1 warning**, frontend vitest 40 passed, type-check clean, oxlint 0 errors / 12 warnings, ruff check + format clean.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: none mechanical — the registry entry's only prior modifications were non-semantic (Phase 2 import sort; Phase 11 added `untrusted_output` to a different tool). Phase 6's hallucinated-tool refusal path is consumed as-is; Phase 11 containment unaffected. `docs/agent.mdx` deliberately untouched (Phase 34 scope per §4.0.5).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) If operators later want analysts to see hook posture, the honest route is lowering ALL THREE surfaces together (REST guards + panel gating + this tool) as a documented decision — recorded here so the re-audit weighs it knowingly. (2) docs/agent.mdx corrections (tier table completeness incl. this tool's new floor) belong to the scheduled Phase 34 sweep. (3) New leads observed: none.
- **Commit**: fix(phase25) — atomic claim makes agent action confirm/cancel single-winner; hooks tool raised to admin

### [FIXED] Remediation hook URLs bypass the codebase's entire SSRF discipline — creation accepts metadata/loopback/RFC1918 targets and execution POSTs to them unpinned

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 26 — Remediation-Hook Safety Medium Fixes
- **Files changed**: `backend/app/schemas.py` (`RemediationHookCreate`/`RemediationHookUpdate` gain `allow_private_networks`; shared `_validate_hook_url`/`_validate_fetchable` helpers), `backend/app/routers/remediation.py` (`_assert_target_allowed` threaded through create/update against the EFFECTIVE post-update flag; flag persisted, echoed in `RemediationHookOut`, recorded in audit snapshots), `backend/app/models.py:615-618` (`RemediationHook.allow_private_networks`), `backend/app/remediation.py` (`post_webhook` fires through `SSRFPinningTransport` honoring the hook's opt-in; module docstring states the gate), `backend/worker/remediation_tasks.py` (`_fire` passes the hook's flag; module docstring updated), `backend/alembic/versions/l6m7n8p9q1r2_remediation_hooks_allow_private.py` (new — additive column), `backend/tests/test_phase26_remediation_hooks.py` (new — shared with the entry below)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live this session with a scratch probe outside the repo (deleted afterwards): real app over ASGI transport on the Postgres harness. All five of the audit's targets — cloud-metadata `169.254.169.254`, loopback v4/v6/v4-mapped-v6, RFC1918 `10.9.9.9` — returned **201 Created** verbatim. Not stale: no prior phase had touched hook validation or the firing path's URL handling (fix-log re-read + git log; Phases 4+5 touched only transition claims in these files).
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: two missing enforcement points around one missing policy binding. (1) *Configuration boundary*: `url_shape` checked only scheme+netloc shape — no address-range policy ever ran, so the most privileged configuration surface (hooks drive destructive actions and auto-execute) was the ONLY outbound-fetch path with zero SSRF discipline. (2) *Execution boundary*: `post_webhook` used a plain `httpx.AsyncClient().post(url)`, so even a check-time-validated target resolved DNS a second time at connect — the rebinding window every other fetch path closes (probe.py via `SSRFPinningTransport`). The missing primitives are the shared `assert_url_allowed` gate applied at save with an explicit opt-in for genuinely-internal receivers, and the pinned transport at fire time bound to that same stored decision.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Concurrent access*: N/A for the save-time gate (pure per-request check before any write); execution-time pinning is per-request stateless like probe.py's existing use.
  - *Empty/malformed input*: scheme/netloc shape still enforced first; unresolvable hosts surface as 422 "Could not resolve host" (the gate's own message); control-character/non-numeric-port URLs rejected at the schema layer (Finding B entry below).
  - *Unicode/boundary*: literal IPv4/IPv6 (incl. v4-mapped `::ffff:127.0.0.1`) classified directly by `ipaddress`; global literals pass without opt-in; IDN resolution failures are clear 422s.
  - *Opt-in semantics*: default False refuses loopback/RFC1918/link-local/metadata; True mirrors `sites.allow_private_networks` exactly via the same `_address_blocked` policy (inheriting Phase 16's superset invariant automatically). Hook CRUD is admin-only, so the opt-in is admin-gated by construction (no additional role gate needed — contrast bulk-import's explicit admin check, which exists because imports are analyst-reachable).
  - *Update-path effective-flag correctness*: PATCHing a URL validates against the NEW flag when the request carries one, else the STORED one — validating X while storing Y is impossible; flipping only the flag persists without re-validation (a stored-URL/new-flag mismatch then refuses honestly at fire time — documented residual).
  - *Partial failure ordering*: validation raises BEFORE `record_audit`/commit → refused creates leave no misleading audit row (Phase 16 pull-endpoint precedent); update aborts with no partial mutation committed.
  - *Auth/RBAC*: unchanged guards (AdminUser on all hook routes); analysts/viewers unaffected.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 16's superset-safe relaxed policy is consumed as-is (shared helper + transport); Phase 4+5 claim machinery untouched; Phase 25 raised the read-side tool to admin — this fix tightens nothing it can see (tool output carries no URL/flag fields, unchanged by design).
  - *Redirects*: httpx default (not followed) preserved — a 302 receiver still yields "webhook returned HTTP 302" (Phase 7 verified contract); the pinning transport would re-validate any hop anyway.
  - *Backward compatibility*: additive column server_default false; legacy hooks pointing at internal targets start refusing at fire time with a blocked-address detail until re-saved with the opt-in — fail-closed posture change, intended, disclosed here; global-target hooks byte-identical behavior.
  - *Performance*: one threaded DNS-resolving validation per create/update (same cost class as site-create/provider-save); fires now pay one resolve+pin per request exactly like probe_site already does inside scans.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: misconfiguration direction is fail-closed — worst case refuses a hook creation / refuses a firing that pre-fix would have POSTed internally; never silently allows more than before.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) save-time `assert_url_allowed` + per-hook `allow_private_networks` column mirroring SiteCreate + pinned-transport execution bound to the stored flag — **chosen** (both halves of the audit's suggested direction verbatim; internal receivers remain supportable because the worker legitimately sits next to LAN automation endpoints). (B) refuse private targets outright with NO opt-in — rejected: drops a legitimate deployment shape the audit explicitly carved out ("if internal receivers must be supported"). (C) execution-time validation only (no save gate) — rejected: admins would discover misconfigurations only at incident time; the finding's harm begins at creation accepting the target. (D) route hook CRUD through a new service layer — rejected: this module's established pattern keeps domain logic in routers; no behavior justification.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: schemas — both models gain the flag field plus `_validate_fetchable` (see companion entry); router — `_assert_target_allowed(url, allow_private_networks=…)` via `asyncio.to_thread(assert_url_allowed, …)` mapping `SSRFBlockedError`→422, called on create (body flag) and update (effective flag), flag persisted to the model and surfaced in `RemediationHookOut`/audit snapshots; remediation.py — `post_webhook(url, payload, *, allow_private_networks=False)` constructs its client over `SSRFPinningTransport(allow_private_networks=…)`, TLS verification left at httpx defaults (receiver is operator-controlled infrastructure, unlike scraped sites); worker — `_fire` passes `hook.allow_private_networks`. Migration adds the boolean column.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in new `tests/test_phase26_remediation_hooks.py` (13 tests total, shared file): `test_internal_targets_refused_by_default` (all five audit targets parametrized → 422, zero rows persisted; **FAILED pre-fix**: all 201), `test_private_target_allowed_with_explicit_optin` (**FAILED pre-fix**: unknown field), `test_global_target_passes_without_optin` (guard), `test_patch_revalidates_new_url` (internal-no-optin 422 / internal-with-optin 200 / bare flag flip persists; **FAILED pre-fix**), `test_legacy_internal_hook_fires_nothing_without_optin` (planted pre-phase-style hook + queued execution → `_fire` returns failed, canary receives NOTHING, row terminal with refused detail; **FAILED pre-fix** — the POST went through, the dangerous direction), `test_optin_hook_fires_end_to_end_with_payload` (opt-in legacy hook → succeeded, exactly one POST, HTTP 200 detail; guard passing both sides proving the flow wasn't broken), three direct `post_webhook` units (refusal-without-optin **FAILED pre-fix** — kwarg absent; loopback-with-optin reaches the receiver; invalid-URL tuple shape). Forced adaptations (Rule 7 pull-forwards, documented): `test_phase5_remediation.py::test_create_hook_encrypts_url` fixture URL → global IP literal (NXDOMAIN under the new gate; intent = encryption round-trip + hint redaction, unchanged); `test_phase17_auth_audit.py` three hook payloads add `"allow_private_networks": True` (their `127.0.0.1:9` never-fireable targets kept deliberately); `test_remediation_claim_race.py::_seed_execution` gains the flag param with fireable seeds opting in (loopback canary). Formal failing-before proof: `git stash push` of the five production files → new test file run → **exactly 13 FAILED** (structural absence signatures quoted in session); stash popped, 13 green re-verified; the Step-1 probe stands as the behavioral failing-before record (5×201 / InvalidURL escape / stuck-queued row captured on unmodified code).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: covered jointly by the phase entry below.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 16 — `_address_blocked`/`assert_url_allowed` consumed read-only; the hooks gate inherits the NAT64/SRV superset repair with zero extra code; its 34 tests re-ran green. Phases 4+5 — confirm/dismiss/fire claims untouched; their race suite (with the flag adaptation) re-ran green. Phase 17 — its adapted label-cap tests green. Phase 25 — tool registry untouched. Frontend untouched: the panel renders known keys only (extra response field ignored); UI toggle for the opt-in NOT added (Rule 2 — API-only surface, recorded as residual).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Hooks whose stored URL is internal and which predate this change will fail at fire time until re-saved with the opt-in — operators should review hook posture after updating (each such firing leaves an honest refused detail). (2) No dashboard toggle for `allow_private_networks` yet — admins use the API; surfacing it in the panel belongs to a deliberate frontend pass, not this finding. (3) Flipping the flag alone doesn't re-validate the stored URL (documented above; the fire-time pinning transport catches any resulting mismatch safely). (4) `docs/remediation-hooks.mdx` says nothing about webhook SSRF policy either way — doc sweeps belong to the scheduled phases; module docstrings ARE truthful as of this commit. (5) New leads observed: none.
- **Commit**: fix(phase26) — hook URLs join the SSRF discipline (save-time gate + pinned fires); unfetchable URLs can no longer wedge executions queued

### [FIXED] Health page presents hardcoded, fabricated telemetry as measured data — operators cannot tell which numbers are real

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 27 — Frontend Data-Honesty & Third-Party Leakage
- **Files changed**: `frontend/src/pages/health.tsx` (all fabricated rows/claims removed or replaced with payload-derived values; `Sparkline` component deleted; `beatFresh` predicate factored), `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` (`HealthDetails` gains `sites_with_degraded_scans` — the field has existed in the backend payload since Phase 24 but was missing from the frontend type), `frontend/tests/health-honesty.test.tsx` (new — 7 tests)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: every cited fabrication confirmed present on current code at the cited lines: gateway pane `ping_lat "1.2ms (stable)"` + hardcoded `status operational`; PostgreSQL card `latency check → "1.2ms"` + `conn_pool limit → "20 (active)"` (plus the duplicate in the topology DB pane); Redis card `"redis://"` broker + `"listening"` queue status; worker card constant `"thread dispatcher active"` beside a real detail row whose fallback fabricated `"online"`; Beat badge label hardcoded `"active"` while its own dot computed degraded; static `Sparkline` polyline rendered as activity data on two stat cards; uptime footer claiming `"heartbeat signal stable · LIVE"`. Cross-checked against the actual data source by re-reading `backend/app/routers/health.py`: the API provides NO latency, connection-pool, broker-URL, or thread-dispatcher telemetry at all — those rows could never be honestly rendered. Also confirmed Phase 24's residual hand-off: `HealthDetails.sites_with_degraded_scans` is computed and returned by the backend but rendered nowhere. Not stale; no fix-effort commit had ever touched this file.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: mockup-first construction. The page's layout was authored with placeholder strings standing in for telemetry fields the backend never implemented, decorative trend art presented alongside real numbers, and status text hardcoded instead of derived from the fetched payload — then real fields were wired in around them without removing the fiction. Because fabricated and measured values are indistinguishably styled, the operator's trust boundary erodes page-wide. The missing primitive is render-only-from-payload discipline: every visible value must be an API field, pure formatting of one, or explicitly non-data decoration.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Loading/error states* preserved: skeletons, `"n/a"` / `"offline"` behavior byte-identical on every touched surface.
  - *Null-vs-zero honesty*: `queue_depth: null` (Redis unreadable) previously rendered as `0` — indistinguishable from an empty queue; now renders `"unknown"` in both the stat card and the Redis service row (null = could-not-measure, per Phase 24's taxonomy).
  - *Missing-detail fallback*: worker `detail ?? "online"` fabricated a healthy reading for a null field; now `"—"`.
  - *Derived-status correctness*: gateway pane status now derives from `gatewayStatus` (payload `status`, error→down) with matching tone classes; Beat badge/dots derive from a single factored `beatFresh` predicate (heartbeat fresher than 5 min) — badge text becomes `active|stale|loading|offline`, so the always-green lie at :1001 is structurally unreachable.
  - *Stale-clock skew*: `Date.now()` vs server timestamps is the pre-existing freshness derivation (topology pane used it already); unchanged semantics, now shared.
  - *Same-surface factual misstatement folded in*: the Monitoring Activity block labeled the liveness route `GET /health/live` while the served route is `/api/health/live` (a Phase-12 session lead never filed as its own finding) — corrected as part of this finding's honesty scope since it sits on the exact surface being repaired.
  - *Phase 24 hand-off fulfilled*: new "Degraded Captures (24h)" cell renders `sites_with_degraded_scans` with an honest explainer line ("sites whose latest scan ran with failed capture/probe layers"); grid widened to four columns.
  - *Unicode/boundary inputs*: N/A — no new parsing surface; all values are formatted API fields.
  - *Auth/RBAC*: unchanged — the page sits behind the existing authenticated route; no dependency touched.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 24 owns the backend aggregate and recorded that rendering belongs to this phase — fulfilled here without touching its production code; its API contract consumed read-only (additive TS field only). No other phase modified any frontend src file (git log verified).
  - *Backward compatibility*: additive type field; older cached bundles unaffected; nothing else consumes `HealthDetails`.
  - *Performance*: nil — strictly less DOM than before.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: worst case is hiding a row (fewer lies, not new ones); fail direction bounded toward silence rather than fabrication.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**:
  - (A) Render only measured values: remove rows the API cannot support, replace fabrications with real equivalents where they exist (Redis card now shows true queue depth), remove decorative art entirely — **chosen**.
  - (B) Keep the sparkline but mark it "illustrative" — rejected: a constant polyline carries zero information and marking does not undo the misdirection; removal is the honest option of the audit's allowed pair.
  - (C) Extend the backend to actually measure latency/pool metrics — rejected: new telemetry surface beyond a frontend-honesty fix (feature-class addition, Rule 2); if operators want those numbers they deserve their own designed feature.
  - (D) Reword the fake claims minimally while keeping the rows — rejected: half-fabrication is still fabrication.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: new `frontend/tests/health-honesty.test.tsx` (7 tests; renders the REAL `HealthPage` under a stubbed fetch with a crafted degraded payload — first page-level render test in the suite): absence of every fabricated value (`1.2ms`, `20 (active)`, `thread dispatcher`, `heartbeat signal stable`, `redis://`, `listening`) once data has resolved; sparkline-path absence gated behind a rendered-payload wait; gateway status row derived from the payload (`statusdegraded` present, `statusoperational` absent); Beat badge reads `stale` with no heartbeat and never shows bare `active`; real worker detail surfaced; null queue depth reads `unknown`; degraded-captures cell renders its count. **Failing-before proof performed formally** via `git stash push` of the six production files: across the four new/rewritten test files the pre-fix tree scored **17 FAILED / 10 PASSED**, including 5 of these 7 with the fabricated values present verbatim in the DOM. The proof itself caught a defect in my own test — the original sparkline check passed vacuously against the loading skeleton before the query resolved (exactly the assert-too-early/vacuous-pass failure class recorded as the Phase-12 lead); fixed to await rendered data, which makes it genuinely red pre-fix and green post-fix.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: covered jointly with the entry below (same commit).
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 24 — its fleet aggregate finally surfaced, contract unchanged; Phase 12 lead (liveness route label) closed; no mechanical overlap with any other phase.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) The Database Size card's odd "vacuum allocation" KB caption derives from real bytes and was outside the finding's citation list — left untouched, flagged here for the re-audit's judgment. (2) The topology Redis pane's static `port_binding 6379` (compose default, not measured) likewise uncited — same disposition. (3) If the backend later grows real latency/pool probes, the removed rows may return as honest ones via a deliberate feature pass.
- **Commit**: fix(phase27) — health page renders only measured telemetry; dashboard imagery goes fully local

### [FIXED] Dashboard leaks monitored hostnames to Google's favicon service on every sites view; AI-provider logos fan out to five third-party CDNs including an unpinned jsDelivr @main ref

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 27 — Frontend Data-Honesty & Third-Party Leakage
- **Files changed**: `frontend/src/components/site-avatar.tsx` (new — local deterministic site avatar), `frontend/src/lib/site-avatar.ts` (new — pure helper), `frontend/src/pages/sites.tsx` + `frontend/src/pages/site-detail.tsx` (getFaviconUrl/Google `<img>` replaced), `frontend/src/components/ai-settings-card.tsx` (six-CDN candidate chain and KNOWN_PROVIDER_DOMAINS map deleted; ProviderLogo serves bundled same-origin assets with letter-avatar fallback; header icon localized), `frontend/src/pages/scan-detail.tsx` (three remote icons replaced with a lucide `Sparkles`), `frontend/src/assets/providers/{ollama,openai,anthropic,google,groq,mistral,deepseek,xai}.svg` (new — bundled marks), `frontend/tests/ai-provider-logo.test.tsx` (rewritten), `frontend/tests/site-avatar.test.tsx` + `frontend/tests/no-third-party-image-hosts.test.ts` (new)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: confirmed on current code by read + grep: both pages constructed `https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=<hostname>&sz=64` from every monitored site's URL and rendered it on view (sites list + site detail header); `buildLogoCandidates` produced a six-candidate chain per provider (search-engine favicons sz=128, svgl.app, `cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/glincker/thesvg@main/...`, simpleicons, iconify, models.dev) consumed at three call sites, plus the literal jsDelivr `@main` icon hardcoded four more times (settings header + three in scan-detail's ExplainCard); the shipped `ai-provider-logo.test.tsx` asserted the external favicon URLs as correct behavior. Finding fully intact.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: convenience integrations embedded third-party image services directly in render paths with no privacy gate anywhere between the product's most sensitive dataset (the monitored-site watchlist, including internal hostnames like wiki.corp.local) and external hosts — disclosed anew on every dashboard view, timestamped at a third party, unbounded by any config. A second instance of the same missing primitive hit provider branding: five CDNs fanned per provider with one mutable branch pin (silent content drift channel) and total breakage offline. The primitive the code needed: zero external image hosts — imagery must be bundled same-origin assets or generated locally from data already in the browser.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Malformed/unparsable URL* fed to the avatar → neutral `"?"` initial + stable tone, no throw (pinned).
  - *Unicode/IDN hostnames* → `URL.hostname` yields punycode deterministically; initial = first alphanumeric uppercased; ports/paths/scheme excluded from hashing (pinned).
  - *Tone distribution* → FNV-1a over hostname into a six-entry palette; guard test asserts multiple tones occur across a hostname set (catches hash-degenerate regressions).
  - *Tailwind v4 constraint* → palette entries are complete literal class strings so the scanner generates them (no dynamic interpolation).
  - *XSS property preserved* → logos remain plain `<img>` elements over same-origin asset URLs; SVG markup is never fetched-and-inlined (the original security intent of the old test, kept verbatim).
  - *Unknown/local providers* (`lmstudio`, custom ids, anything unbundled) → letter-avatar badge, zero network; `openai_compatible` maps onto the OpenAI mark exactly as before.
  - *Air-gapped/offline installs* → zero external requests remain; branding renders identically with no connectivity.
  - *Asset visibility* → two sourced SVGs patched for legibility inside `<img>` context (anthropic's `fill="#ffff"` white-on-light → ink; xAI's `currentColor`, indeterminate in an img, → explicit hex). Documented edits to committed static assets.
  - *Licensing/trademark* → brand marks used for identification exactly as the previous CDNs served them; sourcing moved from runtime CDN fetch to one-time build-time vendoring of the same public library (thesvg), noted here for provenance.
  - *Auth/RBAC*: N/A — presentational change behind unchanged guards.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 14's overview copy discloses CDN favicon loading as a known limitation and records that this phase's fix lets Phase 43 strengthen the claim — that hand-off is now actionable; the audit's Phase-11 note listing ai-provider-logo.test.tsx under "tests codify defective behavior" is resolved here (rewrite pulled forward under Rule 7 exactly as Phases 6/24 did). No earlier phase touched any of these files.
  - *Backward compatibility*: `ProviderLogo` drops its unused-after-fix `domain` prop (three internal call sites updated; component is module-exported, no external consumers); visual change on sites list/detail (favicon → letter avatar) is the intended privacy outcome, disclosed here.
  - *Performance*: strictly fewer requests; avatar costs one string hash.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: worst case a missing logo glyph where a letter avatar renders — cosmetic, never a leak.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**:
  - *Watchlist favicons*: (A) local deterministic avatar (hostname-hash tone + initial) — **chosen**: closes the disclosure completely, air-gap safe, ~40 lines. (B) server-side fetch-once-and-cache proxy — rejected: requires a new endpoint plus outbound-fetch/caching/rate-limit machinery (feature-class addition per Rule 2), still hands each new internal hostname upstream once, and adds a fresh SSRF surface; the codebase treats target confidentiality as worth more than favicon imagery.
  - *Provider logos*: (A) bundle a small set of marks for the providers every install actually encounters (the seeded default ollama, the openai-compatible fallback, and common catalog entries) + letter-avatar fallback for everything else — **chosen**, the audit's own "bundle the handful" direction. (B) pin jsDelivr @main→@commit and keep the chain — rejected: leaves five-CDN fan-out, air-gap breakage, and third-party dependence intact. (C) letter avatars everywhere, no bundling — rejected: discards genuine brand recognition where bundling is cheap and safe.
  - *Drift enforcement*: the audit suggested a CSP `img-src`. Deliberately NOT added — app-wide CSP is a high-blast-radius policy surface far beyond this finding (and would need its own designed pass); the enforced-equivalent implemented instead is a source-tree tripwire test failing on any banned image-CDN literal reappearing in src/, which satisfies "future drift fails loudly" at the layer the change actually lives.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: rewritten `ai-provider-logo.test.tsx` (13 tests: same-origin assertion per bundled id incl. openai_compatible mapping; no-inline-SVG XSS property kept; unknown-provider letter-avatar fallback with no `<img>`); new `site-avatar.test.tsx` (determinism, initial extraction incl. port/punycode shapes, malformed-URL safety, tone-distribution guard, component renders initial with no img); new `no-third-party-image-hosts.test.ts` (recursive src/ scan over the six banned host literals — fails listing offenders, enforcing the no-regression property permanently). Failing-before proof shared with the health entry above: formal stash run scored **17 FAILED / 10 PASSED** — 11 of 13 logo tests failed against the old chain (external URLs rendered, unknown-provider img existed), the tripwire failed with offenders enumerated, and the avatar module failed at import (absent). Stash popped cleanly; all 62 green re-verified.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **891 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~611 s; warning = the pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning recorded since Phase 0; no backend files changed). Frontend `pnpm test` → **10 files / 62 passed** (40 baseline + 22 new/rewritten net); `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → exit 0; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / **12 warnings** (= recorded baseline exactly — my first cut added a 13th fast-refresh warning by co-exporting the helper from the component file, fixed at root by moving pure logic to `src/lib/site-avatar.ts` per the repo's lib-helper convention); `pnpm build` → success (pre-existing chunk-size advisory only). Backend `uv run --frozen ruff check .` → All checks passed! No dependency changes → supply-chain gates stand green from Phase 9.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: none mechanical — git log confirms no fix-effort commit touched frontend/src before this phase. Phase 14's privacy-disclosure copy can be strengthened at Phase 43 per its recorded hand-off. Phase 24's aggregate surfaces elsewhere on the same page (companion entry).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Sites lose real favicon imagery in favor of letter avatars — deliberate, documented tradeoff; if operators want imagery back, the honest path is an opt-in server-side resolver designed as its own feature. (2) Unbundled catalog providers (~30+ of the KNOWN map's former entries) show letter avatars — extend `assets/providers/` + `PROVIDER_LOGOS` opportunistically; the fallback keeps them fully functional. (3) App-wide CSP remains unimplemented (rationale above) — recorded for Phase 42's re-audit to weigh knowingly. (4) New leads observed: none (the vacuous-pass instance my proof caught is the already-recorded Phase-12 lead class, now reinforced with a second live example).
- **Scratch hygiene**: probe scripts lived in `%TEMP%\opencode\p27` (SVG sourcing) — assets were vendored directly into the tree; no scratch files remain in the repo. Repo tree contains only intended changes plus the long-dispositioned items (`scripts/lib.ps1` user-intentional unstaged edit, untracked audit/recon files).
- **Commit**: fix(phase27) — health page renders only measured telemetry; dashboard imagery goes fully local

### [FIXED] Schema-valid but unfetchable webhook URL leaves the execution stuck `queued` forever while the re-delivery sweep re-enqueues it every 5 minutes indefinitely

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 26 — Remediation-Hook Safety Medium Fixes
- **Files changed**: `backend/app/schemas.py` (`_validate_fetchable` — httpx.URL construction gate in both Create/Update validators), `backend/app/remediation.py` (`post_webhook` restored to its documented never-raises contract: `SSRFBlockedError` → user-safe message, other non-HTTPError failures → type-name detail), `backend/worker/remediation_tasks.py` (terminal-state guarantee wrapping payload-build + POST in `_fire`), `backend/tests/test_phase26_remediation_hooks.py` (shared)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live this session (scratch probe, deleted afterwards): `http://127.0.0.1:abc/nope` and a control-character URL both saved as hooks (**201**); driving `_fire` directly raised `httpx.InvalidURL: Invalid port: 'abc'` out of the task body (exactly what the Celery wrapper swallows), leaving the row **status=queued, detail=NULL** with the resweep's `created_at < now−5min` predicate primed to re-enqueue it forever (fresh-row cutoff confirmed in-probe; mechanism matches the filed chain link for link). Also established empirically: `httpx.URL()` raises `InvalidURL` at CONSTRUCTION for bad ports AND control chars (making a pure sync schema validator sufficient), port `:99999` constructs cleanly (connect-fails honestly later), and `InvalidURL` subclasses `Exception` (not `HTTPError`) — confirming the handler gap. Finding fully intact.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: four stacked gaps ending in one structural absence — no guaranteed written outcome per delivery attempt. (1) The schema accepted URLs httpx itself cannot build a request for (`urlparse` shape ≠ fetchability). (2) `post_webhook` caught only `httpx.HTTPError`, violating its own docstring contract ("Never raises") for the `InvalidURL` class. (3) `_fire` performed the POST before any status write, so an escaping exception left the claimed row permanently `queued` (wrapper logs "Unexpected error", returns "error", row untouched). (4) The resweep re-enqueues every `queued ∧ created_at < grace` row each run and `created_at` never advances — a permanently-broken firing becomes an infinite error-traceback + queue-message loop, with dismiss 409-ing non-pending rows so no API path resolves it. The missing primitive is boundary-level fetchability validation plus a delivery path where EVERY attempt terminates in a persisted succeeded/failed state.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Concurrent access*: K duplicate `_fire` messages on a bad-URL row → the existing atomic claim arbitrates a single winner (Phase 4+5 machinery, untouched); the winner now writes the terminal failed state while losers skip `not-claimed`.
  - *Crash-after-claim mid-fire*: the executed_at stamp + STALE_INFLIGHT reclaim remains the recovery backstop for hard crashes (DB-down class); the new wrapper covers every in-process exception source, so soft failures reach terminal states immediately instead of waiting out staleness.
  - *Retry/idempotency*: terminal `failed` rows skip redelivery via the not-queued guard and drop out of the resweep's `status=='queued'` predicate — the infinite loop's fuel is gone; genuine lost-enqueue recovery (a truly stranded queued row) is explicitly tested to STILL work.
  - *Detail honesty*: failure details carry exception TYPE NAMES only (`webhook error: InvalidURL`) or `SSRFBlockedError` messages (host/resolved-address material, designed user-safe) — never the URL, which may embed credentials (pinned by a token-echo test).
  - *Malformed-input matrix*: non-numeric port and control chars → 422 at save (both create and PATCH paths); out-of-range numeric ports (`:99999`) construct fine → save allowed → connect fails cleanly → honest failed row (accepted; matches the audit's own observation).
  - *Auth/RBAC*: N/A — schema/worker-layer changes behind unchanged guards.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 4+5's claim/redelivery guards consumed unchanged (their suite re-ran green); the Phase 24 degraded-signaling work is detection-side (unrelated); Phase 16's gate composes — an SSRF refusal during a fire now lands as a failed row with the gate's message instead of wedging (that cross-finding interplay is exercised by this phase's refusal tests).
  - *Backward compatibility*: legacy unfetchable rows already stuck `queued` heal on their NEXT fire attempt (claim → wrapper → failed) rather than requiring manual surgery; no schema/migration change for this finding.
  - *Performance*: one `httpx.URL` construction per save (microseconds); zero steady-state cost added to successful deliveries.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: if the final status COMMIT itself fails (DB outage) the row stays queued and the resweep retries — correct for environmental failures; every application-level failure mode now terminates the row.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) three-layer fix — save-time fetchability construction check + broad-catch restoration of post_webhook's documented contract + `_fire` terminal-state guarantee — **chosen**: kills the class at the boundary AND makes the wedge structurally unreachable regardless of future exception sources. (B) catch `httpx.InvalidURL` specifically in post_webhook — rejected: any future exception class reintroduces the wedge; the docstring already promises never-raises, so honoring it wholesale is the root repair (matches the finding's "map ANY failure to a failed row"). (C) attempt-counter column bounding resweep re-enqueues per row — analyzed and deliberately NOT done: with every attempt terminating in a written outcome, permanent loops are unreachable from this path and the counter is schema churn without a remaining failure to bound; the genuinely-stuck recovery semantics stay untouched. (D) advancing `created_at` on each resweep — rejected: mutates a display/provenance field to fake progress; superseded by (A)'s terminal-state guarantee.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: schemas — `_validate_fetchable(v)` runs `httpx.URL(v)` catching `InvalidURL`→ValueError("webhook_url could not be fetched: …") in Create and Update; remediation.py — `post_webhook` except-chain extended: `SSRFBlockedError` → `(False, f"webhook target refused: {exc}")` (message host/IP-safe), generic `Exception` → `(False, f"webhook error: {type(exc).__name__}")` with a warning log naming the type; worker — `_fire` wraps payload-build + POST in try/except Exception → logger.exception + `ok, detail = False, "delivery failed unexpectedly"` so the single existing status-write path always records the outcome.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in `tests/test_phase26_remediation_hooks.py`: `test_unfetchable_urls_refused_at_save` (parametrized bad-port + control-char → 422, zero rows; **FAILED pre-fix**: both 201); `test_post_webhook_returns_tuple_on_invalid_url` (**FAILED pre-fix**: raised InvalidURL); `test_detail_never_carries_url_credentials` (token-bearing unfetchable URL → credential absent from stored detail; guard); `test_delivery_crash_marks_failed_not_stuck_queued` — monkeypatched payload-builder crash through REAL `_fire` → returns "failed", row `failed`/"delivery failed unexpectedly"/executed_at set (**FAILED pre-fix**: RuntimeError escaped, row stayed queued); `test_resweep_bounded::test_terminal_rows_excluded_genuinely_stuck_recovered` — crashed row backdated past the grace is excluded from `_resweep_undelivered` (stats==0) while a genuinely-stranded stale queued row IS re-enqueued (recovery preserved; positive control passes both sides). Failing-before proof shared with the companion entry (formal stash run: 13/13 FAILED; Step-1 probe holds the behavioral record incl. the stuck-queued row state).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **891 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~813 s; warning = the pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning recorded since Phase 0; 878 prior + 13 new; includes the DNS64-sensitive migration test passing). Frontend: `pnpm test` → **7 files / 40 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → exit 0; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= recorded baseline exactly). Backend `ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 145 files formatted. Migration verification: fresh-database `upgrade head` clean; `downgrade -1` → `upgrade head` round-trip ends at `l6m7n8p9q1r2 (head)`; `alembic check` reports exactly the four pre-existing drift items from the Phase 15 lead — zero new drift. Post-fix live probe re-run: all five internal targets and both unfetchable shapes now return **422** at save (were 201). No dependency changes → supply-chain gates stand green from Phase 9.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phases 4+5 — claim/idempotency machinery consumed unchanged and re-proven green (adapted seeds documented in the companion entry); the resweep's lost-enqueue recovery semantics explicitly preserved by test. Phase 16 — refusal messages flow into failed-row details safely (host/IP material only). Phase 2 — audit-write path untouched (validation precedes staging). No earlier phase modified the delivery path's error handling (verified via fix-log re-read + git log).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Rows already wedged `queued` at upgrade time self-heal on the next fire attempt OR the next resweep-triggered attempt — no operator action required, but the interim traceback noise stops only after that first post-update attempt. (2) `httpx.URL`-level fetchability checking lives in the hook validators only; extending it to `assert_url_allowed` itself would tighten site/provider surfaces too but changes their pinned behavior — recorded as a candidate for Phase 42's re-audit rather than done here. (3) The resweep still re-messages legitimately-in-flight queued rows older than the grace (pre-existing message-churn lead from Phase 4+5, harmless since claims make duplicates skips) — unchanged by design. (4) New leads observed: none.
- **Scratch hygiene**: probes lived in `%TEMP%\opencode\p26` (reverify script + output) and were deleted after use; probe databases `wardress_p26_probe`/`wardress_p26_migcheck` dropped; repo tree contains only intended changes plus the long-dispositioned items (`scripts/lib.ps1` user-intentional unstaged edit, untracked audit/recon files).
- **Commit**: fix(phase26) — hook URLs join the SSRF discipline (save-time gate + pinned fires); unfetchable URLs can no longer wedge executions queued

### [FIXED] Core navigation rows, expanders, tabs and dropdowns are keyboard-inaccessible and ARIA-bare — scans and audit snapshots are unreachable without a mouse

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 28 — Frontend Accessibility
- **Files changed**: `frontend/src/lib/listbox-keys.ts` (new — shared pure keyboard model for all listbox-style menus), `frontend/src/components/ui/select.tsx` (`CustomSelect` internals rewritten into a select-only combobox; public API unchanged), `frontend/src/components/users-card.tsx` (`RoleSelect` upgraded to the same contract; role options hoisted to module-level `ROLE_OPTIONS`), `frontend/src/components/remediation-hooks-panel.tsx` (action-type dropdown upgraded), `frontend/src/pages/audit.tsx` (snapshot expanders became real disclosure buttons with `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`; the target-type filter dropdown upgraded — same-class instance folded in, see Step 3), `frontend/src/pages/sites.tsx` (row navigation moved from `<tr onClick>` to a row-stretched real link), `frontend/src/pages/site-detail.tsx` (scan rows likewise; tab strip given full ARIA tab semantics with roving tabindex and arrow-key activation), `frontend/tests/a11y-navigation.test.tsx` (new — 9 tests), `frontend/tests/a11y-selects.test.tsx` (new — 11 tests)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: every cited surface confirmed intact on current code by read + grep before any edit: sites rows navigate via bare `onClick` on `<tr>` (sites.tsx:248-251) with only the two delete buttons focusable in the tbody — the audit's keyboard probe signature; scan rows identical (site-detail.tsx:720-723); audit snapshot headers were plain `<div onClick>` with zero focusable elements (audit.tsx:196-203); the four site tabs exposed no tab roles or state (site-detail.tsx:479-534); the hooks action-type trigger lacked `aria-expanded`/`aria-haspopup` and its options were arrow-key-unreachable (remediation-hooks-panel.tsx:218-256); `CustomSelect` handled Escape but no arrows/focus movement/listbox semantics (ui/select.tsx:46-97); users-card's `RoleSelect` was the same shape (:69-107). Repo-wide greps confirmed exactly four hand-rolled menus exist (the three cited plus the audit page's own target-type filter) and that `aria-expanded` already existed on the surfaces NOT flagged (app-shell mobile menu, finding-card evidence, suppression-panel drawer) — the finding's surface list is precise. Not stale: no fix-effort commit had touched any of these files except Phase 27 (avatar/logo imagery on sites/site-detail — orthogonal to this finding).
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: one missing primitive expressed at three levels. The product's interactive affordances were built as *mouse-event handlers attached to non-focusable DOM*: an `<tr>`/`<div>` with `onClick` has no place in the tab order, no role, no accessible name, and no state for assistive technology — so the primary objects of the product (a site, a scan, an audit snapshot) were structurally unreachable without a pointer. For the custom menus and tabs the gap compounded: beyond focusability, the WAI-ARIA interaction contracts themselves (listbox: `aria-expanded/haspopup/controls` on the trigger, focus landing on options, Arrow/Home/End walking, Escape restore; tabs: `tablist/tab/tabpanel` wiring, `aria-selected`, roving tabindex) were simply never implemented — Enter opened a menu whose options could not be reached by any key. The missing primitive is *native interactive elements carrying their own semantics* (links/buttons) plus a single deterministic listbox keyboard model shared by every menu.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Rows vs nested controls*: the delete button inside each site row must stay independently clickable above the stretched-link overlay → button cell raised (`relative z-10`); the old `e.stopPropagation()` became dead once the row handler was removed. Mouse behavior preserved: the overlay covers the whole row, so clicking anywhere navigates exactly as before.
  - *Accessible-name quality*: site links name from visible content ("Acme Corp"); scan rows have no id text, so the link gets a composed `aria-label` ("Open flagged scan started …") that contains the visible status word (Label-in-Name, WCAG 2.5.3). Audit expander names come from the full row content.
  - *Rows without snapshots* must NOT become buttons: non-detail audit rows stay plain content (a disabled button would be announced yet useless) — pinned by test.
  - *Empty-string option values*: settings' "acts as user" select legitimately uses `value: ""` for "None" — this directly ruled out naive adoption of Radix Select (whose SelectItem throws on empty values); the hand-built combobox keeps them working (tested).
  - *Focus management*: opening (by click OR keyboard) lands focus on the selected option; commit returns focus to the trigger so subsequent Tab continues after the control; Tab inside an open menu dismisses WITHOUT yanking focus back; clamped (non-wrapping) arrow traversal like native selects.
  - *Escape ownership inside modals*: the Add-hook dialog and create-user dialog are Radix dialogs whose own Escape handling closes the whole dialog. While a menu is open, the menu must OWN Escape: each menu registers a window-level CAPTURE listener during its open lifetime that stops propagation, closes only the menu, and restores trigger focus — window capture precedes Radix's document-capture listener regardless of registration order. (First attempts — bubble-phase handlers on the container, then React root capture — both lost to Radix's document-capture listener; iteration documented under Step 6.)
  - *Tabs semantics*: roving tabindex (active tab `tabindex=0`, others `-1`), ArrowLeft/Right/Home/End with wrap-around, activation-follows-focus (simple auto-activation set), every panel labelled by its tab via `aria-labelledby`/`aria-controls`.
  - *Concurrent access*: N/A — pure presentational state per component instance; no shared state.
  - *Unicode/malformed input*: N/A — labels render as text; menu math is index arithmetic over the option count.
  - *Auth/RBAC interaction*: none changed — no guards touched; tests authenticate as admin only where the tested control itself is admin-gated (hook panel, delete button rendering).
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 27's SiteAvatar/provider-logo work sits inside the files edited here but touches disjoint JSX; its three test suites re-ran green unmodified. Phase 26's hook save-time SSRF validation is backend-side; the panel change here is purely its dropdown. No other phase touched these frontend files.
  - *Backward compatibility*: `CustomSelect` keeps its exact exported API (`SelectOption`/`CustomSelectProps`/`CustomSelect`) — settings.tsx's two call sites and users-card's third needed zero changes; DOM/CSS structure of every menu preserved (same classes, same backdrop pattern) so visuals are unchanged; no new dependencies; no schema/type changes.
  - *Performance*: nil — a handful of listeners per open menu, removed on close.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: worst case is a cosmetic focus-placement quirk; every behavior added is test-pinned, and the pre-existing mouse paths are byte-equivalent (guards green both sides).
  - *Scope note (folded-in same-class instance)*: the audit page's target-type filter dropdown was not in the finding's location list but is the fourth and last hand-rolled menu, sits in a file already being repaired for expanders, and exhibits the exact defect class named in the title; fixed under the same mechanism and recorded here rather than silently (precedent: Phase 27's same-surface route-label correction).
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**:
  - *Menus*: (A) hand-implement the select-only-combobox contract natively, sharing ONE pure keyboard model (`lib/listbox-keys.ts`: Arrow/Home/End walking with clamping, Enter/Space→commit, Tab→dismiss-without-refocus; Escape deliberately excluded because it must also work from the trigger and is owned by each menu's window-capture listener) — **chosen**: zero dependency churn, empty-value-safe, pixel-identical UI, fully deterministic in jsdom. (B) Back all menus with Radix Select/DropdownMenu (the audit's suggested convenience) — rejected with cause: SelectItem forbids the empty-string value settings depends on, portal-based rendering would rework backdrop/z-index/layout across four styled variants, and Radix Select's pointer-capture machinery is fragile under jsdom CI tests; recorded as a deliberate deviation from the hint per protocol §0.
  - *Rows*: (A) real `<Link>` stretched over the row via `after:absolute after:inset-0` with the row as positioning context — **chosen**: native anchor semantics, one navigation path instead of two, works for AT/mouse/keyboard identically. (B) `tabIndex=0` + synthetic `role="link"` + keyDown replication on the `<tr>` — rejected: overriding row roles breaks table semantics for screen readers and re-implements what anchors give for free.
  - *Expanders*: real `<button type="button" aria-expanded aria-controls>` wrapping the existing header grid (shared markup factored so detail/non-detail branches render identically); Tailwind preflight normalizes button typography so layout is unchanged.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: `listbox-keys.ts` — pure `listboxAction(key, index, count)` returning `prev|next|first|last|commit|dismiss|null`; docstring records why commit and Escape live where they do.
