All four menus now share: trigger `aria-haspopup="listbox"` + dynamic `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`; `role="listbox"` container (`aria-labelledby` where an associated label exists); options as `role="option" aria-selected tabIndex={-1}` buttons; a `justOpenedRef`-guarded effect focusing the selected option on genuine opens (click or keyboard — click-open arming added during Step 6 after testing caught its absence); commit/dismiss/walk handling through the shared model; and a window-CAPTURE Escape listener registered only while open (stop-propagation + close + refocus). `sites.tsx`/`site-detail.tsx` rows converted to stretched links (row-relative overlay; delete cell raised above it); `site-detail` tabs rebuilt from a `TAB_ITEMS` table with `role=tablist/tab`, roving tabindex, arrow/Home/End activation-following-focus, and `role=tabpanel` panels wired by id.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: `tests/a11y-navigation.test.tsx` (9): site-row link existence/href/hit-target classes (**FAILED pre-fix**), row-link navigation to `/sites/s1` (**FAILED pre-fix**), delete-button-above-overlay guard (passes both sides by design); tab-strip ARIA + roving tabindex (**FAILED pre-fix**), arrow-key selection incl. wrap-around ×2 (**FAILED pre-fix**), scan-row descriptive link (**FAILED pre-fix**); audit expander disclosure-button contract incl. toggle + panel-id wiring (**FAILED pre-fix**) and non-detail rows staying inert (**FAILED pre-fix**). `tests/a11y-selects.test.tsx` (11): `listboxAction` unit contract; CustomSelect trigger-state/option-roles incl. empty-string option (**FAILED pre-fix** via collection failure of the shared helper import — the file fails to LOAD against the stashed tree); arrow-walking/clamping, click+Enter commits with focus restoration (**FAILED pre-fix**), Escape-to-trigger and Tab-dismissal-without-refocus; UsersCard integration (live trigger inventory, focus-on-selected-open, Enter-commit issuing the PATCH, self-row inert branch); hooks-panel dropdown inside the REAL Radix dialog (open→focus→walk→commit→reopen→Escape closing ONLY the menu while the dialog survives — the containment property); audit filter dropdown committing its filter into the query string. **Failing-before proof performed formally**: `git stash push -u` of the seven production files → `a11y-navigation`: **exactly 8 FAILED / 1 PASSED** (every fix-dependent test failing with structural-absence signatures: "Unable to find role=link", "Unable to find role=tablist", zero `button[aria-expanded]`); `a11y-selects`: fails at COLLECTION (`Failed to resolve import "../src/lib/listbox-keys"`); stash popped, all 20 green re-verified. **Iteration record (Rule 8, each round produced new information)**: (1) first cut relied on native button activation for Enter — jsdom never synthesizes it, so commit became an explicit shared action with `preventDefault()` (single-fire guaranteed in every environment); (2) several intermediate assertions passed VACUOUSLY because `document.activeElement` was `<body>` whose `textContent` contains every string — assertions rewritten to node-identity checks, which immediately exposed a REAL production gap: click-opening did not move focus into the menu (only keyboard-open did); fixed in all four menus and now pinned; (3) Escape inside the Add-hook Radix dialog closed the entire dialog — bubble-phase and root-capture interceptors both lost to Radix's document-capture listener; final design owns Escape at WINDOW capture while the menu is open, verified by the dialog-survives assertion.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **891 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~627 s; warning = the pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning recorded since Phase 0; no backend files changed — count identical to Phase 27's post-fix total; disposable `wardress-test-pg` restarted after host sleep before the run). Frontend `pnpm test` → **12 files / 82 passed** (62 prior net + 20 new); `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → exit 0; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / **12 warnings (= recorded baseline exactly** — my first cut introduced a 13th `exhaustive-deps` warning from RoleSelect's inline options array; fixed at root by hoisting the static `ROLE_OPTIONS` to module level, matching the file's existing `ROLE_DESCRIPTIONS` pattern); `pnpm build` → success (pre-existing chunk-size advisory only). Backend `uv run --frozen ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 145 files formatted. No dependency changes → supply-chain gates stand green from Phase 9.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 27 — coexists cleanly inside sites.tsx/site-detail.tsx (its SiteAvatar/logo JSX untouched; its health-honesty/logo/avatar/tripwire suites re-ran green inside the 82); Phase 26 — hook-panel edit confined to the add-hook dropdown, save-time validation flow untouched; Phases 11/25 — agent surfaces untouched. Verified via git log that no earlier phase modified the six edited production files except Phase 27.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) If focus escapes a menu's container entirely while the menu is open (reachable only via programmatic blur oddities in real browsers), Escape now closes BOTH the menu and any enclosing Radix dialog — the degraded path matches pre-existing platform behavior and the primary contained path is pinned by test. (2) The stretched-link pattern positions content relative to the `<tr>` (`relative` on the row) — well-supported in Chromium/Firefox/Safari for this dashboard use; if a future browser quirk appears, the fallback is moving the overlay to the first cell at the cost of partial-row hit area. (3) Scan-row links announce a composed label rather than the full row content; sighted-keyboard users get the same row visually, and the label contains the visible word per Label-in-Name. (4) Menu typeahead (typing letters to jump options) is not implemented — not part of the filed defect; native `<select>` parity there would be a deliberate enhancement, not a fix. (5) New leads observed: none.
- **Scratch hygiene**: probe logs lived in `%TEMP%\opencode\p28_*` and were deleted after use; repo tree contains only intended changes plus the long-dispositioned items (`scripts/lib.ps1` user-intentional unstaged edit, untracked audit/recon files).
- **Commit**: fix(phase28) — keyboard access + ARIA semantics for core rows, expanders, tabs and dropdowns

### [FIXED] Database backup/restore silently destroys all non-ASCII content when run under Windows PowerShell 5.1 — the exact shell every script header documents

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 29 — Ops/Scripts Robustness
- **Files changed**: `scripts/uninstall.ps1` (dump section 3b rewritten: in-container `sh -c pg_dump` + `docker compose cp` out + ASCII-header sanity check; RESTORE.txt dump step rewritten to cp/psql -f; header comment updated), `backend/tests/test_uninstall_backup_safety.py` (fake-docker shim taught the new protocol: sh-exec stages a fake-container file, `compose cp` copies it), `backend/tests/test_phase29_ops_robustness.py` (new — 6 encoding/gate tests shared with the entries below)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced live on current code with a scratch sandbox outside the repo (deleted afterwards): a shim `pg_dump` emitting raw UTF-8 bytes (Arabic اختراق, Japanese 日本語, em-dash, é) captured by the old `> $dumpFile` redirect produced **188-byte byte-exact output under pwsh 7 but 388 bytes starting `FF FE` (UTF-16LE+BOM) under Windows PowerShell 5.1** — the audit's signature. One environment nuance measured beyond the filed text: on THIS session's ambient console codepage (CP 65001 inherited from the tool shell) the 5.1 capture preserved TEXT while changing its encoding, whereas the audit's own Phase 9 console state (OEM CP437) destroyed it outright — i.e. content survival is ambient-console-dependent, but the byte stream is never preserved and the file is always UTF-16LE+BOM (fatal to psql). The restore leg was then proven independently broken: piping the dump through `Get-Content | docker compose exec -T db psql` under 5.1 (`$OutputEncoding` = ASCII) delivered literal `'arabic: ??'` to the receiver (captured via the receiver's own error echo) while pwsh 7 delivered intact UTF-8 — both legs defective exactly as filed.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: both legs routed a binary byte stream through PowerShell's text layer. Capture: `docker … > $file` makes PowerShell decode the child's stdout using the ambient console output encoding and re-encode through Out-File's default (UTF-16LE+BOM under 5.1) — any non-UTF-8 console codepage destroys non-ASCII characters irreversibly before a single byte reaches disk. Restore: `Get-Content | psql` re-encodes each line with `$OutputEncoding` (ASCII by default under 5.1), flattening every surviving high-byte character to `?`. The missing primitive is a path where no host shell ever touches the bytes — which is precisely what container-side execution plus `docker compose cp` provides.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Shell parity*: the new pipeline must be byte-identical under BOTH documented shells — automated for pwsh 7 and Windows PowerShell 5.1 (the latter executed for real on this machine during this phase; the test carries an explicit platform skipif with stated reason for CI runners without 5.1).
  - *pg_dump failure inside the container* → `sh` exits nonzero → historical reason string `"database.sql (pg_dump failed)"` preserved verbatim (Phase 13 contract pin) and the completeness gate blocks teardown (tested).
  - *Copy-out failure after successful dump* → NEW distinct reason `"database.sql (database dump could not be copied out)"`, gate blocks (tested).
  - *Exit-0-but-empty/malformed dump* → superseded the old size>0 check with a format sanity check (plain-format dumps begin with an ASCII `--` header): reason `"database.sql (database dump verification failed)"`, partial file removed, gate blocks (tested). Old code caught empties via Length>0 with the pg_dump-failed reason; the new check also catches binary-garbage copies.
  - *Partial-failure leftovers* → a failed attempt deletes any partial destination file so Phase 13's reused-BackupPath stale-clearing invariant stays airtight (its existing tests still green).
  - *Quoting/injection* → pgUser/pgDb/dump-path pass as positional `sh` parameters quoted as `"$1"/"$2"/"$3"` inside the container script — values come from .env regex parsing, never interpolated into the command string.
  - *Concurrent access / auth* → N/A — local interactive ops script; no guard surface.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 13's completeness machinery consumed unchanged ($missing reasons feed the same gate/summary/exit codes); its 9 sandbox tests re-ran green unmodified in ASSERTION terms after their shim was adapted to the new call shape (adaptation note below).
  - *Backward compatibility*: backups made by OLD script versions keep their own generated RESTORE.txt (already inaccurate about their own corruption — unrecoverable regardless); new backups are self-consistently byte-safe. No schema/config change.
  - *Performance*: one extra exec (cp) + one best-effort rm per uninstall — nil against pg_dump itself.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: fail-closed — every new failure mode lands in $missing and stops teardown; worst case operator friction, never silent loss.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) dump INSIDE the container (`exec sh -c 'pg_dump … > /tmp/file'`) + `docker compose cp` out + sanity-check + container-side cleanup — **chosen** (the audit's preferred direction verbatim; immune to every host shell, codepage, and PS version). (B) `cmd /c "docker … > file"` host-side byte redirect — rejected: keeps the bytes on the host; works today yet leaves the class one refactor away from PowerShell's text layer again. (C) `Start-Process -RedirectStandardOutput` streaming — rejected: `-Encoding Byte` (5.1) vs `-AsByteStream` (7) divergence makes the variant version-fragile. (D) fixing only the RESTORE.txt instructions — rejected: instructions cannot repair an already-corrupt file.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: see Files changed; RESTORE.txt now instructs `docker compose cp "<backup>\database.sql" db:/tmp/restore.sql` → `psql -U <user> -d <db> -f /tmp/restore.sql` → `rm`, explicitly warning never to pipe the file through a host shell.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in `tests/test_phase29_ops_robustness.py`: `test_dump_is_byte_exact_under_windows_powershell_51` (**THE failing-before proof**: pre-fix produced 388-byte UTF-16LE+BOM; post-fix byte-identical 188 bytes — executed for real under powershell.exe 5.1 on this machine), `test_restore_recipe_uses_container_copy_not_host_piping` (**FAILED pre-fix**), `test_copy_failure_blocks_teardown_with_named_reason` (**FAILED pre-fix**), `test_empty_dump_rejected_by_verification` (**FAILED pre-fix**), `test_dump_is_byte_exact_under_pwsh` + `test_pg_dump_failure_keeps_historical_reason_and_gate` (guards passing both sides by design). Formal stash-run recorded below.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: covered jointly by the phase entry below.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 13 — same script, same gate machinery; all nine of its assertions hold unchanged (one shim adaptation inside its test harness, recorded below); `test_full_backup_completes_and_teardown_proceeds`, `test_reused_backup_path_cannot_inherit_stale_artifacts`, `test_missing_env_is_a_gap_not_a_failure` etc. green. Finding "Uninstall documentation promises a backup-then-delete safety contract" (Phase 33) becomes MORE true with this fix — docs wording still reserved for the scheduled sweep per §4.0.5.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) `docker compose cp` requires Compose v2.6+ (2022; bundled with every Docker Desktop since) — accepted, consistent with the stack's other compose-centric recipes. (2) RESTORE.txt itself is written with Set-Content UTF8 (BOM under 5.1) — readability-only surface, not data; untouched. (3) New leads observed: none.

### [FIXED] Every entry-point script hangs forever when the Docker CLI wedges — preflight `docker info` probes have no timeout

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 29 — Ops/Scripts Robustness
- **Files changed**: `scripts/lib.ps1` (`Invoke-NativeWithDeadline` helper added), `scripts/install.ps1` + `scripts/update.ps1` + `scripts/uninstall.ps1` + `scripts/validate.ps1` (preflight engine/compose probes rewired; validate's step-8 resource check bounded behind a liveness probe), `scripts/diagnostics.ps1` (dot-sources lib.ps1; DOCKER STATUS section bounded with a printed probe label), `backend/tests/test_phase29_ops_robustness.py` (shared)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced on current (pre-phase-edit) code with a scratch watchdog harness outside the repo: a fake `docker.cmd` sleeping on `info` left `uninstall.ps1` running past a 20 s watchdog with zero completion and zero error — matching the audit's own live reproduction (validate hung >240 s, externally killed) — and demonstrated the finding's second-order harm live: killing the outer process orphaned the pipe-holding child tree, so even external termination leaves stragglers. Not stale; no prior phase had touched these scripts except Phase 13 (uninstall backup section only).
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: every entry point's first action is a synchronous native invocation with no deadline (`Invoke-Quiet { docker info }`); diagnostics' try/catch catches errors but structurally cannot catch a hang. A wedged CLI (stuck daemon pipe, hung WSL2 backend, credential-helper stall) therefore blocks forever with no output. Secondary root discovered DURING implementation (iteration record below): a naive kill path silently does nothing under PowerShell 7 because any assignment to `$isWindows` collides with the read-only automatic `$IsWindows` (variable names are case-insensitive) and throws — a swallowed exception that would have made the fix itself unreliable. The missing primitives: a hard wall-clock deadline around first-contact probes, and a kill path whose failure is observable.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Wedged engine probe* → bounded (~30 s default) failure with a distinct actionable message ("did not respond within 30 seconds … Restart Docker Desktop"), exit nonzero, ZERO destructive calls afterwards (tested end-to-end for validate, install, uninstall, diagnostics).
  - *Fast-failing engine (stopped daemon)* → historical messages preserved byte-for-byte and kept DISTINCT from the wedged message (tested guard passing both sides).
  - *Multi-probe scripts* → validate continues validating after a wedged probe (records a validation ERROR rather than dying mid-checklist — its contract is a report, not an installer); later probes are NOT skipped (each gets its own deadline).
  - *Process-tree cleanup* → taskkill /T /F on Windows (docker.exe can spawn plugin children), plain Kill() elsewhere; kill FAILURE is now reported on screen instead of swallowed (this phase found and fixed the silent-swallow variant of exactly that failure — iteration record below); post-kill WaitForExit(3 s) bounds the helper.
  - *Pipe-buffer deadlock* → impossible by construction: the helper does not redirect stdio (output flows to the console), so nothing waits on pipes while it waits on the process.
  - *Batch-file resolution* → Get-Command-resolved paths ending `.cmd/.bat` launch through `cmd.exe /d /s /c` (CreateProcess cannot exec batch files directly); everything else launches directly. This also keeps the test sandbox's PATH-shimmed fake docker working.
  - *PS 5.1 compatibility* → no ArgumentList (manual quoting into .Arguments), no ternary/null-coalescing, no $IsWindows usage; parser-clean under 5.1 syntax rules.
  - *Timeout value* → 30 s at every site: real engines answer `docker info` in <2 s warm and ~10–15 s cold-starting; the audit's wedge was indefinite. Constant documented in-code.
  - *validate step 8* (`docker info --format json` resource capture — same-class instance folded in, precedent Phases 27/28): bounded behind a fresh liveness probe; if unreachable, a validation WARNING records the skip instead of hanging.
  - *diagnostics* → prints "Probing Docker engine (30 second deadline)…" BEFORE the probe (the audit's visibility ask; the other four scripts already print Step labels immediately before theirs) and records the wedged condition INTO the support bundle (tested).
  - *Concurrent access / unicode input* → N/A — single-threaded ops scripts; argv are fixed literals.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: none mechanical — the helper is additive; Invoke-Quiet remains for all non-probe callers (volume inspect/rm, image inspect, rmi, compose up/exec probes) whose hang risk only exists AFTER an engine has already answered.
  - *Backward compatibility*: success/failure semantics unchanged for healthy machines; only the previously-infinite case gains a terminating outcome.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: if the deadline mechanism itself misfires, worst case equals pre-fix behavior on false timeout (a readable failure where the engine was merely slow) — bounded by the generous 30 s budget.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) generic `Invoke-NativeWithDeadline` built on System.Diagnostics.Process with WaitForExit(ms) + tree-kill — **chosen**: works identically under 5.1/7 and Windows/Linux, none of Start-Job's per-probe second-PowerShell spawn cost or cleanup residue, returns a tri-state ($true/$false/$null) so callers keep their distinct messages. (B) Start-Job + Wait-Job -Timeout — rejected for the costs above. (C) bounding EVERY docker call in all scripts — rejected: far beyond the finding (first-contact probes are where a wedge strikes; later calls run only against an already-answering engine), documented as residual.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: see Files changed. All five entry points translated; message wording distinguishes "engine is not running" (fast fail) from "did not respond within 30 seconds — the Docker Desktop backend appears wedged" (deadline).
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in `tests/test_phase29_ops_robustness.py`: `test_deadline_helper_returns_false_true_and_null_on_timeout` (unit contract incl. elapsed bound; **failed pre-fix** via missing helper), `test_wedged_docker_preflight_fails_fast_with_readable_message[validate|install]` (**hang pre-fix** — excluded from the formal stash-run because the pre-fix tree deadlocks the test harness itself; their failing-before evidence is the pre-edit session probes: uninstall hung past a 20 s watchdog with a surviving orphaned tree, plus the audit's own >240 s validate hang), `test_wedged_uninstall_preflight_never_reaches_teardown` (same evidence basis; asserts zero teardown calls post-fix), `test_engine_down_still_reports_the_distinct_message` (guard, passes both sides), `test_diagnostics_bounded_under_wedge` (**hang pre-fix**, same evidence basis; bundle contains the wedged line post-fix).
- **Iteration record (Rule 8 — each round produced new information)**: (1) first cut killed via an `$isWindows` flag → the ENTIRE first sandbox-test run hung for 15 minutes: root-caused with file-marker instrumentation to the read-only-automatic-variable collision (`Cannot overwrite variable IsWindows`) being eaten by a blanket catch, silently skipping every kill and leaving pipe-held orphans that deadlock any pipe-capturing parent; (2) fixed the collision AND made kill failures observable (yellow console line naming pid + reason) instead of swallowed — exactly the silent-swallow class this effort exists to eliminate, caught by this phase's own tests before commit; (3) verified zero survivors after deadline via a dedicated survivor-count probe before re-running the suite green.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: covered jointly by the phase entry below.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 13's suite green unchanged (its scenarios never hit the deadline). No earlier phase modified lib.ps1 logic (verified via git log; the user-intentional unstaged Build-Service handle-cache edit predates the effort and is preserved untouched in the working tree, excluded from this commit via surgical staging).
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Later-script docker calls remain unbounded by scope decision above — a wedge striking MID-uninstall (after a healthy preflight) can still stall; revisit only if field reports justify it. (2) A killed tree can theoretically leave an orphan if taskkill fails — now VISIBLE on screen instead of silent, with the message advising a Docker Desktop restart anyway. (3) New leads observed: none.
- **Commit**: fix(phase29)

### [FIXED] ADMIN_RESET_PASSWORD emergency-recovery knob is unreachable through every documented configuration surface — the tool's own printed instructions silently no-op

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 29 — Ops/Scripts Robustness
- **Files changed**: `docker-compose.yml` (app service forwards `ADMIN_RESET_PASSWORD`), `.env.example` (documented `ADMIN_RESET_PASSWORD=false` block), `backend/app/seed_admin.py` (already-exists hint replaced with the working three-step procedure), `docs/user-management.mdx` (new "Emergency Admin Password Reset" section), `backend/tests/test_phase29_ops_robustness.py` (shared)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: confirmed on current code: `docker compose config` rendered with `ADMIN_RESET_PASSWORD=true` exported shows only `ADMIN_EMAIL`/`ADMIN_PASSWORD` rows in the app service env — the flag is dropped by compose's fixed allow-list exactly as filed (names observed only; secret values not recorded); `seed_admin.py:76` still printed the unusable hint; install.ps1 execs seed_admin without `-e`; repo-wide grep: zero occurrences outside seed_admin.py; docs/README/scripts silent. Not stale; no prior phase touched any of these surfaces.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: three stacked reachability gaps around one honest-but-wrong hint. (1) Compose forwards only enumerated variables, so setting the flag in .env (the file users are told to edit) never reached the container. (2) install/update exec seed_admin without injecting flags, so re-running the installer doesn't help either. (3) The tool's printed instruction referenced the var with no procedure — following it verbatim ends in "User … already exists" and a locked-out admin. Additionally, even WITH forwarding, a bare "set it in .env" flow has a correctness trap the fix must address: compose environment changes need a container RECREATE to take effect, and the reset reads ADMIN_PASSWORD from the same (possibly stale) container env. The missing primitive: a forwarded variable PLUS a printed procedure whose every step is actually executable in order.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Stale-password trap*: resetting via transient `-e ADMIN_RESET_PASSWORD=true` inherits the CONTAINER's ADMIN_PASSWORD — if the operator changed .env since creation, they would reset to the OLD password. The printed procedure therefore puts the .env update FIRST and requires `docker compose up -d app` (recreate) BEFORE the reset exec, making the exec inherit fresh values; the transient `-e` form needs no cleanup and never puts a secret on the command line.
  - *Flag hygiene*: leaving `ADMIN_RESET_PASSWORD=true` behind makes every future seeding run overwrite the admin password — docs carry an explicit Warning to remove it; compose default `${ADMIN_RESET_PASSWORD:-false}` keeps absent-var deployments inert; .env.example ships `false` so generated installs document the knob without enabling it.
  - *Case/value parsing*: seed_admin accepts `true` case-insensitively (existing behavior, unchanged); anything else is off.
  - *Who else reads it*: exhaustive grep — seed_admin.py only; worker/beat/telegram containers do NOT receive the variable (app-service-only forward, least exposure).
  - *Auth/RBAC interaction*: the reset path reactivates a deactivated account and revokes refresh tokens (pre-existing emergency semantics, now functionally pinned by test); no API surface changes.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: none mechanical — Phase 17's login lockout counts failures, unaffected; seeding remains manual-exec only (installer step unchanged).
  - *Backward compatibility*: installs with existing .env files simply lack the key → compose default false → behavior identical to today until an operator opts in; no migration.
  - *Docs placement*: user-management.mdx gained a NEW accurate section (implementing the finding's discoverability requirement, not correcting a stale claim — Phase 33/41 sweeps untouched; grep confirms no scheduled-sweep overlap).
  - *Fix-failure mode*: tripwired by test — if the compose forward or .env.example entry regresses, repo-file tests fail loudly; if the hint regresses to the bare parenthetical, the captured-stdout test fails on both the missing procedure and the exact old string.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) forward the variable AND replace the hint with the ordered recreate→exec procedure AND document it in user-management.mdx — **chosen** ("ideally both" per the audit, plus the docs leg since the finding's title is reachability-through-documented-surfaces; content is new-and-true, not a stale-doc edit). (B) print only the one-shot `exec -e` incantation without compose forwarding — rejected: leaves the .env surface (which users are told to edit) a silent no-op, the finding's core defect. (C) auto-run seed_admin at container start when flagged — rejected: turns an explicit recovery tool into startup behavior with permanent-overwrite risk (feature-class change, Rule 2). (D) admin-panel forgot-password flow — out of scope by definition (no admin able to log in).
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: compose app service gains `ADMIN_RESET_PASSWORD: ${ADMIN_RESET_PASSWORD:-false}` with an emergency-use comment; .env.example documents it adjacent to the admin credentials block; seed_admin's already-exists branch now prints: set ADMIN_PASSWORD in .env → `docker compose up -d app` → `docker compose exec -T -e ADMIN_RESET_PASSWORD=true app python -m app.seed_admin`; user-management.mdx documents the full flow including the remove-the-flag warning and what the reset does (password back to current ADMIN_PASSWORD, reactivation, session revocation).
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: in `tests/test_phase29_ops_robustness.py`: `test_compose_forwards_admin_reset_password_to_app` (**FAILED pre-fix** — key absent), `test_env_example_documents_the_reset_knob` (**FAILED pre-fix**), `test_seed_hint_prints_a_working_recovery_command` (**FAILED pre-fix** — captured stdout contained the old bare parenthetical verbatim; post-fix asserts the ordered procedure and the absence of the old string), `test_seed_reset_flow_end_to_end` (functional coverage of the documented emergency semantics — create → deactivate + open session → flag flips password/reactivates/revokes sessions → WITHOUT the flag the env password is never silently applied; guard passing both sides, pinning behavior the docs now promise for the first time).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: covered jointly by the phase entry below.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: none mechanical. The audit's Phase 12 lead ("docs are silent on ADMIN_RESET_PASSWORD") resolves here by adding the documentation; the six unrelated non-forwarded Settings fields remain Phase 39's cluster and were deliberately untouched.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) An operator who sets the flag in .env and forgets leaves overwrite-on-seeding enabled — mitigated by the docs Warning and the shipped `false` default in fresh installs; the transient `-e` route avoids the state entirely. (2) New leads observed: none.
- **Commit**: fix(phase29)

### Formal failing-before proof (phase-level record)

- **Method**: `git stash push -- scripts/uninstall.ps1 scripts/lib.ps1 docker-compose.yml .env.example backend/app/seed_admin.py docs/user-management.mdx` → run `tests/test_phase29_ops_robustness.py` against the stashed (pre-fix) tree → pop. The six wedge-class tests were EXCLUDED from the stashed run because the pre-fix tree hangs indefinitely under them (deadlocking the harness) — their failing-before evidence is the pre-edit session probes recorded in the entries above (uninstall hung past a 20 s watchdog with a surviving orphaned process tree; the audit's >240 s validate hang) plus the post-fix greens.
- **Result**: exactly **8 FAILED / 2 PASSED** on the stashed tree — `bytes_51`, `restore_recipe`, `copyfail`, `emptydump`, `compose_forwards`, `env_example`, `seed_hint` failing with the filed signatures (incl. the old hint string captured verbatim in stdout), plus `bytes_pwsh` failing on the stashed tree because the committed shim speaks the NEW wire protocol only (the pure encoding defect's failing-before evidence remains the direct pre-edit probe: 188 B byte-exact under pwsh vs 388 B UTF-16LE+BOM under 5.1); guards `dumpfail29` and `seed_reset_flow_end_to_end` passed BOTH sides by design. Stash popped cleanly; full file green (16/16) re-verified.

### Session notes (transparency)

- **Mid-session working-tree change from outside the effort**: `.gitignore` gained a `project_structure.txt` line during this session (not made by this session; matches the dispositioned recon-artifact pattern). Left untouched, NOT part of this commit — same disposition class as the Phase 0 baseline items.

Disclosed for completeness; all subsequent probes ran shim-only (PATH-prepended).
- **Scratch hygiene**: all probes lived under `%TEMP%\opencode\p29*` and were deleted after use. Repo tree contains intended changes plus the long-dispositioned items (`scripts/lib.ps1` user-intentional unstaged edit — preserved via surgical staging, NOT in this commit; untracked audit/recon files; the user's `.gitignore` line).


## Phase 30 Notes (partial scope record — remainder completed 2026-08-24, see entry below)

Phase 30's cluster is two findings: "Tautological and vacuous assertions sit on
safety-critical surfaces" and "Non-hermetic unit test makes live external DNS
queries". Both are fixed this session (entries below). The tracker stayed
"In progress (partial)" for one reason only: the audit-era lead recorded in this
log during Phase 12 (the vacuous-pass failure mode observed live then —
assert-on-stub-called guards, noted as a candidate for the Phase 30 mechanical
sweep) has NOT been swept. That lead was a candidate assignment, not filed
finding scope; all three assertion sites of the actual finding are repaired.

**Explicit remainder (for the next session finishing Phase 30)**: sweep the
backend suite for assert-on-stub-called guards of the Phase-12-lead class
(tests whose subject can go dead while the test stays green because an
unrelated stub/mock assertion carries the pass). Method: in each
monkeypatch-based test module, find assertions that reference a stub/mock
rather than an effect on production state; verify each would fail if the
production call it names were removed; rewrite those that would not.

**RESOLUTION (2026-08-24)**: the sweep was executed and is recorded as the fix
entry "[Sweep] Assert-on-stub-called guard sweep (Phase 30 recorded remainder)"
below. Outcome: one vacuous upper-bound counter rewritten; all other sites
verified falsifiable by analysis plus mutation spot-proofs. Phase 30 is now
Complete.

### [FIXED] Tautological and vacuous assertions sit on safety-critical surfaces: the fusion "determinism" test asserts only Python cache identity, and two XSS/garbage-input guards assert nothing

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 30 — Test-Quality Mechanical Fixes
- **Files changed**: backend/tests/test_detection_fusion_pipeline.py (test_fusion_model_is_deterministic rewritten as test_fusion_model_matches_committed_artifact; json + MODEL_ARTIFACT_PATH imports added), backend/tests/test_detection_layers.py (test_parse_html_handles_garbage tautology replaced with concrete expectations), frontend/tests/ui-enhancements.test.tsx (img-onerror loop replaced with concrete assertions)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: all three cited sites confirmed verbatim on current code before editing: the fusion test body was exactly two get_fusion_model() calls plus `assert m1 is m2`; test_detection_layers.py carried the literal `parse_html("\x00\x01\x02") is not None or parse_html("\x00\x01\x02") is None` disjunction; ui-enhancements.test.tsx iterated querySelectorAll("img") results with no empty-set guard. Executed this session: the disjunction evaluates True identically when parse_html returns None (its real behavior, measured through the production function) AND under a simulated never-None regression; the old frontend loop passes against the real renderer, against a live-img-onerror broken renderer, AND against a payload-swallowing empty render. All three defects fully intact pre-fix. No prior phase had touched these three test files except Phase 27's orthogonal avatar/logo work in different files.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: three shapes of one disease — assertions whose truth value does not depend on the property they name. (1) Fusion: `m1 is m2` tests Python memoization; it holds for ANY cached value including a corrupted artifact load or a per-call-random fit, so the determinism-of-fit property was unchecked (it stayed green beside Finding 5.1's sign-inverted coefficients). (2) parse_html: `X is not None or X is None` is a logical identity over any boolean — unfalsifiable by construction. (3) Frontend: a for..of over querySelectorAll("img") executes zero iterations whenever no imgs render, so the sanitization claim passed vacuously exactly when it mattered most. The missing primitive is falsifiability: every rewritten assertion must be able to fail under some reachable defective state of the system it names.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Fusion rewrite scope*: value-equality against the committed artifact pins coefficients/intercept/lambda_selected/dataset-sha256 (a corrupted file, wrong-schema load, or regression to an unconstrained refit now fails); a non-negativity check re-states finding 5.1 structurally inside the suite; the cache-identity line is KEPT but demoted to a documented consequence of loading identical bytes, not the headline.
  - *Artifact-drift coupling*: equality-vs-committed-file means a legitimate future refit updates the artifact (which IS the deployment contract since Phases 8-10 — the runtime loads exactly that file), so the pin cannot false-alarm on intended changes while still catching unintended runtime/artifact divergence loudly.
  - *parse_html boundary honesty*: every expected value measured through the REAL function before being asserted (never assumed): control-chars-alone -> None (libxml2 recovers no elements from bare NUL bytes); embedded-NUL markup -> tree recovered with surviving text; stray `<` -> None without raising; empty/whitespace -> None (pre-existing assertions kept).
  - *Frontend mechanism correctness*: the first cut asserted innerHTML contains no "onerror" — its own run FAILED against the REAL component because react-markdown renders the payload as entity-escaped TEXT (&lt;img onerror=...) where the substring legitimately appears as escaped content, not as an attribute. Root-caused to confusing the sanitization mechanism (escaping, not stripping) and fixed by asserting the escape marker itself — iteration recorded per Rule 8.
  - *Vacuity self-guard*: each rewritten test asserts positive evidence that hostile input was actually exercised (payload text visible / recovered tree contains surviving text / artifact actually read), so an empty render or short-circuit can never green them — the exact class they replace.
  - *Concurrent access / unicode / partial failure / retry / RBAC*: N/A — test-only change; no production surface moved anywhere in this finding.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 9's committed artifact consumed read-only (its own byte-identity tests unaffected); Phases 6/20-23 detection suites untouched (parse_html behavior unchanged — only its test changed); zero production files edited.
  - *Backward compatibility*: pure test-quality change; CI gate commands unchanged; no dependency additions.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: worst case a rewrite is stricter than production guarantees — bounded by measuring every expectation from real execution before committing it (NUL-recovery and escaped-text both came from probes).
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**:
  - *Fusion*: (A) value-equality vs committed artifact + non-negativity — chosen: the artifact IS the deployed model since Phase 10, so file-vs-runtime equality is the truest form of "the shipped fit", catching corruption/refit-regression classes the audit demanded. (B) hard-pinning exact coefficient literals in the test — rejected: duplicates the artifact as a second hand-synced source of truth; file-equality gives equal falsifiability without drift hazard. (C) rename only — rejected: leaves the vacuity in place.
  - *parse_html*: (A) assert the measured outcome per input class — chosen (the audit's own direction: pick one valid outcome and assert it). (B) try/except "does not raise" — rejected: another unfalsifiable shape.
  - *img-onerror*: (A) concrete rendered-output assertions incl. escape-marker and payload-visibility guards — chosen (audit direction verbatim). (B) swapping renderers/libraries — out of scope: production rendering is correct; only its test was vacuous.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: fusion test renamed honestly and re-pointed at the artifact contract (coefficients tuple equality, intercept equality, all-nonnegative, lambda and dataset-sha256 equality, identity kept as documented cache consequence); parse_html test states four concrete outcomes across the empty/whitespace/control-char/recovery boundaries plus a stray-`<` case; frontend test replaces the possibly-empty loop with three positive assertions (zero img elements, opener entity-escaped, payload text visible).
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: these ARE the test changes; net +14 backend tests (16 new in the companion DNS module and fusion/layers rewrites counted at file level: fusion file 36->37, layers file 27->27 same count rewritten, ai_migration unchanged count). Failing-before/vacuity proofs performed formally with scratch runners outside the repo (deleted after use):
  - *Fusion*: proof script injected the audit Finding-5.1 seed fit behind get_fusion_model and executed BOTH bodies — OLD: PASS (blind to negative l2 −6.5359 / l6 −1.3853); NEW: FAIL on five properties (coefficients != artifact, intercept != artifact, negatives present, lambda mismatch, dataset sha256 mismatch). Healthy-state control: both PASS.
  - *parse_html*: OLD vs NEW bodies under current behavior AND under a simulated never-None parser regression — OLD: PASS under BOTH states; formal identity `(v is not None) or (v is None)` == True recorded; NEW: PASS on reality, FAIL under the regression.
  - *img-onerror*: scratch vitest falsifiability matrix over three renderings — real MarkdownMessage+payload (OLD=PASS NEW=PASS), live `<img onerror>` broken renderer (OLD=FAIL NEW=FAIL-live-img), swallowed-payload empty render (THE audit vacuity class: OLD=PASS, NEW=FAIL-not-escaped) — proving the old form could not fail exactly where its claim went unchecked and the new form fails in every defective state. The scratch spec sat briefly under frontend/tests purely for vitest's include-pattern discovery; deleted immediately after each run, never staged (post-run git status verified).
  - *Stash-method note*: a conventional pre-fix-tree stash run cannot demonstrate failing-before for TAUTOLOGY/VACUITY fixes — the old bodies pass on ANY tree, which IS their defect; the defective-state injections above are the stronger and correct demonstration for this class. The companion DNS finding below uses the conventional stash form.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → 907 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning (~774 s; warning = the pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning recorded since Phase 0; 891 prior + 16 net-new). Frontend: pnpm test → 12 files / 82 passed; pnpm type-check → exit 0; oxlint → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= baseline exactly). Backend ruff check → All checks passed!; format --check initially flagged my edited file → formatted → 146 files formatted clean; touched module re-run green post-format (37 passed). No dependency changes → supply-chain gates stand green from Phase 9.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phases 8-10 — artifact consumed read-only; equality target is the same file their loaders validate; their suites green inside the 907. Phases 20-23 — detection behavior untouched; amended fixtures green. Phase 12 — real-vector suite unaffected. Zero production code changed for this finding.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) The fusion pin keys on the committed artifact file — intentional single-source-of-truth coupling; regeneration updates it automatically (no false alarm), divergence fails loudly. (2) The broader Phase-12-lead sweep class remains explicitly open — see the phase-notes block above for the recorded remainder and method.
- **Commit**: fix(phase30)

### [FIXED] Non-hermetic unit test makes live external DNS queries: the suite's red/green state depends on the host resolver's answer at run time

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 30 — Test-Quality Mechanical Fixes
- **Files changed**: backend/tests/test_ai_migration.py (test_ollama_cloud_key_flows_into_deployment gains a monkeypatch stub of app.ssrf.resolve_host + explanatory docstring; ipaddress import added)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: reproduced on current code by execution. The un-stubbed test drives create_provider(base_url="https://ollama.com") → validate_base_url → assert_url_allowed → blocking socket.getaddrinfo("ollama.com") — observed live this session via a counting wrapper around socket.getaddrinfo (one ollama.com query recorded during the plain run). Environment-dependence re-derived rather than assumed: Phase 16's superset-safe policy means the historical red-on-DNS64 OUTCOME no longer reproduces (a simulated NAT64 resolver now takes the allowed path — verified end-to-end through validate_base_url with ssrf.resolve_host returning 64:ff9b::2224:850f), but the test still consults whatever resolver the host offers at run time: resolution failure today surfaces as a loud error, and future policy/network shifts reintroduce environment-flakiness. Formal git stash push of the file confirmed the OLD version passes on the pre-fix tree WHILE issuing the live query — the defect is non-hermeticity, not a red outcome; the policy half of the historical incident closed with Phase 16, the hermeticity half closes here.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: the test exercises provider creation end-to-end, which legitimately runs the SSRF gate — but nothing stubbed the gate's DNS resolution, so a unit test reached the live network. The missing primitive is the established Phase-12 seam pattern (hermetic stub replacing network I/O while production logic keeps running): applied to ssrf.resolve_host, the single resolution chokepoint assert_url_allowed uses.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Patch point*: resolve_host patched as a module attribute (assert_url_allowed looks it up on app.ssrf at call time — verified by read + probe), so every caller of the shared helper is covered by one stub; socket.getaddrinfo itself deliberately NOT patched globally (that would mask genuine resolution-bug classes elsewhere).
  - *Stub fidelity*: returns [IPv4Address("34.36.133.15")] — ollama.com's real current A-record shape — a plain global address passing every policy branch unchanged, so the SSRF gate executes its full logic and only I/O is faked.
  - *Scope isolation*: monkeypatch-scoped to this one test; no autouse fixture; grep confirmed this was the sole live-DNS site in the suite (matching the audit), so nothing else needs the treatment.
  - *Determinism proof*: a scratch pytest plugin (outside the repo) patched resolve_host to the historical NAT64 AAAA AND wrapped socket.getaddrinfo with a counter, then ran the COMMITTED test — passed with ZERO live getaddrinfo calls: the exact hostile environment that once failed this test red is now harmless AND un-consulted.
  - *Auth/RBAC/concurrency/backward compat/performance*: N/A — test-only change.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 16 owns the policy half and pins NAT64 handling deterministically by literal-address tests (test_phase16_outbound_fetch.py); this phase closes the hermeticity half without duplicating that coverage; its 34-test suite re-ran green inside the regression. Phase 1 harness conventions followed (hermetic, loud-fail).
  - *Fix-failure mode*: worst case the stub masks a hypothetical resolve_host signature change — bounded because assert_url_allowed resolves the patched name directly; a rename breaks loudly (AttributeError), never silently.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) stub ssrf.resolve_host in this one test — chosen: minimal, scoped, matches the audit's suggested direction verbatim, keeps the full create_provider→SSRF-gate chain executing real logic. (B) patch socket.getaddrinfo globally in conftest — rejected: hides genuine network misuse suite-wide and over-mocks the one place production intentionally resolves. (C) switch base_url to a literal IP — rejected: weakens coverage (real deployments use hostnames; the hostname path through urlparse/literal-detection is worth exercising). (D) delete the test — rejected: it uniquely pins key→litellm-deployment flow.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: the test accepts monkeypatch, stubs app.ssrf.resolve_host before opening its db session, and documents why — including pointing at test_phase16_outbound_fetch.py as the deterministic home of the policy assertions.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: the rewrite itself; proofs as recorded in Step 1 (live-query observation on the old form incl. the stash-run green-on-pre-fix-tree; zero-query hostile-resolver pass on the new form).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: joint with the entry above — backend 907 passed / 0 failed / 1 warning (~774 s), including this test passing under both the normal and the plugin-simulated NAT64 environment with zero live queries. All other gates as recorded there.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 16 — complementary halves of the audit's Finding-0.1 pair (policy + hermeticity); no coverage overlap. No other phase touched test_ai_migration.py.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) If future tests add live-DNS call sites, this session's counting-wrapper technique (wrap socket.getaddrinfo, assert zero calls) is the ready-made guard — recorded rather than added suite-wide now (a second instance would justify an autouse fixture). (2) New leads observed: none.
- **Commit**: fix(phase30)

### [Sweep] Assert-on-stub-called guard sweep (Phase 30 recorded remainder)

*(Header deviates from the §3 template deliberately: this is not a filed audit finding — it is the explicitly recorded Phase 30 remainder, the Phase-12-lead class ("a test can keep passing after the behavior it names stops executing"), swept to completion so Phase 30 could be flipped to Complete.)*

- **Original severity**: N/A (lead-class sweep; candidate assignment recorded during Phase 12's failing-before proof and in this log's New Leads section)
- **Phase**: 30 — Test-Quality Mechanical Fixes (recorded remainder)
- **Files changed**: `backend/tests/test_phase20_semantics_drift.py` (`test_encode_budget_is_bounded_on_huge_pages` gains a lower-bound assertion + docstring note)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: the lead's live incident shape was re-derived from the Phase 12 entry: removing a monkeypatch left `test_escalation_benign_does_not_upgrade` GREEN while its subject (the escalation path) had gone completely dead — an assert-not-called-shaped expectation trivially satisfied by a dead path. That specific test no longer exists in that shape (Phase 12 reworked both escalation tests onto a deterministic mid-band scenario through the real gate), so the sweep asked whether ANY other test carries its pass on a stub-referencing assertion without a production-state anchor.
- **Method (as recorded in the phase-notes remainder)**: complete inventory first — every monkeypatch-based backend test module enumerated via grep; every assertion referencing a stub/mock/counter/fake/spy collected (full consumer list of conftest's `stub_all_enqueues`, fixture-local fakes such as `fetch_calls`/`sent_tasks`/`pull_canary`/`captured`/`Counting`, spy lists in test_phase16, scripted-model event assertions in test_agent_prompt_injection). Each site then classified: (a) stub-call-IS-the-subject tests, where the stub-referencing assertion names the tested behavior itself; (b) corroborating negative guards paired with positive production-state assertions; (c) unanchored cases where death of the named production call would leave the test green.
- **Findings**:
  - *Class (a) sites* — `test_phase16_outbound_fetch.py` validate/limiter spies, `test_phase26_remediation_hooks.py` resweep `sent == [stuck_id]`, `test_phase5_remediation.py::test_confirm_queues_and_enqueues`: falsifiable by construction. Mutation-proof executed live: neutralizing `enqueue_remediation(execution.id)` in `routers/remediation.py`'s confirm path made BOTH `test_confirm_queues_and_enqueues` (assert at :202) AND `TestConfirmRace::test_concurrent_confirms_single_winner_single_enqueue_single_audit` (assert at :180) FAIL — production file restored byte-identical afterwards.
  - *Class (b) sites* — every `fetch_calls == []` / `sent_tasks == []` / `stub_enqueue[...]` count / `stub_all_enqueues["scan"] == []` / `must_not_run` guard across test_scan_tasks, test_scheduler, test_sites, test_agent, test_agent_prompt_injection, test_end_to_end_flagging, test_phase18, test_remediation_claim_race, test_main: each individually verified to carry a positive production anchor in the same test body (return-value contracts like `"baseline-already-ready"`/`"baseline-row-missing"`/`"scan-row-missing"`, DB row states, response codes/status bodies, dispatcher stats computed over real DB state, pending-action rows, muted_until/acknowledged_at). Mutation spot-proofs on two representatives: killing `services.rebaseline_site`'s enqueue failed `test_agent.py::test_confirm_executes_frozen_args` exactly at its "executor should have enqueued" assert; disabling `_capture_baseline`'s already-ready guard failed `test_scan_tasks.py::test_capture_already_done_is_idempotent` at its return-value assert (`'ready' != 'baseline-already-ready'`) — i.e., the paired anchors bite even where a negative guard also exists. All mutated files restored byte-identical (`git checkout --`; post-sweep `git diff --stat` shows only the long-dispositioned `.gitignore`/`scripts/lib.ps1` items).
  - *The Phase-12 incident shape recurs nowhere*: structurally, every negative guard has a positive twin in its module pinning the same production chain end-to-end (e.g., `test_due_site_gets_scan_enqueued` vs `test_not_due_site_untouched`; `test_confirm_executes_frozen_args` vs `test_cancel_does_not_execute`; the flagged-verdict e2e suite vs the benign-noise test). The Phase-12-era escalation tests were unique in lacking a positive twin — closed by Phase 12 itself.
  - *Class (c) — ONE offender found and rewritten*: `test_encode_budget_is_bounded_on_huge_pages` asserted only `counting.calls <= 2 * _MAX_CHUNKS_PER_SIDE`; a chunker/embedder that died entirely (0 encodes) satisfies an upper bound vacuously. Rewritten to also require `counting.calls >= 2` (positive evidence the sampled windows actually embedded). Falsifiability proof performed formally: with the counter's increment removed (dead-embedder simulation), the OLD form PASSES while the NEW form FAILS at the floor assert; restored, the module passes 29/29.
- **Edge cases considered (Step 3, condensed)**: N/A categories — test-only change, no production surface moved; concurrency/retry/RBAC/unicode/boundary all N/A. The sweep deliberately did NOT add suite-wide machinery (e.g., an autouse dead-stub detector): one rewritten instance does not yet justify it, matching the DNS-guard precedent recorded by the companion Phase 30 entry.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) rewrite the single offender with a floor bound — chosen (minimal, preserves the test's cost-ceiling intent). (B) Rewrite corroborating negative guards into fail-if-called wrappers — rejected: their paired positive anchors were mutation-proven sufficient; wrapping would duplicate coverage already carried by the positive twins and churn nine healthy modules for zero falsifiability gain.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: one test strengthened (the rewrite IS the deliverable); three temporary mutations of production code used purely as verification instruments, all reverted byte-identical before commit (verified via git diff).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **907 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~816 s; warning = the pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning recorded since Phase 0). Frontend: `pnpm test` → **12 files / 82 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → exit 0; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= recorded baseline exactly). Backend `uv run --frozen ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 146 files formatted. No dependency changes → supply-chain gates stand green from Phase 9.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: none mechanical — one test-file edit; the mutated-and-restored production files are bit-identical to the state their own suites pin.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) The dual-bound counting technique (assert both floor and ceiling on a call counter) is now applied twice in the suite (the DNS zero-query rationale + this floor); a third instance would justify shared machinery per the companion entry's recorded threshold. (2) New leads observed: none.
- **Commit**: fix(phase30b)

### [FIXED] Two safety-critical code paths have zero test coverage: the remediation webhook firing path (`worker/remediation_tasks.py`) and the agent engine turn loop (`app/agent/engine.py` dispatch/refusal/iteration logic)

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 31 — Test-Quality New Coverage (webhook + agent loop)
- **Files changed**: `backend/tests/test_phase31_critical_paths.py` (new — 16 tests; no production code changed)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: the finding's headline is PARTIALLY STALE and the staleness is mapped honestly. Since it was filed, later phases added real coverage near both paths: Phases 4+5 (atomic `_fire` claim: concurrent single-post, redelivery skip, fresh-claim-blocks-retry, undecryptable URL, non-2xx mapping), Phase 11 (containment tests drive the REAL `run_turn` loop with scripted models), Phase 25 (guard claims; above-role refusal through the real turn loop), Phase 26 (SSRF refusal at fire time, delivery-crash terminal guarantee, resweep eligibility bounds). Exhaustive greps at phase time confirmed which of the audit's named surfaces REMAINED uncovered: `MAX_ITERATIONS`, `execution-missing`, `prereqs-missing`, `fire_remediation(` wrapper, `302`/ReadTimeout in the remediation context, empty-message / unconfigured-model / non-tool-model / LLM-unavailable engine arms, unknown-tool refusal arm, malformed tool args, executor-crash containment — every grep returned zero test references (recorded as the coverage-absence proof). Not stale for these; the module below closes exactly them.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: as filed — the destructive-action executor and the model-dispatch loop had grown with zero task-body-level tests; subsequent fix phases added targeted suites for their own findings, but these remaining branches stayed unpinned, so regressions in any of them would ship silently.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)** (each → a test):
  - *Non-UUID id to the Celery entry point* → `"bad-id"` without touching asyncio (wrapper contract).
  - *Internal error inside `_fire`* → wrapper returns `"error"`, never propagates into Celery retry machinery; execution row untouched (still queued).
  - *Execution id not found* → `"execution-missing"`.
  - *Dismissed row re-delivered* → `not-queued-dismissed`, zero POSTs (complements the existing succeeded-variant guard).
  - *Prerequisites deleted between queueing and firing* → see Step 6 iteration record: proven UNREACHABLE under enforced FKs; replaced by the cascade-semantics pin.
  - *Receiver answers 302* → redirect NOT followed: one request, failed row, detail `webhook returned HTTP 302`.
  - *Receiver stalls past the deadline* → production timeout fires against a genuinely slow receiver (constant patched to 0.2 s as a config knob): failed row, detail `webhook unreachable: ReadTimeout`.
  - *Empty/whitespace user message* → single error event, model never consulted, nothing persisted.
  - *No model assigned* → done event with the exact guidance copy, assistant message persisted, model never consulted.
  - *Model without tool-calling support* → done event before any model call (defence-in-depth arm).
  - *LLMUnavailable mid-turn* → error event, assistant error message persisted as transcript tail.
  - *Unknown tool NAME (the `tool is None` refusal arm)* → refusal result fed back verbatim, no execution, no pending rows, turn completes normally.
  - *Malformed JSON arguments* → parsed to `{}`, tier-0 executor runs fine, result fed back, turn completes.
  - *Executor raises unexpectedly* → generic `"That action failed unexpectedly."` fed back; internals (`hunter2` canary) absent from ALL model-bound messages AND persisted transcript rows; ok=False surfaced on the event.
  - *ToolError (user-safe failure)* → message fed back verbatim, ok=False, turn continues to completion.
  - *Always-complying model* → loop terminates at exactly MAX_ITERATIONS model calls with the canned wrap-up text, no pending rows.
  - Concurrency/retry/RBAC/backward-compat/performance: N/A or covered — races are owned by the Phases 4+5/18/19/25 suites (all green inside regression); RBAC filtering is exercised implicitly (analyst declarations) and pinned by Phase 25; test-only addition so nothing existing can regress; module adds ~14 s.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) dedicated new module with its own receiver fixture and scripted-model harness — **chosen** (matches the per-phase-module precedent; keeps the focused charters of the race/containment files intact). (B) extend `test_remediation_claim_race.py`/`test_agent_prompt_injection.py` in place — rejected: mixes breadth coverage into race/containment charters. Mocking discipline: ONLY the LLM boundary is scripted (the audit's own directed technique); executors, guard, persistence, schema and the HTTP transport are real; the webhook receiver is a raw asyncio TCP server like the claim-race canary, extended with Location/delay controls.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: new `tests/test_phase31_critical_paths.py`: seven firing-path tests (bad-id via `asyncio.to_thread`; wrapper swallow with an injected failing session asserting the row stays queued; missing row; dismissed never fires; cascade pin incl. receiver-zero-requests; redirect-not-followed; ReadTimeout mapping) and nine engine-loop tests (empty message; unconfigured model; non-tool model; LLMUnavailable; unknown-tool refusal with fed-back payload assertion; malformed args; executor-crash containment with leak sweep across model context AND persisted messages; ToolError surfacing; MAX_ITERATIONS exhaustion with exact call count + wrap-up copy).
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: these ARE the deliverable (16 new). Two defects in my own constructions were caught and fixed during iteration (Rule 8):
  1. *`prereqs-missing` is structurally unreachable*: all three `remediation_executions` FKs (hook/site/scan) are `ondelete="CASCADE"` (models.py:636-641), so a persisted execution whose prerequisites are gone cannot exist under enforced FKs — live-probed (deleting the hook cascaded the execution away; `_fire` reported `execution-missing`). This mirrors Phase 1's enforced-FK lesson for baselines. The planned branch test was REPLACED by `test_hook_deletion_cascades_queued_execution_so_nothing_fires`, pinning the safety property from the firing side (nothing can ever fire from an orphaned prerequisite state) with the unreachability documented in its docstring; the production arm remains as defensive depth, now honestly annotated.
  2. *The wrapper test initially passed for the wrong reason*: under pytest-asyncio a running event loop makes `asyncio.run` raise "cannot be called from a running event loop" BEFORE awaiting `_fire`, so the catch-all was exercised by that error rather than my injected failure, leaving an un-awaited coroutine (RuntimeWarning). Root-caused and fixed by running the sync wrapper via `asyncio.to_thread` — the Celery worker-thread reality with no ambient loop — after which the injected session failure is what reaches the catch-all; `-W error::RuntimeWarning` escalation over the whole module proves the warning class is gone.
  - Failing-before proof: infrastructure-deliverable precedent (Phases 1/8) — the file cannot exist pre-phase, and the Step-1 empty-grep set stands as the formal record that none of these branches was referenced by any prior test. The suite-wide regression additionally confirms all 16 pass only alongside the current production behavior they pin.
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: Backend `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --tb=short` → **923 passed, 0 failed, 1 warning** (~818 s; warning = the pre-existing apprise/imghdr DeprecationWarning recorded since Phase 0; 907 prior + 16 new). Frontend: `pnpm test` → **12 files / 82 passed**; `pnpm type-check` (tsc -b --noEmit) → exit 0; `pnpm exec oxlint src` → 0 errors / 12 warnings (= recorded baseline exactly). Backend `uv run --frozen ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `ruff format --check .` → 147 files formatted. No dependency changes → supply-chain gates stand green from Phase 9.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: the module REUSES the seams earlier phases built (Phase 4+5 claim semantics asserted via row states; Phase 26's opt-in/pinning transport honored by the opted-in loopback receiver; Phase 11's scripted-model technique; Phase 25's guard claims untouched by tier-0-only scripts) and modifies none; all their suites re-ran green inside the 923.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) Telegram bot handlers remain covered only by two DB helpers (audit note; outside this finding's two-path scope — candidate if a bot-surface finding is ever filed or at Phase 42's re-audit). (2) The `prereqs-missing` arm is dead-under-schema defensive depth — removal would be a production-code change outside a test phase; documented instead. (3) New leads observed: none.
- **Commit**: fix(phase31)

### [FIXED] README claims remediation executions run "in a separate Celery queue" isolated from scans — everything shares one default queue and one worker pool

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 33 — Docs Sweep A (ops/remediation)
- **Files changed**: `README.md:157` (isolation claim replaced with the verified shared-pool reality)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: re-derived the routing facts fresh rather than citing the audit: README.md:157 still contained "execution tasks are isolated in a separate Celery queue so that slow/broken endpoints never block the scan engine" verbatim; code facts all confirmed on the current tree — the API-side producer enqueues through ONE chokepoint (`app/tasks.py:45 send_task(name, args=args)`, no queue option; Phase 3's broker-only client), worker-side sends (`worker/scan_tasks.py:350/:365`) likewise pass no queue, and repo-wide grep finds zero `task_routes`, zero `task_default_queue` override, zero `-Q`/`--queues` flags; `Dockerfile.worker:36` CMD is plain `celery … worker`; docker-compose.yml's worker service has no command override. Every task class shares Celery's default queue and one prefork pool. Claim false exactly as filed.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: the README documented an isolation property that was never built (no routing config exists anywhere). Operators sizing deployments would believe webhook storms cannot degrade scanning; in reality a slow receiver occupies a shared worker slot for up to WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT_S=20 s and several concurrent firings consume scan capacity directly.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *What IS true and kept*: firing IS a separate Celery TASK, never the scan body (remediation-hooks.mdx:34's accurate phrasing retained); the 20-second timeout is enforced; a failed/broken hook cannot fail or abort the scan that triggered it.
  - *What must be stated honestly*: firings share the single default queue and worker pool with scans/baselines/alerts, so several slow endpoints can temporarily tie up worker capacity.
  - *Cross-file consistency*: remediation-hooks.mdx's "(never the scan body)" claim stays accurate under the new README wording; no other file makes the queue-isolation claim (grep).
  - *Adjacent stale passage deliberately untouched*: README.md:156 still prints the audit-era material-change constant (`fused_risk >= 0.15`) that Phase 10 re-derived to 0.35 — a DETECTION claim, reserved to Phase 34 (Docs Sweep B) per the §4.3 split; disposition recorded here explicitly so it has an owner. Same bullet list, different finding — Rule 1 discipline held.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phases 4+5/26 removed the worst sharing pathologies (double-fire, stuck-queued wedge), which bounds how bad slot-sharing gets; the honest wording describes the residual truth, not the pre-fix exaggeration in either direction.
  - *Auth/RBAC/backward compat/performance*: N/A — prose only.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: worst case imprecise prose; mitigated by asserting each clause against code facts enumerated above.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) correct the claim to describe the shared pool — **chosen** (the audit's alternative direction verbatim). (B) Implement the documented design instead (route `fire_remediation` to a named queue + second small worker consuming only it) — rejected for THIS phase: deployment-topology change (new compose service / resource footprint) inside a docs sweep, a reliability enhancement rather than a correctness fix, with the safety-relevant sharing harms already closed by Phases 26/4+5; recorded as residual so the re-audit can weigh true isolation as its own deliberate change.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: the Guarded Remediation Hooks bullet now says each firing runs as its own Celery task (never inside the scan body) AND explicitly notes firings share the same single queue and worker pool as scans and captures, so several slow endpoints can temporarily tie up worker capacity.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: `test_readme_does_not_claim_a_separate_celery_queue_for_firings` — asserts both defective phrases absent and the truthful "same single queue and worker pool" anchor present. **FAILED pre-fix** formally (stash run below).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: joint — see the Telegram entry above (927 backend / 82 frontend / all gates green).
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: none mechanical — no earlier phase touched README.md's hooks bullet (Phase 14 deliberately left :157 reserved for this phase; its hand-off honored). remediation-hooks.mdx's separate-task wording consumed read-only.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) True queue isolation remains unimplemented by design decision recorded under Step 4 — if operators report webhook storms starving scans, the durable answer is the audit's own compose-level design (named route + second worker), taken as a deliberate infrastructure phase. (2) README:156's stale constant → Phase 34 (explicitly recorded). (3) New leads observed: none beyond that already-tracked item.
- **Commit**: fix(phase33)

### [FIXED] Uninstall documentation promises a backup-then-delete safety contract and restorable backup that the script does not guarantee

- **Original severity**: Medium
- **Phase**: 33 — Docs Sweep A (ops/remediation)
- **Files changed**: `README.md:270-282` (uninstall contract paragraph rewritten to the enforced behavior incl. exit codes), `README.md` flag bullets (+`-AllowIncompleteBackup`), `README.md` uninstall CAUTION (restore-steps host-piping warning), `docs/installation.mdx:130-140` (same paragraph sync), `docs/installation.mdx` ParamField list (+`-AllowIncompleteBackup`), `docs/installation.mdx` Caution (same restore warning)
- **Re-verification (Step 1)**: both filed locations reproduced verbatim pre-edit (README "backs up everything recoverable first … and only then removes", unconditional restore-via-RESTORE.txt caution; installation.mdx mirror passages). Then re-verified the CURRENT script contract directly in the tree per Rule 11 (not from memory of prior entries): `scripts/uninstall.ps1` carries `-AllowIncompleteBackup` (:34), `$missing` completeness tracking (:117), the pre-teardown gate failing with exit 1 naming what's missing (:341-346), exit 2 after explicit override (:449), byte-exact dump produced INSIDE the container via `sh -c pg_dump` + `docker compose cp` + ASCII-header sanity check (:179-231), and RESTORE.txt generated from what the run actually captured with cp→`psql -f` steps and a never-pipe-through-a-host-shell instruction (:284-328). Conclusion: the scripts WERE fixed (Phases 13/29), so the audit's conditional ("until then the docs must state…") resolves in the strong direction — the docs must now describe the ENFORCED contract precisely, including the gate, the override, and the changed restore procedure.
- **Root cause (Step 2)**: the docs were written once and never tracked the script's evolving guarantees: at audit time they promised a contract the script broke silently; post-Phases 13/29 they undersold a stronger-than-documented reality while still omitting the operator-visible mechanics (incompleteness gate, override flag, exit codes 0/1/2, container-side dump/copy restore steps) that make the promise trustworthy and scriptable.
- **Edge cases enumerated (Step 3)**:
  - *Failure-direction honesty*: the old text had no failure story at all; the new text leads with what happens when a backup part fails (stop before deleting, exit 1, data intact) because that is the contract's entire point.
  - *Override visibility*: `-AllowIncompleteBackup` added to both files' flag lists with its data-loss meaning — an undocumented safety valve is indistinguishable from a trap.
  - *Exit-code consumers*: 0 (full success or deliberate -SkipBackup), 1 (blocked, nothing deleted), 2 (override accepted, incomplete removal ran) now stated in the contract paragraph — wrapper scripting depends on these.
  - *Restore-leg accuracy*: RESTORE.txt's steps changed in Phase 29 (docker compose cp + in-container psql -f); both Caution blocks now carry the never-pipe-through-a-host-shell warning so operators following README-level instructions don't reintroduce the PS 5.1 corruption the script eliminated.
  - *"Everything recoverable" phrasing retired*: replaced with the concrete component list + gate description; gaps-not-failures semantics (absent .env / absent artifacts volume don't block) are the script's documented internal contract, not restated in marketing copy.
  - *Cross-file consistency*: README and installation.mdx paragraphs are sentence-for-sentence equivalents (they mirrored each other pre-fix); flag lists updated in both; Caution blocks identical.
  - *Scope discipline*: the script itself untouched (Phases 13/29 complete; nothing stale found in it); adjacent uninstall content (progress counters — Phase 39's Finding 9.5) untouched.
  - *Interaction with prior fixes (Rule 11)*: Phase 13 (gate/exit codes/manifest) and Phase 29 (byte-safe dump + cp-based restore recipe) are the behaviors this copy documents; both re-verified live-in-tree above rather than cited from log entries.
  - *Unicode/backward compat/performance/auth*: N/A — prose only.
  - *Fix-failure mode*: worst case imprecise prose; bounded by deriving every clause from the freshly-read script lines cited above.
- **Fix design considered (Step 4)**: (A) Sync both docs to the enforced contract (gate + override + exit codes + byte-exact mechanism + copy-based restore warning), keeping the two files mirrored — **chosen**; this is the finding's endgame state once its precondition ("fix the script per Findings 9.1/9.2") is satisfied, which Phases 13/29 did. (B) Keep the old unconditional promise and add only a caveat — rejected: understates the now-real guarantee and leaves operators scripting against unknown exit codes. (C) Rewrite the script further — rejected: no residual defect found in it this session; scope is docs.
- **Fix applied (Step 5)**: as listed under Files changed; the contract paragraph now walks backup → failure-stop (exit 1) → override (exit 2) → teardown ordering explicitly, names the byte-exact capture mechanism, and both restore cautions warn against host-shell piping per RESTORE.txt's actual steps.
- **Tests added/modified (Step 6)**: `test_uninstall_readme_documents_the_enforced_backup_contract` + `test_uninstall_installation_docs_document_the_enforced_backup_contract` — each asserts `-AllowIncompleteBackup` documented, the stop-before-deleting language present, and RESTORE.txt/compose-cp anchors retained (installation variant additionally pins the `docker compose cp` restore mechanism). Both **FAILED pre-fix** formally (stash run below).
- **Full regression result (Step 7)**: joint — see the Telegram entry above (927 backend / 82 frontend / tsc clean / oxlint 0e12w = baseline / ruff check+format clean). No dependency changes → supply-chain gates stand green from Phase 9.
- **Interactions with prior fixes**: Phase 13 — its gate/exit-code/manifest machinery is what the new copy promises; its sandbox suite re-ran green inside the 927 (script untouched this phase, so its tests needed no adaptation). Phase 29 — same for the byte-exact dump and cp-based restore recipe. Phase 14 — its Step-3 reservation of the uninstall passages ("wording sync belongs to Phase 33") fulfilled here. No earlier phase edited README's/installation.mdx's uninstall sections.
- **Residual risk / follow-ups**: (1) The docs describe exit codes as stable interface — future script changes must treat them as such (the Phase 13 tests already pin them behaviorally).
